# Queue & History

*What the machine-local queue is and what the line in it is; how a job's identity, folder and label are composed and shown; how held jobs sit outside the line and what the entry says about them; how every act on the line — an insertion, a group moved as one block, a cancel that leaves nothing, a clear-then-stop whose order is the promise — is made safe against a machine consuming the line with nobody connected; how a running render is read in both of its stream shapes and what the two bars may claim; how an ending is decided, what the machine keeps against a job, and how one job holds two records on two lifetimes; what survives the machine going down; adoption, catch-up, idle timing, deletion and the previews.*

*2026-10-27 — Removed the withdrawn paid-lock reference and replaced its deleted-plan link with the decisions record. No auditor has seen this removal.*

Rule 1's plain-English rule is suspended here, per the Architecture stage's exception.

**Scope, from [The shape](The%20shape.md#16-queue--history):** composing machine-local queues into one list, held jobs and groups, ordering, live readings, machine and local history, adoption, catch-up, idle timing, previews, deletion, and reconciliation. This document takes jobs and render events from `Rendering`, state from `State, identity and recovery`, server readings from `Rented-server runtime` through `Servers`, and output readings from `Job files and outputs`; it supplies live-job use to `File Browser` and `Server Setup`, history setup to `Rendering`, transfer triggers to `Download`, and reporting state to `Telegram`. **`Rendering` and `Servers` are written after this document; [Section 17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) states what each takes from here so that their half is a citation rather than a second decision.**

**Sixty permanent coverage rows are assigned here — Rows 47 to 87, Row 302, Rows 319 and 320, Rows 377 to 382, Row 424, Rows 432 to 434, Row 442, Rows 447 to 450, Rows 494 and 497 and Row 516, less Rows 63 and 86, which are struck by the both-ways check and get no answer — with Row 496's entry half answered here beside them, its machine half `Job files and outputs`'.** *(Rows 319, 320 and 377 to 382 were added on 2026-08-21, out of the stage-close sweep of the Design documents; N12 to N16, B3a–B3b and C2a are written for them, and no other clause moved. ## What binds this before anything is decided

**[State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md) §1.1 and §1.2 are the store inventories and this document opens no store outside them.** On the machine, three of §1.2's stores are this document's by that table's own owner column — **the machine-local queue**, **the per-job note**, and **the machine's own history copy** — and their contents are decided here. On the user's side, the local record is the only durable store that holds state, and *"A builder that needs a second state store has found a fault in this section rather than a case it does not cover."* Two record kinds this document puts in the local record — the catch-up notice record and the idle-spoken mark — are not named in §1.1's inventory; that is the same store-inventory seam five documents have now found, recorded at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 13 and in the report to the runner, never repaired here.

**§3 D4 already rules Row 78, and there is no fourth exception.** The desktop keeps no durable copy of an observed machine state the machine still holds, with exactly three exceptions named there: the last-known queue reading, **period-held**; the history record, Smoozy's own note and not a copy; the remembered server state, **subject-held**. Everything in [Section 11](#11-one-job-two-records-two-lifetimes) is that rule applied.

**§3 D1 and D2 govern every act on the line.** *A job's place in a machine's line* is in D1's own table: one durable desired state, one writer — the supervisor — separately observed readings, never one field, and no desired state is ever inferred from an observed reading. Every act in Rows 51, 56, 57, 58, 59 and 302 is a write to that desired state that the machine reconciles, and D2 is what stops the observed Batch A failure — a stop honoured and reversed two seconds later.

**§3 D5 governs every time this document shows or counts.** Every observed reading carries the moment it was taken and the clock it was taken on, and readings taken on different clocks are never subtracted from one another. A duration about work on a machine is computed from that machine's own readings; the desktop's clock is presentation and nothing a promise rests on. Row 55's half hour and Row 76's times are both inside that rule.

**§7's six ordered steps are the desktop's reconciliation and four of them are this document's rows.** Step 2 resolves provisional job records and already states what Row 56 requires of a job that never rendered; step 3's read-forward from the event cursor is what Row 77's notice is composed from; step 4's fill-in is what a history tombstone refuses; step 6 is adoption, one offer per machine, the identity kept rather than reissued.

**§10.1 is Row 82's tombstone, already built** — keyed by the job identity alone, holding the deletion moment and whether the machine-side copy has gone, refusing step 4 unconditionally, carrying the owed erase as a recorded debt, kept for the life of the local record. §10.3 rules that a cancelled job gets no tombstone and that a later connection finding one offers adoption instead. No second mechanism is invented below.

**§11.1's five classes are the local lifetimes, there is no size-based class, and ~~there is no age-based class~~ — CORRECTED 2026-08-22 by the Creator's ruling: a job's fetched derivatives, and nothing else in that table, now carry an age ending as well.** The fetched derivative is **subject-held on its job record and is not throwaway on the user's computer** (§11.3), cleared when the record is deleted **or when it has been held longer than thirty days, whichever comes first** — this clause used to read *cleared when the record is deleted and at no other moment*. **The period is thirty days, from the moment the copy was published on the user's own computer, on that computer's clock — ruled 2026-09-02 under the Creator's standing delegation of that date and written at [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#111-five-classes-and-the-reason-there-is-no-sixth) §11.1 R16, with the sweep's comparison at §11.2 R19–R21 and the class at §11.3 R23; that document's §13 item 12 is answered and is no longer a park. Corrected here 2026-09-02, this sentence having said the period is not set.** The machine-side derivative may be cleared only once the fetched copy exists or the record is gone; a downloaded output is in no class at all. §11.4: **nothing on a machine is cleared on the strength of a free-space reading alone.**

**§1.3 rule 5 binds every section below: nothing reads a file's presence, its name or its size as state.** The single carve-out is `Job files and outputs`' already-there test, a rule about what to render and never a reading of state. Every queue state, every ending and every figure below comes from a record Smoozy or the supervisor wrote.

**[Rented-server runtime](Rented-server%20runtime.md) §8 is the one hold mechanism and no second waiting mechanism exists.** An unreleased hold removes its subject from the admission point entirely (U5, A10); a hold survives every restart (U24); both answer routes reach one supervisor command path (U10); the first answer recorded wins and the second acts on nothing (U13); the loser is told which answer was applied and when (U14); a *no* after a *yes* is not converted into a stop (U15); nothing expires a hold (U22, U23). Its §11 is the captured streams, the exit and the closed set of four collection outcomes; its §7 the common arbitration mechanism, under which A9 rules that **what waits behind an exclusive render is ordered by this document**; its §12 the health readings Row 66's figures travel through; its §16 item 12 and §17's tenth check are Fact 1's disk-full negative already ruled against all three escalations, reaching none — **it is not re-escalated below and nothing below claims more than it allows.**

**[Job files and outputs](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md) is the heaviest input.** Its §13 hands this document its output readings by name: N3–N4's folder naming, the recorded reading's fields, the walk's outcomes and *show outputs*' folder, the preview's candidate list and rungs, the two picks made once and never recomputed, C14's job that owes nothing, and C27–C28's completeness statement naming the absent and empty paths. Its §10 is completeness entire — the reckoning on the machine, the zero-byte rule and C15's limit, the last frame asked for — and nothing below re-derives a clause of it. Its 1.2 names the machine-local queue as living in the package area; its H10: *"The queue entry is `Queue & History`'s and what it says here is this document's."*

**[Desktop app and delivery](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md) rules the now-versus-catching-up boundary and the group speaking once.** T18: everything applied at §7 step 3 is the catch-up notice's and posts no notification; events after that step are *now*. T11 and T27: a batch of scene-jobs speaks once, composed from the group identity on its own job records, needing no new store. T9 and T14: the idle kind is this document's reading and its half hour. T22: a notification advances, clears and records nothing, and the idle reading and the catch-up notice are durable in-app records. None of that is re-decided below.

**[Dependency Upload & Remap](Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md) §11 hands over, as citation:** the resolved set fixed at submission (K9, V5), naming the job's remap material beside its mirrors and fixed copies; the disturbance marks Smoozy's own acts leave (V6, V13), what an entry says about them being this document's and Design's; and the cleanup act — deleting a job's machine-side record removes its remap material (U2).

**[Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md) §7 is the transfer contract in both directions**, with the per-server admission point and the desktop-side shared budgets; T8–T10 are the priority mechanism, the class declared by the arranging module and the order among the standing classes, declared at `File Browser` [6.2 O4–O5](File%20Browser.md#62-the-order-and-the-shares-row-189) as Rows 189's and 207's. Row 84's preview fetch declares its class against that mechanism ([15](#15-the-previews) P6).

**No secret and no licence record is touched anywhere below.** [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md) and `Audits/Architecture/Licence service/Decisions record.md` are consumed, not extended.

**The frozen evidence is read as *this is what that box did on that day*, never as a general statement.** Fact 1 at the promise level, Fact 40 in full, Fact 41 in part and Fact 2 in full are [The last two boxes](The%20last%20two%20boxes%20—%20the%20hook%20that%20cannot%20be%20read%20back,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20the%20unit%20that%20kills%20a%20render.md) §1, §15.1, §15.2 and §3; Facts 28, 29, 43, 45 and 46 are [Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md) *(Fact 45 added to this enumeration 2026-08-29: L21, L22 and N19 rest on it and it was named nowhere here or in the sources file)*; Fact 6's choice half is [Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md); Fact 29's name limits and the receiving-end loss are [One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7. **Every Blender constraint below is taken from the joined answers `Preparation/Blender Research/Across topics/While a render runs.md` and `When a render ends.md` and the comparison questions they name, or from the row file's exact quotations of them, with two exceptions, each named in place — B6's write rule, which is the Glossary's **Output** entry, **Creator-stated (2026-07-24)**; and **E13a's colour-substitution lines, added 2026-09-02, which are the joined answer `What has to travel with a file.md` and the comparison question it names, *Dependencies 15*** — a third joined answer, read because the spec's ruling of 2026-09-02 puts the telling on this document's entry and neither of the two above covers it. Nothing is assembled from topics here and nothing comes from memory.**

---

## 1. The machine-local queue, and the line

**Constraint:** Row 47's two quotes — the queue lives on the machines themselves, and what Smoozy puts there has to keep going with nobody connected, start again by itself after a reboot or a crash, and read back the line it was holding. Fact 1 is answered at the promise level on both machine kinds, with the disk-full clause no ([Facts](Facts.md), Fact 1).

**Q1. The machine-local queue is one durable store in the package area, holding one job record per job, written only by the supervisor.** That is [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#12-on-each-rented-server) §1.2's own inventory row and `Job files and outputs` 1.2 L6's placement — outside every job folder, so nothing the user browses, uploads, downloads or renders reaches it and no job cleanup removes it. The supervisor is the only writer (S1), which is what makes every rule below a rule rather than a hope.

**Q2. A job record holds, and holds nothing that is an observed reading:**

- the **job identity**, and the **group identity** where the job came from an all-scenes submission — the same pair the manifest carries (`Job files and outputs` 3.1 M1), so a group is readable off the queue with no group store anywhere ([4](#4-acting-on-the-line) L18);
- the **sequence key**: the job's desired place in its machine's line, which is D1's durable desired state for *a job's place in a machine's line*, written by the supervisor and by nothing else;
- the **queue state**, from a closed set: *waiting*, *held* (with the hold identity, `Rented-server runtime` U2), *rendering* (with the render operation's identity), *ended* (with the recorded outcome's identity) — each written at the event that makes it true and journalled;
- the **reference to the job record directory** `Job files and outputs` owns, where the manifest, the recorded reading, the attempt records and the derivatives live. Nothing of theirs is copied into the queue record;
- the **removed-build mark**, present only where a Blender removal covered this job's recorded build path — [4](#4-acting-on-the-line) L21's field, written at that removal, read at the admission point alone, and not a queue state. *Added 2026-08-23 with L21, per Row 447.*

**Q3. A job is in the line exactly when its job record exists with state *waiting* or *rendering*, and the record is written only after the job's material is published.** The submission's atomicity — a job reaches the machine whole or does not exist — is `Rendering`'s Row 2 and `Job files and outputs` M5's single-rename publication, cited and owed; what this document fixes is the order: material published first, then the job record and the per-job note ([10](#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in) N1) written by the supervisor in one act, then the event journalled. A published material set with no job record is never a job; its removal is `Rendering` S4b's recorded cleanup debt — `Job files and outputs` M7's sweep, which this sentence used to cite, reaches only an output folder whose record was never published and never carried this case *(corrected 2026-08-22, the scenario replays' routed clauses item 13)*.

**Q4. The line is the job records with state *waiting*, in ascending sequence-key order, and the machine consumes it at the admission point.** When no render operation is running — the render kind holds the cards **exclusively** (`Rented-server runtime` A2, this document's allotment promise) — the supervisor takes the lowest-keyed *waiting* record, writes the render operation record (W3), and starts attempt 1. That is Row 51's *the machine starts the next job in its line straight away*: the trigger is the previous operation's recorded outcome being written, entirely supervisor-local, with no connection and no Smoozy in it. **A9's question — what waits behind the exclusive render is ordered by this document — is answered: the order is the sequence-key order and nothing else.** **Where the record it takes carries L21's removed-build mark it is dropped rather than started and the admission point takes the next in the same act ([4](#4-acting-on-the-line) L22) — added 2026-08-23, per Row 447.**

**Q5. A held job's record is not in the line.** Its state is *held* and the admission point never considers it (U5, A10); it holds no sequence key while held, which is what *takes no place there and is not reordered* means concretely. [Section 3](#3-held-jobs) H3 is where it gets one.

**Q6. Untracked work is in the same store and the same line.** A job record whose identity the desktop's local record does not name is still a job record; the machine's own order decides what runs next, tracked or not, which is the spec's own sentence. What the desktop shows about it is [Section 13](#13-reading-a-machine-and-adoption)'s.

**Q7. Sequence keys are per machine, opaque outside the supervisor, and unique among the records live in a machine's queue at any one moment.** The rule binds the live line and not the machine's whole history, so no durable high-water mark is kept and a value may recur after the record holding it is gone; H3 assigns on the same terms and the two say one thing. A new job takes a key greater than every key then in the line — joining the end. Nothing parses a key, nothing infers submission order from one, and the desktop never composes one: it names jobs and positions ([4](#4-acting-on-the-line) L6) and the supervisor writes the keys.

**Q8. What a rented machine permits is answered, and the one negative is closed here rather than worked around.** The reboot and crash halves are yes on both machine kinds: a real kernel panic left the supervisor returning with a fresh run identifier, the held job still held with its hold byte-identical and unreleased, the running job at attempt 2 with `re-run once` journalled, and the line read back over the connection with every job's state, admission point, operation and unreleased hold ([The last two boxes](The%20last%20two%20boxes%20—%20the%20hook%20that%20cannot%20be%20read%20back,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20the%20unit%20that%20kills%20a%20render.md) §1, §15.1). **The disk-full form is no**: every write is refused loudly with errno 28 and nothing moves behind it, every read answers truthfully with free space `0` — but the supervisor cannot start on a full state mount; after space is freed, the container heals by itself while the systemd unit needs a plain start rather than recovering merely because room returned; and the arrangement's own death is recorded nowhere Smoozy writes. `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 12 carries it and §17's tenth check records it checked against all three escalations, reaching none; **the repair is `Server Setup`'s and `Desktop app and delivery`'s automatic supervisor-unavailable trigger, and nothing in this document is that trigger.** While the arrangement is down, no queue act, no note write and no journal write happens, and from the desktop the machine is the supervisor-unavailable condition of [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#83-the-condition-identity-and-who-supplies-each-observed-value) §8.3 — Smoozy says it could not check, never a guessed line. **A supervisor already running when the mount fills is the other case, and it is no longer unwritten — added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R10**: it goes on answering, every durable write refuses loudly, and what it could not write about an ending is held pending and re-attempted until it lands, under `Rented-server runtime` [3.3](Rented-server%20runtime.md#33-reconciliation-is-the-supervisors-and-it-runs-at-more-moments-than-a-start) S14 — so the queue reading moves an ended job off *rendering* from the supervisor's own answer, and the journal, the note and the previews catch up at the landing (N5, P1b). [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 1 names what breaks.

---

## 2. Identity, folder, and label

**Constraint:** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#21-the-rules-that-bind-every-identity) §2.1's seven rules and §2.2's register row — the job identity is issued by the desktop application at submission, before the submission is sent, unique across every job Smoozy has ever made, held in the local job record and the machine's durable job record. Fact 29's byte limits and the receiving-end loss ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md); [One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7).

**Y1. A job identity is 128 bits drawn from the operating system's cryptographic random source at the moment of issue, and nothing else.** **That every supported system publishes such a source, and fills a caller-sized buffer from it, is documented on all three: Linux's `getrandom(2)`, Apple's `SecRandomCopyBytes(_:_:_:)`, Microsoft's `BCryptGenRandom` — sixteen bytes is inside every one of them.** Label: **documented**, all three read 2026-08-23, quotations and addresses in the sources file. **Labelled here for the first time on 2026-08-23; the claim stood unlabelled in this document and unsourced anywhere in `Preparation/` until then, recorded as an assumption with no source found.** No clock — the user's computer's clock is undefended (Fact 13, `Security and licence` §12.3). No counter — a counter is state a reinstalled application loses, and §2.1 rule 1 forbids reuse for ever. No machine reading, no user input, no path (§2.1 rules 3, 4, 6, 7). Uniqueness rests on the width of the draw alone, which is what makes two jobs unable to collide *however quickly one follows another* and across every machine and every rental, per §2.2's scope note.

**Y2. Its textual form is fixed-length lowercase letters and digits, nothing else.** No capitals, no spaces, no accents, no punctuation. Each exclusion is an observed hazard: names differing only in capitals, and two spellings of one accented name, collapsed to a single file on arrival at the Mac with every fetch reporting success ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7.1); one space in a path broke three tools three different ways ([The rented machine](The%20rented%20machine.md)). The form is short enough that N7's byte budget keeps a bounded label rendering beside it, and it is opaque — nothing parses it, sorts by it, or reads meaning from adjacency (§2.1 rule 2).

**Y3. The identity is issued before the submission is sent, and that is what makes a dropped submission answerable.** The provisional local job record is written first (§7 step 2); a lost acknowledgement is resolved by asking the machine for the identity, never by re-issuing the work (§12 rule 4). `Rendering`'s matching after a dropped submission reads this and is owed there.

**Y4. An adopted job keeps the identity the machine's record carries.** §7 step 6 already rules it, and §2.2 establishes there is no adoptable work without such an identity — every route to untracked work leaves work this desktop submitted. One job never has two identities.

**Y5. A re-run is the same job with the same identity and writes where its first attempt did; a fresh attempt the user asks for is a new job with a new identity and a new folder** (§5.4). The constraints Row 48 lists — the folder named for the identity, no job ever writing over another, a finished folder still saying which job made it — are kept by `Job files and outputs` N3, N9 and N10 over this identity, cited and not re-derived.

**Y6. Where the job's folder sits is inherited, not chosen here.** `Job files and outputs` 1.1 chooses one root on one mount, once per installation, by a per-path available-space reading; the job output folder is under the job area of its 1.2; the hand-over of a root-owned second filesystem is `Server Setup`'s step under its 1.3. A rental's choice of disk is a quota rather than a second device, and nothing here assumes otherwise. **Corrected 2026-10-04, on the Creator's ruling of that date: the job output folder sits where the file's own outputs point** (`Job files and outputs` [6.6](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P18–P22), **and under the job area of its 1.2 only where P19's fallback is taken.** Where it sits is still inherited and still not chosen here. *Corrected later on 2026-10-04: the fallback takes the server's chosen fallback folder where the user set one and it can be used, and the job area otherwise (`Job files and outputs` P23). Y8's telling of P19's case is unchanged: it stays true in either place, and the path shows which.* *(And where a chosen folder was passed over, Y8 as widened later that day says so as well.)*

**Y7. Fact 6's survival half is owed and this document does not paper over it.** Both disks survived a stop and a start; survival across the machine's own unattended housekeeping over days is unobserved. **A machine with more than one physical disk is no longer unobserved — corrected 2026-08-22**: one seller ships two real block devices as standard, 97 GB and 787 GB, which `Job files and outputs` L4 carries. **What is unobserved there is whether the larger mount survives a stop, a start or a restart**, which is a narrower question than the one this clause used to name. What rests on it — the job folder, the record directory, the last-frame figure's ground — is carried at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 6. Nothing below reads a folder's survival as assured, and a folder found gone reads as the completeness statement's absent paths, never as a silent success.

**Y8. The entry always shows the full label, from the record, and the folder name shown is the manifest's recorded path verbatim.** The naming half is settled — `Job files and outputs` N3–N4: the output folder's name is the identity's textual form plus a bounded rendering of the label, substitution rather than refusal, the full label authoritative in the record — and is not re-decided. What was left to this document is what the user sees where a label was substituted: **the label they typed, everywhere a label is shown; the folder's real name, everywhere a path is shown** — *show outputs*, the file browser, the entry's folder fields all read the manifest's recorded path and never a re-rendering of the label. No warning and no marker is raised about the difference; presentation of the two side by side is Design's. **Widened 2026-10-04 with `Job files and outputs` [6.6](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits).** Where the render worker chose the folder at render start (P21), the path shown is the one the job record carries, reached by N9 and held on the local record; until it arrives the job has no folder and *show outputs* is withheld, as N17 withholds any reach-back that cannot work. **Where P19's fallback was taken, the entry says which of its two cases it was** beside the path — wording Design's. *Widened later on 2026-10-04, on the build runner's ruling under Rule 8: where the job records that the server's chosen fallback folder was passed over (`Job files and outputs` P23), the entry says that too, in one more sentence beside the case — wording Design's. It rides M1 or N9 with the case.* Checked against the third escalation and declined: the substitution is the spec's own ruling of 2026-08-18, and nothing new is put in front of the user ([Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator)).

**Y9. Nothing anywhere parses a folder name back into an identity or a label.** The identity travels in records; the label travels in the record and the note (Fact 40's note answered the label); the folder name is written once from them and read never (§2.1 rule 7, §1.3 rule 5).

---

## 3. Held jobs

**Constraint:** Rows 52 and 53's mechanism is `Rented-server runtime` §8's and is not this document's — one hold record, two origins, one release path, no second waiting mechanism. What is this document's is the ordering the released subject enters, the release predicate of Row 54, and what the queue and the entry say about all of it.

**H1. A held job's entry says it is waiting on an answer, and offers both answers, and that is the whole of what a waiting entry adds.** The entry's state derives from the hold record — read on a connection with the line ([13](#13-reading-a-machine-and-adoption) A1) and kept current by journal events (U26) — never from a second store. Both kinds of hold — the first-frame check's second job, held from submission, and the job held at the start of its render — read identically on the entry apart from the stated reason, which the hold record carries and this document shows without interpreting (U2). *A third reason, added 2026-10-11 on the Creator's ruling of that date: a job held for the driver (`Rendering` S18a) offers no answers, because nothing waits on a person, and says what it waits for instead — [H12](#3-held-jobs). The reason decides which the entry draws; nothing else is read for it.*

**H2. The desk's answer is an ordinary command with its own operation identity, naming the hold identity and carrying the answer, submitted over the connection** (U9). The phone's route is `Telegram`'s tap arriving at the same supervisor command path (U10). The two differ in how they reach the machine and in nothing after that.

**H3. The released subject enters the line behind whatever is waiting at the moment the release outcome is written, and the supervisor writes that placement in the same act as the release.** U8 fixes the moment; this document fixes the ordering: the released job record takes state *waiting* and a sequence key greater than every key then in that machine's line. Not the front, not its old place — the spec's rule is *behind whatever is waiting on that machine at the moment the answer comes*, and the moment is the release outcome's write, on the machine, so it is right whether the answer came from the desk, from a phone, or hours after Smoozy closed.

**H4. Two answers to one hold are settled by the machine's serialization and the entry tells the truth about it.** The first answer recorded wins and the second acts on nothing (U13); the loser is told which answer was applied and when, as a recorded outcome rather than a failure (U14); a *no* arriving after a *yes* is not converted into a stop (U15), and the ordinary stop — `Rendering`'s — remains offered on the now-running entry. What this document adds is only presentation ground: the entry shows the recorded release outcome from the moment it is learned, so a queue that answered second shows the job running (or gone), never a stale *waiting*.

**H5. The entry's two answers are offered while, and only while, the hold's release outcome is unwritten as far as the desktop knows.** A tap racing that knowledge is safe by H4; nothing here withdraws the offer on a guess.

**H6. A held job with a dependency shows what it waits on.** The hold record names the dependency (U2); the entry states it — the first-frame job it waits behind — so a machine standing still on account of a held job has a stated cause on the entry as well as in the idle message ([12](#12-the-idle-half-hour) W2). Where the dependency has ended and H8 left the hold standing, the stated cause is that ending, and both answers stay offered (H5).

**H7. Row 54's predicate, declared in full: the waiting job is dropped when its dependency's ending carries a completeness reading in which no frame counts as already there.** The reading is `Job files and outputs`' completeness statement — C27's four sets, produced from the expected-output inventory and one walk of the job output folder (C7) — and the requirement on it, stated in full because it is wider than the path U30 covers: **every recorded outcome that ends a job carries a completeness reading, taken as a step of that ending, whoever writes the outcome.** Where a worker writes it, the reading is that worker's own ending step carried with the outcome it reports, which is U30's requirement kept from this side. **Where the outcome is written by reconciliation with no worker alive** — *given up* from §6's table ([9](#9-the-machine-going-down-and-coming-back) D12), and *left stopped* written from a durable *should be stopped* — the same requirement stands: the reckoning of `Job files and outputs` C1 and C7 is run on the machine **before the outcome is written** and its statement is carried with the outcome exactly as on the worker path. C1's reckoning runs on the machine with no connection and no render in flight, so the reading is available at that moment. **`Rented-server runtime` [3.3](Rented-server%20runtime.md#33-reconciliation-is-the-supervisors-and-it-runs-at-more-moments-than-a-start) S11 to S13 own that step, added 2026-08-21**, and [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 17 records the close. *Already there* is C12 and C13: every file the frame owes present and none of them empty; a file of zero bytes never counts, wherever it sits, per the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-12 owned by [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md). The exit status decides nothing here — **twenty separate endings report success, six of them ending at 0 with an empty output folder** (corrected 2026-08-31 out of the repaired Blender research; this read *twelve*, and the sharper half for a completeness reading is the six rather than the count) — and the frame in flight is never counted.

**H8. The predicate is evaluated at U19's one moment — when the dependency's recorded outcome is written — against the reading carried with that outcome, and at no other moment and against no other reading.** A dependency whose ending is *finished with its frame already there* releases nothing and drops nothing: the hold's own release path (the user's answer) is what acts on it. A dependency that ended any other way — failed, left stopped, given up — with no frame in its carried reading drops the hold: the release outcome *dropped* is written with the dependency's ending as the reason and journalled (U20). **Where the recorded outcome carries no completeness reading and the dependency did run, the predicate is not evaluated**: no drop is written, and the hold stands with its own release path — the user's answer — still the route, H5's offer unchanged and H6's stated cause naming the dependency's ending. H9's *no reading is no frame* does not extend to this case and may not: H9's dependency never ran, where no-frame is a fact, while a dependency that ran and was given up may have written its first frame, and dropping the waiter against absent evidence is irreversible under U2's write-once rule. What that costs where an outcome carries no statement is `Rented-server runtime` S13's case, recorded at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 17.

**H9. A dependency that never ran is a dependency that produced no frame, and the drop fires at the moment its job record is removed.** A first-frame job cancelled while waiting has no operation record and no worker ending, so U19's moment — a recorded outcome on the dependency's operation — never arrives for it. This document supplies the missing moment: **the supervisor, in the same act that removes a job record whose identity a hold record names as its dependency, evaluates the predicate with no carried reading, and no reading is no frame.** The hold is dropped with *the dependency was cancelled* as the reason. Whether `Rented-server runtime`'s U19 should name this moment beside its own — and U21's flat *a dependency whose ending is never recorded never drops the hold*, which the spec's own *cancelled before it ever runs* clause overrides — is a seam recorded at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 12 and in the report — this document's half is complete either way.

**H10. For a video-shaped dependency the predicate reads the same statement at its one limit.** The inventory's video entry is one file; it counts only where present and not empty, and C15's limit — a file short but not empty still counts — is carried, named, and not repaired. A first-frame check is one frame, so the case is narrow; where it bites, the waiting job runs against a first frame the user must judge, which is the check's own design.

**H11. The dropped waiter leaves nothing anywhere, and the user is told why, once.** The drop event is journalled (U20); applying it removes the machine-side job record and its material by the same enumeration as a cancel ([4](#4-acting-on-the-line) L3), and on the desktop the provisional record is removed when the event is applied (§7 steps 2 and 3) — a job that never rendered leaves no trace. The telling is the catch-up notice or a *now* notification per T18's boundary, wording Design's; after it, nothing anywhere shows the job was submitted, which is the same shape as saying no.

**H12. A job held for the driver says what it waits for, offers no answers, and is released by the desktop rather than by a person. Added 2026-10-11, the Creator's ruling (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's rulings on OptiX and the driver, 2026-10-11.md`, its last section): *"the user has to be notified about it somehow, probably the job in the queue should get a warning of some kind or something."* Label: Creator-stated. No auditor has seen this text.** Which jobs are held, and when they are released, are `Server Setup` R1c's; the hold is placed at submission by `Rendering` S18a, reason *the driver*, no dependency. What is this document's is the entry and the line.

- **The entry.** Its state derives from the hold record, as H1 has it, so it reads the same after Smoozy is reopened. It says the job is waiting for the driver update, names the job's Blender (the version of N19's pair), says Smoozy updates the driver and restarts the server once the other work there is done and then starts the job, and says this happens only while Smoozy is connected. **It offers no answers**: H5's offer is for a hold awaiting a person, and nothing here does. Wording Design's, following what the job's row stops (`Rendering` L23a, added 2026-11-02).
- **The line.** The job takes no place in its machine's line while held (Q5, U5). On release it joins behind whatever is waiting at that moment (H3); R1c sends the releases one at a time in submission order, so several such jobs keep their order among themselves.
- **What the user can do to it** is what they can do to any held job, and nothing is added. **Cancel** is L5's and leaves no trace; the entry offers it where *Stop* sits, as on any job that has not started, since nothing is rendering to stop. **Reorder** is not offered (L9). A **group** move gathers only waiting members, so a held member stays out of the block (L7); a group cancel, and a stop-and-clear, take it with the rest (L14, L19). A cancel racing the desktop's release is settled by the single writer like any other pair (L1, U13).
- **Idle.** A machine whose only work is such a job has nothing that can start (W1).
- **A phone is sent nothing about it** (`Telegram` §17 A3): no waiting message, no buttons and no message for the release; where the status message names the job, it reads as this entry does. The job's ending is told like any other's.
- **The release is the go-ahead**, sent by the desktop as U9's command.
- **It is live work** for every reader of [Section 17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)'s live set **except `Server Setup` R1b's swap**, which leaves it out.
- **Where the swap missed the bar, the release still comes** (R1c), and N23's mark is on the entry before the job runs.

---

## 4. Acting on the line

**Constraint:** Rows 56, 57, 58, 59 and 302. Every act below is a write to D1's desired state, serialized by the single writer, applied by the machine with or without anybody connected. D2 — no desired state inferred from an observed reading — and §9.3 — an ordered set is one unit — are the tools throughout.

**L1. A cancel of a waiting job is a command with its own operation identity; the supervisor removes the job record from the line and journals the removal, and the material cleanup follows.** The command is recorded durably before acting (C4), so a lost acknowledgement is answered from the record and never re-acted. The deciding write is the job record's removal — one durable write, after which the job cannot start (the admission point no longer sees it). **A named job that is no longer waiting — the admission point took it, or it ended, while the command travelled — is not cancelled, the outcome says so and names why, and the material cleanup is not sequenced**; L4 is the desktop's routing rule taken on a period-held reading and the supervisor's refusal is the authority, because no desired state is inferred from an observed reading (D2). The material — the job record directory with its manifest, reading, attempt records and derivatives (`Job files and outputs` §11), the remap material (`Dependency Upload & Remap` U2), the per-job note, and the empty job output folder — is removed after it, partial across directories and atomic within each, per that document's own declaration; a unit that fails is retried by the ordinary owed-work route, and none of it can resurrect the job, because the record is already gone.

**L1a. Two controls sit on the job's own entry and belong to `Download` rather than to this document. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Download](Download.md#15-unresolved) §15 item 19.** The entry carries **an auto-download switch** — off and on, at any time while the job runs, not only at submission — and **a narrowing control** saying which of the job's outputs the arrangement takes. **Both are placed on the entry rather than anywhere else**, which `Preparation/Design/Transfers.md` already draws, and **what they do is `Download`'s** — R1's arrangement created afterwards from the entry, N3's narrowing, R11's off-and-on, and J6's mid-render switch-on all rest on them. **What this document owns is only that the entry is where they sit**, and that neither is a desired state of the job: switching auto-download on writes nothing to the machine, changes no job record on the machine, and touches nothing in Section 4's line. `Rendering`'s *auto-download is switched on at the moment of submitting* stays true as the submission-time route and is no longer the only one.

**L2. The local half of a cancel is §7 step 2's, and the two copies disappear in a fixed order: machine first, then the desktop.** The cancel needs a connection — the spec grants no offline cancel — and the provisional or confirmed local record is removed only when the cancel's recorded outcome (*removed*) is learned: on the acknowledgement, or on a later connection asking the command's identity, or on the journal event. Until then the entry may show the job cancelling; presentation is Design's. After it there is no entry, no hidden row and no tombstone — §10.3 rules a cancelled job gets none.

**L3. The reconciliation of *leaves nothing anywhere* with *the record is written at submission* is the order above, and the adoption rule covers the one gap honestly.** The record written at submission outlives the machine precisely because it is removed only on a confirmed unwriting. A job whose cancel never reached the machine is not a cancelled job — it is still submitted work; if its server row was removed meanwhile, the local record went subject-held with the row, and a later connection finds the work untracked and offers adoption (§10.3's own ruling). That is a correct outcome, not a restoration: nothing the user was told was deleted comes back, because a cancel is only ever reported done when the machine's copy is gone.

**L4. A cancelled job that was rendering is not a cancel but a stop**, and takes L10's path; the spec's cancel covers waiting jobs alone.

**L5. A held job is cancellable on the same terms**: the hold record's subject is removed, the hold ends with its subject (U22's third ending), and where another hold names the removed job as dependency, H9 fires.

**L6. A reorder names jobs and positions, never indexes, and the supervisor applies it as one durable write.** The command carries the job identity to move and the position, expressed as *after job X* or *at the front of the waiting line*. The supervisor rewrites sequence keys in one write; the single writer serializes it against the admission point, so the top of the line cannot be consumed *during* the write. A named job that is no longer waiting — it started rendering or ended while the command travelled — is not moved, and the outcome says so; a named predecessor no longer waiting resolves to the front of the waiting line, which is where its position went. Nothing is inferred from what the desktop last saw: the command states desire, the machine applies it to what is true (D2).

**L7. A group block move is a gather-and-insert, one command, one write.** The command names the group identity and the position; the supervisor gathers every *waiting* member — read from the group identity on the job records, L18 — keeps their current relative order among themselves, and writes them as one contiguous block at the named position, in one durable write. A member already rendering stays where it is and is named in the outcome as not moved because it is rendering; a member that ended is simply absent. §9.3 makes the block one partition unit: it lands whole or the write fails whole, and a half-moved group cannot exist.

**L8. The race Row 57 names is closed by serialization, not by care.** The machine may start the next job at any instant with nobody connected — but starting is the supervisor's own act at the admission point, and the reorder is the supervisor's own write, and one writer does one thing at a time (S1). A job taken by the admission point before the reorder's write is a job the reorder reports as not moved; a job still waiting when the write lands is moved. There is no interleaving in which a moved job renders in the old order.

**L9. Reordering never touches a rendering job and never touches a held one.** The first is the spec's rule; the second is U5's — a held job has no place in the line to move.

**L10. A stop is `Rendering`'s to send and this document's to record and follow.** The stop sequence is `State, identity and recovery` §5.3 and `Rented-server runtime` X8 — desired state *should be stopped* written durably before the signal, the interrupt to the recorded render process and never a shell, completion never inferred from delivery. What the queue does: on the recorded outcome **left stopped**, the entry moves to history marked *stopped*; the admission point starts the next job on the outcome's write (Q4).

**L11. A stop Smoozy asked for is told from an ending it was not by the recorded outcome alone.** *Left stopped* is written only by the stop path; a job that ended on its own **before the stop's termination was issued** records its own ending (D3) and the entry shows that ending, not *stopped*; an ending past the issuance is *left stopped*. **The boundary is `State, identity and recovery` §5.3 step 7's one boundary — the issuance, the only one the supervisor holds a record of its own about — cited rather than re-decided** *(fixed 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R6: this said "between the request and the delivery", one of three different windows three documents gave for one boundary)*. No stream, no exit number and no timing is read to decide it (`Rented-server runtime` X13, M20).

**L12. A stop is not instant and nothing here waits on it without a deadline of its own.** Observed 1–3 seconds on the virtual machine and 1.9 to 14 on the container (Fact 2). Every waiter uses its own per-waiter deadline (`Server protocol and transfers` C16–C17); expiry is reported as the waiter giving up, never as the stop having failed or succeeded.

**L12a. Between the stop's submission and its outcome, the entry says the stop is happening. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R26.** From the moment the stop command's record is written — durably, before anything acts, per the same rule L1 and L13 already cite — until its recorded outcome is learned or its own waiter expires (L12), the entry reads *stopping*, composed from the desktop's knowledge of its own outstanding command: the same shape as L2's *cancelling*. It is Smoozy's statement about Smoozy's own act and never a sixth queue state — Q2's closed set does not move, the machine's job record still reads *rendering* until the outcome is written, and no reading is reinterpreted. When the outcome is learned the entry shows what it says: *left stopped*, or the job's own ending where it ended on its own meanwhile (L11). Where the waiter expired, the entry returns to what the records say, the expiry reported as the waiter giving up per L12. Wording and presentation are Design's. The paired stop inside stop-and-clear takes no rule from this — its presentation is `Servers`' Row 42 (L17). The third-escalation check is at [Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator).

**L13. Stop-and-clear is one request, two ordered halves, applied on the machine, and the order is held by the supervisor rather than watched by the desktop.** The desktop submits the clear and the stop as two commands with their own operation identities, marked as one ordered pair; both are recorded durably before anything acts (C4), so a connection dropping mid-way changes nothing — the machine completes the sequence with nobody connected and the desktop learns both outcomes by asking the identities.

**The pair names no render operation, and the stop's subject is resolved on the machine.** The desktop cannot name a target that is still true when the clear lands: the only identity it holds is the operation its **period-held** queue reading last showed rendering ([13](#13-reading-a-machine-and-adoption) A1, [11](#11-one-job-two-records-two-lifetimes) C7), and D2 forbids a desired state inferred from an observed reading. So the stop carries the clear's operation identity as its pair and no target, and its subject is **whichever operation holds the exclusive render at the moment the clear's write lands**, resolved by the supervisor inside the same serialized sequence as that write. The resolved identity is written into the stop's command record at resolution, so a re-sent command carrying the same operation identity is answered from the record by identity and never acted a second time (C4, §5.4). Where the resolved operation is not the one the desktop's reading last showed, the outcome names both. From the resolution onward `State, identity and recovery` §5.3 steps 2 to 7 apply to the resolved target unchanged — desired state *should be stopped* written durably first, the recorded execution group validated, the kind's own termination, nothing inferred from delivery, the recorded outcome, and step 7 where the worker ended on its own. **That §5.3 step 1 and §5.1 as they stand do not admit a subject identity written at resolution is the seam at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 16**, and that document is not edited here.

**L14. The clear comes first and is one durable write removing every job record it covers.** It covers every job record on that machine that is not the running one — waiting in the line and held outside it alike, because the promise is an emptied machine and a held job left behind would start nothing but would sit unanswerable on a machine the user asked to empty. ***The running one*** **has one reading and only one: the operation holding the exclusive render at the moment of this write, which is the same operation L13 resolves as the stop's subject — never the operation the desktop's reading last showed.** So a job the admission point took after the desktop composed the pair is the one the clear exempts and the one the stop then takes, and no record the clear removes has a worker running. The removal of all of them is a single write by the single writer; the material cleanup follows per L1, per job, partial across.

**L15. There are two windows, not one, and both are closed — the first by the single writer, the second by the stop having no target until the clear's write.** The window Row 59 names: the running job ends on its own **between** the clear and the stop. The clear's one write empties the line entire, so after it there is nothing for the admission point to take, and the two acts are acts of one supervisor that cannot interleave (S1). The window Row 59 does not name: the running job ends **before** the clear's write lands, while the pair is in flight from the desktop, and the admission point starts the next job — entirely supervisor-local, needing no connection (Q4). A stop naming the operation the desktop's reading last showed would be applied to an operation whose outcome already exists, recording that job's own ending and not *left stopped* (L11), while the machine went on rendering the job it just started. L13 removes that: the stop has no subject until the clear's write, and at that write the operation the admission point started is *the running one* — exempted by L14, resolved as the stop's subject, and stopped. **Neither window rests on what the desktop saw** (D2). The ordering the spec forbids reversing is kept, and the outcome it exists to prevent — the user asks for an emptied machine and gets a fresh render instead — has no path left in either.

**L16. Where the clear's write is refused, the stop is not attempted, and says so.** §9.3: the pair is one unit because the second is unsafe without the first; the stop's recorded state is **not attempted**, naming the clear as what stopped it. The observed refusal is the disk-full one — errno 28, loud, nothing moved behind it (Fact 1) — and a machine in that state cannot render the next job either, but the promise is kept by the ordering, not by that accident.

**L17. The two halves keep separate outcomes, and three results are distinguishable at the desktop:** could not begin at all; cleared and stopped; cleared and the stop failed or expired its waiter. The outcome record of §9.4 on the desktop side holds the enumeration durably before the first unit is attempted, so an interruption loses the saying and never the fact. Presentation is `Servers`' Row 42, owed there and cited here.

**L18. A group's membership is held in exactly two places, both per-job, and there is no group store.** On the machine: the group identity on each member's job record (Q2) and in each manifest (M1). On the desktop: the group identity on each member's local job record, from which T11 composes the one finishing notification with no count kept beside them. Membership is data on the members, so it survives scattering (order is not membership), a machine-side reorder, the cancel or stop of any member, Smoozy being closed, the machine going down (the records are durable), and the later single notification (T11, T27 — composed from local records, needing no machine).

**L19. Group acts are member acts under one command, in the stop-and-clear order.** Cancel-group clears the group's waiting and held members first, then stops its rendering member, by L13's, L14's, L16's and L17's machinery scoped to the group; reorder-group is L7. **L15 does not scope and is not imported**: its proof is that the clear empties the line entire, and a group clear leaves non-members the admission point can and should take — which is correct, because the user asked for the group and not the machine. What the group case rests on instead is L13's resolved subject read against the group: the stop's subject is the operation holding the exclusive render at the moment the group clear's write lands **and whose job record carries this group identity**, and where the operation running at that moment is not a member, no stop is attempted and the outcome says so. L13's pairing and command records, L14's exemption, L16's not-attempted rule and L17's three results scope unchanged, reading *the line* as the group's members. A scene-job remains an ordinary job throughout — any single-member act is the ordinary act, and a member moved alone leaves its group's membership untouched, which is what lets the gather find it again.

**L20. A group is drawn as a group from the group identity on the members' own local records, and A1's queue reading carries no group field. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 434.** L18 fixes where membership is held; what no clause said is which of the two copies the desktop's grouping reads, and the answer decides whether a heading survives a machine that cannot be read.

- **The identity is Smoozy's own and exists before any of it reaches a machine.** `State, identity and recovery` §2.2 issues the group identity when an all-scenes act is composed (`Rendering` S2), and it is written on every member's local job record, on every member's machine-side job record (Q2) and in every manifest (M1).
- **The desktop groups its rows on the local record's copy, joined to the period-held reading by job identity, and composes the grouping at the draw.** So the heading needs no field of A1's reading, nothing durable copies a machine state (§3 D4, C6), and **a group whose machine cannot be read is still drawn as a group** — with its rows carrying C7's stamp and not-updating statement, or A3's could-not-check condition, exactly as an ungrouped row does. **An adopted member is grouped on the identity step 4's fill-in carries from its manifest** (A7), which is the one route by which a member's group identity is not already on the desktop.
- **The act across the group is L19's and each row's own act is unchanged.** Cancel-group and reorder-group are member acts under one command, scoped by the group identity read off the job records on the machine (L7, L19, L14's exemption and L16's not-attempted rule scoping with them); a single-member act is the ordinary act. The plan totals stay per member (B1) and the one finishing notification is `Desktop app and delivery` T11's, composed from the same identity. **Which acts a group offers beside the single-job ones, and how the heading is drawn, are Design's.**

**L21. The one ground a job's chosen Blender may be read as gone is a removal Smoozy performed and recorded, and the mark it leaves is a field of the machine's job record. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 447.** **Constraint:** Fact 45 — a plain unpacked folder is invisible to every route but a whole-disk file search and a renamed binary defeats that search too — so **the search did not find it** and **it is not there** are two readings, **and a drop taken on the first destroys a job whose build stands.** **No clause of this answer or of L22 reads the machine for a build:** `Server Setup` [B10–B12](Server%20Setup.md#54-the-search)'s search is never consulted, no path is stat'd, run or asked its version, and §1.3 rule 5 stands untouched. The recorded pair N19 keeps is not re-derived and not changed by any of this.

- **What is read instead is `Server Setup` B14's own predicate, at the removal.** [B15](Server%20Setup.md#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s removal — the folder deletion, or the machine's own software manager — is an operation of that document's setup kind with a recorded outcome, and [B14](Server%20Setup.md#55-taking-a-blender-off) composes, before anything is removed, the set of live jobs whose recorded build path **is the path of the build being removed, or sits under it where the removal is a folder deletion**. That predicate is cited and not re-decided here, and **the set it marks and the set the warning named the user are one set, composed once.**
- **The supervisor writes the mark, in the same act in which the removal's recorded outcome is written**, on every job record the predicate covers — the live set being the job records with state *waiting*, *held* or *rendering* (Q2, [17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)). `Server Setup` writes nothing: its §12 states outright that it writes nothing of its own into any record this document defines, and this is this document's own store under its single writer (Q1). It is one durable act riding a durable act, never a poll — `Rented-server runtime` U19a's shape.
- **What the mark holds is Q2's fifth field: the removal operation's identity, and the answered version of the build this job was submitted with**, copied from that job's manifest (N19) while the manifest stands. It is **not a queue state** — Q2's closed set does not move — and **not a desired state** (D1, D2): nothing desires the drop and no observed reading produced the mark. It is written at the event that makes it true and journalled, as a queue state is (N2). It is not in A1's reading, reaches no entry and is drawn nowhere.
- **Nothing clears it and nothing re-checks it.** A build standing again at that path is a build no record of Smoozy's names: `Server Setup` B8 never publishes over an existing folder and names every install's folder with an identity of its own issue, so a Smoozy install never re-occupies a removed path, and a build restored by an act outside Smoozy is reachable only through the search this clause refuses. [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 23 names what that costs.
- **A mark whose write is refused is the disk-full condition and nothing else** ([Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 1): the write refuses loudly, no mark stands, and that job takes L22's last path.

**L22. The drop fires at the admission point, leaves no history entry and no tombstone, and the reason is journalled rather than kept on the job. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 447.**

- **The moment is the job's turn.** At Q4's admission point, where the lowest-keyed *waiting* record carries L21's mark, the supervisor does not start it: it removes the job record, journals the drop, and takes the next record in the same serialized act, dropping each marked record it reaches until it reaches a startable one or the line is empty. The material cleanup follows per L1's enumeration. The whole act is supervisor-local, with no connection and no Smoozy in it (Q4, S1) — which is why what the user is told is read off the machine afterwards (Fact 40) rather than said at the moment.
- **The mark fires on a *waiting* record and on no other, which is what keeps the spec's three sentences apart.** A record marked while *rendering* never returns to *waiting* — an interruption leaves it *rendering* and §6's disposition re-runs it (D4) — so the admission point never reaches it, and *a job already rendering is unaffected and finishes* is `Server Setup` B14's, carried there with Fact 110 and with its stated limit: the folder-deletion route observed, the software-manager route **not established**. This document adds nothing to it — a render that ends anyway ends from its recorded outcome by E1 and E2, like any other. A **held** record carries the mark unfired; released, it takes its place in the line (H3) and is dropped when its turn comes.
- **Nothing is written to history and no tombstone is written.** The job never started rendering, so it never entered history (C9) and there is nothing to keep; the machine-side record goes in the drop's own act, so no erase is owed, A5's offer can never reach the identity, and §10.3's no-tombstone rule holds for the same reason it holds for a cancel. On the desktop, applying the drop event removes the local job record, provisional or confirmed alike (§7 steps 2 and 3) — H11's own sentence, a job that never rendered leaves no trace, and after it nothing anywhere shows the job was submitted.
- **The telling comes off the journal event, because every record of that job's is removed in the same act.** The event carries the job identity, a bounded rendering of the label (`Job files and outputs` N3) and the reason — the Blender the job was submitted with was removed from the machine, named by the answered version L21's mark holds. **It names no record and carries no path**: the note it would have pointed at is being removed, which is why the label is copied rather than referenced, and no clause here requires the path on a screen (N19). All of it sits inside E6a's per-entry budget. The desktop reads it forward at §7 step 3 and the telling is the catch-up notice or a *now* notification per T18's boundary, wording Design's (C1); the phone reads the same event ([17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)). A job the local record does not name is dropped the same way and told to nobody, the desktop never having known of it (A2, A4). Where the journal's cap has passed the cursor, the account says so rather than smoothing it (C4, E5).
- **A build that went away by an act outside Smoozy is not this path.** Nothing marked the job, so it starts, and `Rendering` [L14](Rendering.md#15-the-version-confirmation)'s confirmation — the binary asked its own version before the line is composed — fails the attempt before the render instruction. That is a job that started, with an entry saying so, which is loud rather than silent and is a different act from the one the spec's paragraph is about. [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 23.

**L23. The clear carries a durable record of its own act on the machine, written before the first removal, and the record is what makes the clear whole. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it.** The clear covers several job records and this store is one file per record (Q1, Q3, Q6), so the one write [Section 16](#16-declarations--atomic-or-partial-arbitration-and-the-one-termination-contract)'s table used to declare cannot exist — [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#9-the-atomic-or-partial-boundary) §9.6 P8 settles that no single filesystem operation updates two independent files atomically, and calls it a settled constraint rather than an open part.

- **Before the first removal the supervisor writes a durable record naming every identity the clear covers** — the set L14 composes at the moment of the clear, exempting the running one. It is that document's §9.4 outcome record, on the machine side, where the clear actually happens.
- **Each identity's outcome is written as it becomes true** — done, failed, or not attempted naming what stopped it — and an identity already absent is *done*, the same rule X3's units already keep.
- **The record is cleared only when the last covered record is gone**, so a record still standing is an outstanding clear and nothing else.
- **What the clause buys, and it is the whole point of it: L15's proof that Row 59's window is shut stops resting on one write and rests on this record instead.** A supervisor killed between two removals comes back, finds the record, finishes the clear from it, and only then may the admission point start anything (D14). Without the record it would come back to a half-cleared line and start a job the user asked to have cleared.
- **The desktop's §9.4 record under L17 does not stand in for this and never could**: it holds the pair's two halves — the clear and the stop — and names none of the covered identities, so nothing on the desktop can finish a clear the machine began.
- **The clear's removals are journalled one by one, and the desktop removes what they name. Added 2026-09-29, out of the reading of the removal's stop and clear.** Each covered record's removal, as its outcome is written *done*, is journalled as a drop is (L22): the job identity, and nothing that names a path. The desktop, applying the event at `State, identity and recovery` §7 step 3, removes that job's local record, provisional or confirmed alike, as L2 does for a cancel — which is that document's H8, first bullet. An identity *failed* or *not attempted* is not journalled and its local record stays; the job is what the machine still says it is. Nothing is inferred from a job's absence from a reading (`State, identity and recovery` D2). ~~A removal needs none of this, since `Servers` W25's local forget takes the server row and everything held under it; it is the kept server — `Desktop app and delivery` V15 and L19's group form — that needs it.~~ **Corrected 2026-09-29, out of step 10c's S2, on the build runner's ruling: a removal needs it too.** `Servers` W25's local forget takes the server row but keeps its job records, marking any whose start was never learned *start never heard* (C9, Creator-stated 2026-09-18). So where the pair came to *cleared and stopped*, the removal first applies what the machine journalled, over the same connection, before the forget — each cleared job's local record goes, and the stopped render's ending reaches its entry. Without it, every waiting job the clear removed would stay in history as a job whose start was never heard. The kept server — `Desktop app and delivery` V15 and L19's group form — needs it for the same reason.

**L24. A reorder and a group block move carry the same record, and it holds the whole intended key assignment. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it.** The record is written before the first key moves, names every waiting record's key as the rewrite intends it, and is cleared only when the last moved key is written; each record's own rewrite is one durable write, and an outstanding record is finished at [Section 9](#9-the-machine-going-down-and-coming-back)'s reconciliation before the admission point may start anything (D14). **It is needed because the rewrite writes back only the records whose key actually moved** — a sound build decision, recorded in this module's decisions record, and one that leaves a refusal part-way with an order that is neither the one the line had nor the one the user asked for, with nothing left to recompute it from. L7's outcome, its named-as-not-moved members and L8's serialization are unchanged; what the record adds is that the intended order survives the refusal.

---

## 5. The reader of a running render

**Constraint:** Row 60 — the stream has two shapes, parting at one boundary, the whole stream moving together across it, no single kind of line exempt; the error stream is not reliably empty on success; on Linux the shell can add lines of its own; and whether there is anything to read at all is decided by how the render was started, which is `Rendering`'s Row 11 — *"Where nothing is printed, nothing above can be read."* All from `Preparation/Blender Research/Across topics/While a render runs.md` and the comparison questions the row file quotes.

**R1. There is one reader, it rides the render worker's own copy loop, and it produces readings, never state.** The worker already holds both of the render's streams and copies them to the sinks itself (`Rented-server runtime` M3); the reader is a tee on that loop, observing bytes as they pass — the same discipline as M28's marker observations and M4's byte count. Nothing opens the sink to read progress, nothing polls the folder, and nothing the reader produces is ever a job state: the ending comes from the operation record, the group's validation and the reaped exit (M20), and a stream that has stopped moving is not an ending.

**R2. The reader is composed per version, selected by the manifest's Blender version, in two grammars.** The 4.5 grammar: plain lines, no frame announced before it is rendered, the progress line naming frame and view layer. The 5.0-and-later grammar: timestamped, sorted lines, every frame announced, the frame range announced once at the start. The boundary is one and the whole stream moved together across it — seven kinds of line, recorded separately — so no line is parsed by a rule shared across the boundary. One thing parts again one version later and it is the only one: the statistics a render can be asked for, whose units and headings change at 5.2; nothing here reads those statistics, so the second boundary costs nothing.

**R3. The reader consumes the ordinary stream only, and a line that matches no grammar yields nothing and is kept anyway.** The complaints stream is captured, retained and shown (Section 8) and is not parsed for progress; it is not reliably empty on a run that succeeds, and on Linux the shell can add lines of its own — both of which the reader survives by construction, because an unmatched line is not an error, it is a line with no reading in it. The sink keeps every byte either way (M8).

**R4. Whether anything prints at all is `Rendering`'s and this reader states the dependence rather than repairing it.** Started by calling the render operator from a script, the three newer versions print nothing until the frame is saved; started from the command line with no window, every version prints in full. The route is `Rendering`'s Row 11, owed there; where nothing is printed, every reading below is absent and the entry reads without them — absence of readings is never an ending (E1).

**A second way to read nothing is named here for the first time, 2026-08-21, and it is narrowed on 2026-09-02 to a Python script's own printed lines: the reader's own pipe may be holding those bytes, and it does not hold a render's own.** *Reports 14*, named by `While a render runs` and settled by tests in all four versions on both machines, answers the half this clause had left open in both directions. **A render's own printed lines reach a pipe exactly as they are produced** — dozens of separate arrivals spread across a run, in bursts at each frame boundary, and a plain file grows in steps whose steps are the frames — **so a pipe is no different from a terminal for them**, and every reading R6 lists is a reading of those lines. Label: **observed in a test**. **A Python script's own printed lines reach neither a pipe nor a file until the run ends**: a script printing one line a second for fifteen seconds delivered nothing until the run finished and then all fifteen at once, and to a file stayed at nought bytes and reached full size in one step at the end; both behaviours were then shown inside one run, the script's line waiting while the render's own lines came through. Label: **observed in a test**. **What decides the timing is which part of Blender printed the line, not whether anybody is watching**, and the two switches that change it are `--python-use-system-env` and `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`, which change nothing on their own and make the lines prompt when used together, with `print(…, flush=True)` doing the same from the script's own side and the `stdbuf` wrapper doing nothing for a script's lines in any version — **tried in 4.5 and 5.2 only and on the Creator's Mac alone**, so the switch half is thinner than the split it modifies. **What this costs the reader: nothing.** `Rendering`'s Row 11 starts renders from the command line with no window, so no line this reader consumes is a script's own printed line, and the buffered-silence case does not reach a reading of this document's; where a route of `Rendering`'s prints from a script, its lines arrive only at the run's end unless one of those switches is in force, which is that document's to state and is not repaired here. **The standard's own sentence is kept because it is the mechanism behind the script half**, and nothing below moved when the split was learned. The worker copies both streams from pipes (`Rented-server runtime` M3) and this reader tees that loop, and a stream on a pipe is buffered by the standard rather than by anybody's choice. The Open Group Base Specifications Issue 8, IEEE Std 1003.1-2024, `stderr, stdin, stdout — standard I/O streams`, read **2026-08-23** (address in the sources file). Label: **documented**. *"When opened, *stderr* shall not be fully buffered; *stdin* and *stdout* **shall be fully buffered if and only if the file descriptor associated with the stream is determined not to be associated with an interactive device**."* A pipe is not an interactive device. **That sentence is the whole ground of this clause. The supporting run is a demonstration and is labelled as one — corrected 2026-08-23**: a two-line pipe test posed on the Creator's Mac — one line, two seconds' sleep, a second line, arriving two seconds apart on a terminal and **both at once, at exit, on a pipe**, after a note written later on the unbuffered error stream — **has no observation record anywhere in `Preparation/`**, so it is not evidence at this stage's bar and nothing here rests on it. *(This read **Posed on the Creator's Mac** with no such qualifier.)* **Why it mattered here rather than elsewhere:** a render whose progress sat in a buffer would present as a working render with no figures, which is byte-for-byte the shape of the genuine no-print case above, and this clause made that an ordinary state rather than something to look at. **Which of the two it is, is not distinguishable from the readings** — and on the answer above the case does not arise for a render's own lines at all. **Whether Blender is affected was written here as not known and not guessable, parked in `Open Threads` for a blind researcher; it is answered and no researcher is needed** — *Reports 14* measured it directly, with a reader on the far end of the pipe that was proved able to show both patterns before anything was concluded from it. **That parked item is spent and `Open Threads` is not this document's to edit; it is routed to the runner.** **Nothing was built on the answer either way**: no clause below infers an ending from silence, and E1 governs the buffered case and the genuine no-print case identically. What the answer changes is only that a live progress figure on the command-line route is live after all.

**R5. Readings travel worker → supervisor → desktop, and are period-held.** The worker reports its current readings to the supervisor over the endpoint at its own bounded interval; the supervisor holds them in its in-memory view — volatile by §1.2's own listing — and answers the desktop's queue reading with them on request — requests that recur under `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d while the entry is on screen, each answered as it comes, the supervisor holding no schedule for them. Every reading carries the moment it was taken and the clock it was taken on (D5); on the desktop it is period-held (§11.1) and nothing durable is written from it. **Across a supervisor restart the reporting re-establishes on the worker's own next interval, by `Rented-server runtime` W12's route and not by a contract of this document's**: the worker re-attempts its report at the endpoint's recorded path until it is accepted, and a supervisor that has written *resumed* accepts a report from a group recorded under an earlier run identifier. So a resumed render's entry carries no live figures for the gap and carries them again from the first accepted report — absent readings, which R4 already makes an ordinary state of the entry, never a stale one.
