The one live reading that is kept is the stream itself, in the sinks, which is Row 68's and not this rule's. **The worker's interval is two seconds**, and the supervisor holds the newest report per running job and carries it in that job's part of the queue reading's answer. *Added 2026-10-02, out of step 10c's map of the Creator's notes on 0.1.8 (note 7), on the build runner's ruling; no auditor has seen it.* **The readings are held as they stood when a stop's interrupt was issued on the render, and that job's part of the answer carries a *stopping* mark, until the render ends.** Blender goes on through the range after the first interrupt and saves nothing, so a later report is not the job's progress and is not taken in. The mark is the supervisor's own fact about its own interrupt, set whoever sent the stop: it is not one of R6's readings, which stay closed, and not a queue state, so Q2's set does not move and L12a's *stopping* on the desktop is unchanged. *(Added 2026-10-19, out of the live pass on Vast for the thirteenth and fourteenth notes: Queue read Frame 121 · Whole job 80% with 52 frames on the server, and Telegram frame 130 of 150. No auditor has seen it.)*

**R6. The readings the reader produces, and the closed list of them:** the frame in hand (R9); the view layer, in 4.5 only (R9); the largest sample count seen for the frame in hand, with the printed total beside it (R11); the frame's stopped-short mark, on an untiled frame only (R12, corrected 2026-08-31); the tile figures where a tiled line carries them (R13); Cycles' time estimate for the frame in hand, where a line carries one, marked as Blender's and as rough — the spec's own presentation rule; and the count of frames announced or completed, which is what the bars consume (Section 6); plus R6a’s simulation pair before rendering. Nothing else is read: no percentage (none in the stream ever moves), no card memory (nothing printed anywhere is marked as the card's — the closed door Row 66 walks past), no device name (that is a closing report, E12), **and no colour-management substitution line (that is E13a's observation at the copy loop, added 2026-09-02)**. **The list is not opened by E13a and the exclusion is stated rather than slipped past**: this list is the closed set of readings *the running-render reader produces*, and the two lines named as excluded are not in it for the same reason — neither is a progress reading, and each is observed at the worker's copy loop as its own act, on E13's discipline. **A builder adding anything to the list above has found a fault rather than a case, exactly as before.** **Corrected 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3b): *the view layer, in 4.5 only* is no longer true.** The view layer is read in every version that prints it — off 4.5's progress line, and from 5.0 off its own announcement at each layer's start (R10's correction below). That is the same reading, not a new one, and the list stays closed. *No auditor has seen this sentence.*

**R6a. Simulation progress before rendering. Added 2026-11-04, the build runner’s ruling.** The reader accepts `Rendering` L10b’s own `SMOOZY-SIMULATION` lines in every version: the frame being played and the highest frame, both signed scene numbers, or the closing line. These travel in the same worker report as R6’s readings, as an optional pair. They never count as rendered frames, samples, tiles or completed work. A closing line or the first render progress clears them. Missing or unreadable lines change nothing. Queue shows the pair without a bar or a time estimate.

**R7. The tiled line is on record end to end, and the reader's tiled grammar follows the recorded shape. Corrected 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** This clause read *what a tiled line holds end to end has never been recorded* and *every progress line kept anywhere came from a picture too small to be split*. **Both are false.** **Thirteen tiled lines are on record in full** — `Rendered 780/53284 Tiles, Sample 1/1` among them, and `Rendered 3/4 Tiles, Sample 0/16` ending a 2049 by 2049 run that exited 0 and wrote its picture. Label: **observed in a test**. The grammar reads the done-against-total tile figure and the sample pair beside it, in that shape, per version by R2's rule; a line it does not match still yields nothing rather than a wrong reading, by R3's. What [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 8 still names is the threshold at which a picture is split, not what a tiled line holds.

**A note in brackets can follow the sample pair, and the count is read before it. Added 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3b).** On this Mac's graphics card, 5.2.2 ended every count line, tiled or not, with a note in brackets — `Rendered 0/4 Tiles, Sample 129/1000 (1 kernels to optimize)`, `Sample 1000/1000 (Using optimized kernels)` — and the grammar read no count at all from such a line. Label: **observed in a test** (5.2.2, this Mac's card, the Creator's 4K, 1,000-sample shape with no noise threshold, 2026-10-04). So the grammar takes the count before any note in brackets, whatever the note says, in both grammars. Whether a rented card prints a note is not observed. *No auditor has seen this paragraph.*

**R8. The reader never fails the render and never slows it by more than its own copy loop.** Parsing is an observation on bytes already being copied; there is no second read of anything, and a reader error is recorded against the reader, never against the job.

**R9. The frame in hand: read off the line in 4.5, counted off the announcements in 5.0 and later.** 4.5's progress line names the frame; the newer three announce every frame, and frames are worked through ascending, one at a time, in the order given, under every engine on every machine tried — so the current frame is the last announcement, and a reader that knows the attempt's frame list (it does: the attempt record holds it, M8) knows which real frame that is. The statistics-handler route is not used: in the newer three its string no longer carries the frame number, and the handler exists only inside a script route `Rendering`'s Row 11 does not start renders by.

**R10. The renderlet is named where it can be and the frame alone where it cannot, and the fallback is the decision, not a gap.** In 4.5 the line names frame and view layer — one renderlet — and the entry names it. In 5.0 and later the view layer comes from nowhere at all, so the entry names the frame alone, which is the spec's own allowed fallback, taken here once and not revisited per job.

**CORRECTED 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3b): in 5.0 and later the view layer does come from somewhere — its own announcement — and the paragraph above is kept as the record of what it said.** 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 each print `Start rendering: <scene>, <view layer>` on the ordinary output at the start of every view layer of every frame, under Cycles and under EEVEE; 4.5.12 never prints it. Label: **observed in a test** — two view layers over two frames, Cycles on the processor and EEVEE, in all four versions on the Creator's Mac, 2026-10-04; the same line was already in the record for one layer (*Reports 3*'s single-frame opening) and in the Creator's own render record. **So the 5.0-and-later grammar reads that line, and the renderlet is named there as it is in 4.5**: each layer is its own unit, the readings start over at it (R11), and the entry names the layer. **Two limits, both observed the same day.** **First, the scene and the layer are split at the first `, `**, the rule 4.5's own field is read by, so a scene whose name holds `, ` gives a wrong layer name (`Start rendering: Shot, 01, Back, plate` in 5.2.2); each layer's wrong name still differs from the next one's, so the boundary holds and only the name shown is wrong. **Closed 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3c, on the build runner's ruling): the scene's name is known from the job's reading, so it is taken off the front of the line — this announcement and 4.5's own scene-and-layer field alike — and the text is split at its first `, ` only where the scene is not known**, or the line does not begin with it. *No auditor has seen this sentence.* **Second, 4.5's progress line names no layer under EEVEE** (`Fra:1 Mem:41.24M (Peak 41.24M) | Time:00:00.22 | Rendering 1 / 16 samples`), so there the entry names the frame alone and this paragraph's fallback stands. *No auditor has seen this paragraph.*

**R11. The sample count: the largest seen for the frame in hand, never the latest, and both figures are shown as printed.** A frame that stops short prints one line reading zero of the total just before it ends, so the latest reading is wrong at exactly the moment a frame ends early. And the count is a ceiling, not a quantity — five things in Cycles stand between the number given and the number a pixel gets, and the printed total can be a per-layer stored number rather than the count handed in at launch — so the entry shows the line's own figures as Blender's, and never substitutes Render Settings' number for the printed total, and never converts the pair into a claim of completion.

**Two bounds on that reading, added 2026-08-31 out of the repaired Blender research.** **First, *largest seen for the frame in hand* is a frame-wide reading on an untiled frame only.** Where the line carries a tile figure the sample number restarts inside each tile, counted against the scene's own undivided maximum (R13), so what is read there is that tile's count and it is shown beside the tile figure as the pair the line printed — never carried across tiles, never presented as the frame's count, and never fed to a samples bar (B4). **Corrected 2026-10-04 (piece 3c): it is fed to both bars now, through B4's arithmetic as corrected that day** — the tiles finished times the samples, plus this count — and it is still shown as printed and never as the frame's count. *No auditor has seen this sentence.* **Second, the count reaching its printed total is not evidence that a picture reached disk.** One recorded run prints `Sample 600/600` and then `Finished`, then `Cancelled`, exits 1 and leaves no file. Label: **observed in a test**. That is E3's rule met at the reading rather than at the ending, and it is why the clause above forbids converting the pair into completion — the forbidding is now an observation rather than a caution.

**The frame in hand's reading starts over at each named view layer. Added 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3b).** Every version starts each layer's count again at `Sample 0/N` — observed in all four on the Creator's Mac that day, two layers of one and two frames, and in 5.2.2 and 4.5.12 at 1,024 samples. A fold over the whole frame met the second layer's opening zero after the first layer's `N/N` and read it as R12's stopped-short mark, so the entry showed the first layer's figures, stopped short, for the whole second layer, and B3b filled the first bar to full through it. **So the largest seen is the renderlet's where the layer is named (R10), and the frame's only where it is not.** A named layer is the boundary; a falling count is still never one (B3a). *No auditor has seen this paragraph.*

**R12. Whether the trailing zero is the frame's stopped-short mark is decided by the tiling condition, and the condition is read off the line itself. Corrected 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** This clause read the trailing zero as the stopped-short mark *and as nothing else*, at every picture size. **The research carries that only for a picture small enough not to be split into tiles**: the runs behind it were 480 by 360 and 320 by 240. **At 2049 by 2049 a run that exited 0 and wrote its picture ends on `Rendered 3/4 Tiles, Sample 0/16`** — a trailing zero on a frame that finished — because the sample number restarts inside each tile (R13). Label: **observed in a test**. As written this clause marked such a frame stopped short, and the picture sizes the untiled reading was measured at are far below the ones a working job renders, so the untiled case is never assumed.

- **The deciding condition is the printed line, never a computed size: a line carrying a tile figure is a tiled frame, and a line carrying none is not.** 2048×2048 at the default tile size printed no tile figure; 2049×2049 printed four. This is why B2's open threshold costs nothing here — nothing about a reading turns on the arithmetic.
- **Untiled frame — the mark is produced, and the old clause is kept whole.** The trailing zero says the frame ended short of its count — converged, out of time, or stopped — and it does not say which; the entry states the fact without a cause, which is Row 62's own second quote kept.
- **5.3 prints no closing zero, and the mark has a second source. Added 2026-11-02 (*Reports 3*, *Reports 7*, *Reports 11*), outside any round; no auditor of this document has seen it.** A frame cut short goes `Sample 160/4096`, then `Finished`, and a finished tiled frame ends on `Sample 16/16` where 4.5 to 5.2 end on `Sample 0/16`. Label: **observed in a test**, the 5.3.0 Beta on the Creator's Mac. **So on an untiled frame the `Finished` stage word, with the largest count seen still short of its printed maximum, is the mark too.** That is the research's own one rule, the largest count against its maximum, read when the frame's sampling ended. It takes no version: in 4.5 to 5.2 the zero has set the mark by then, and a frame at its full count sets nothing. **`Finished` is read as the end of sampling and never as a picture on disk** (E3). ~~A frame already showing a tile figure is tiled, and its `Finished` line, which carries none, produces no mark.~~ **Struck 2026-11-02, the bullet below.**
- **Tiled frame — ~~no mark is produced at all~~ no line carrying a tile figure produces the mark.** The trailing zero there is the tile-boundary sample counter and carries no statement about the frame's ending; the entry says nothing about the frame having stopped short, and the figures are shown as printed beside the tile figure (R11, R13). ~~Nothing substitutes for the absent mark: a tiled frame's ending is read where every other ending is read, from the recorded outcome (R1, E2).~~ A tiled frame's ending is still read where every other ending is read, from the recorded outcome (R1, E2).
- **`Finished` is the mark on a frame with tiles too, in every version, at any count. Added 2026-11-02, the first 5.3 live pass and the build runner's ruling; no auditor of this document has seen it.** Every real 4K frame on a card has tiles, and the last tile figure printed is one short of the total, so both bars sat at 75 or 84 per cent through about 34 seconds of denoising and saving. On a tiled frame every count is a tile's, so *short of its count* means nothing there, and the mark says only that sampling on this unit has ended. **What was read.** Label: **observed in a test**, 2026-11-02, Cycles on the processor, the Creator's Mac, 2049 by 2049, with a full count and with adaptive sampling, on 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2, 5.2.3 and the 5.3.0 Beta: every frame's last tile line is `Rendered 3/4 Tiles`, then one `Finished`. 4.5 to 5.2 end the tile lines on `Sample 0/16` and 5.3 on `Sample 16/16`, so both read 75 per cent there. On two View Layers there is one `Finished` per layer, each before the next layer's announcement. Denoising and saving come after it. `Finishing` is a different word. A frame stopped part-way printed no `Finished` on 5.2.3 and 5.3.0 Beta, as *Reports 11* found for `Cancelled`. Linux and a card under OptiX print the same shape on 5.3 (`Audits/Build/Blender 5.3/The live pass — Vast — what happened.md`, item 5). **`Finished` is still never a picture on disk** (E3). The figures beside the bar stay as printed (R11, R13).

**R13. Tiles: shown where printed, and never combined with samples into one figure. The unit is settled, and the rule it forced is unchanged. Corrected 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** This clause read *whether a sample count counts inside a tile or across the whole frame is unsettled* and *every tiled run on record was a single-sample render*; both are false. **The sample number restarts inside each tile and is counted against the scene's own undivided maximum** — thirteen tiled lines on record carry it. Label: **observed in a test**. So the entry still shows the tile figure and the sample figure as two readings and computes nothing across them, now because the sample number is a tile's rather than because nobody had seen which it was (Section 6 B4 is where the bar copes; [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 8 is now the threshold alone). **Corrected 2026-10-04 (piece 3c): the entry still shows them as two readings, as printed, but the bars now compute across them** — B4 as corrected that day, the Specs' own *tiles multiplied by samples*. *No auditor has seen this sentence.*

**R14. The engine decides how much there is to read, and the entry reads what is there.** Cycles prints stage lines, sample lines, a memory figure and an estimate; EEVEE prints far fewer lines with its own sample count and no estimate; Workbench prints no progress line at all. Smoozy offers Cycles and EEVEE (`Render Settings`'), so a Workbench stream arises only under an adopted job, and reads as an entry with no live figures — truthfully, per R4.

**R15. Blender's own estimate is R6's reading spelled out: which field carries it, in which versions, and what the entry does on a line that has none. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 432.** R6 already names the reading and B9 already declines everything wider; what no clause said is where the figure is read from and that its absence is a state rather than a failure.

- **What Blender gives is the same in all four versions, and is read and never composed.** A `Remaining:` field on Cycles' progress lines, sitting between the elapsed time and the memory figure; covering **the frame in hand** and starting over at each new frame; absent from the first sample line, the final sample line and the `Finished` line; stated in minutes, seconds and hundredths. **No line in any version carries a remaining figure for the animation as a whole.** Label: **observed in a test** — all four versions on two operating systems, which agreed on the field's spelling, its units, where it appears and what it covers; every version's own sources document one sentence about a tooltip at the window and nothing about the printed field (`While a render runs`, *Reports 5*). It exists only where the stream prints at all, which is R4's dependence and `Rendering`'s Row 11.
- **The field parts at R2's one boundary and the two grammars carry it separately.** 4.5 writes `Remaining:00:00.05` with no space after the colon; 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2 write `Remaining: 00:00.05` with one. That is the whole of the difference, and it is read by each version's own grammar — no rule shared across the boundary reads it (R2).
- **EEVEE prints no estimate at all, in every version, and Workbench prints no progress line at all (R14).** So on those engines the part is **absent from the line**, not empty, not held over from an earlier line, and not apologised for; the same holds on a Cycles line that carries no field, by R3's rule that a line matching no grammar yields nothing. The entry never shows the last figure it saw.
- **It is shown as printed, marked as Blender's and as rough, and nothing is computed from it.** Not summed across frames, not carried into the next frame, not fed to either bar (B3, B5), not extended to the job or the machine (B9, W1a). **Smoozy re-labels no unit**: the figure is Blender's statement about the frame in hand, and on a multi-layer frame the entry states nothing about which renderlet it belongs to — no run on record separates them.

---

## 6. The two bars, and the countdown that is not offered

**Constraint:** Row 64 — both bars count samples, a renderlet's size is its tiles multiplied by its samples (Creator-stated, 2026-08-13), the sum honours whichever sample count Render Settings put in force, a total is a plan not a promise, and the bars are written to survive it being wrong. Fact J is unanswered and left the stage; the spec's own fallback is written into the promise.

**B1. The plan total is computed once, from the recorded reading, and carried on the entry.** For every frame the job covers, the reading already answers which view layers render at that frame (`Job files and outputs` I6); each such pairing is one renderlet; its planned samples are the count actually in force for that layer under Render Settings' rule — carried in the settings payload and the reading, never one number multiplied by everything; its tiles are B2's arithmetic. The total is the sum, per group member separately — a group is several counts, never one. **It is worked out on the machine**, by the render worker, from the recorded reading the job record holds once `Rendering` P1 step 4 has run, and it travels in every report (R5) and so in the queue reading. A job submitted without the file read has no reading on the desktop, and the machine always has one by then. *Added 2026-10-02, out of step 10c's map of the Creator's notes on 0.1.8 (note 7), on the build runner's ruling; no auditor has seen it.*

**B2. The plan's tile count is Smoozy's own grid arithmetic over the frame's output size and the file's tile size, and what is open about it is the threshold rather than the arithmetic. Corrected 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** Tiles = the count of tile-sized steps across the width times the count across the height, each rounded up — which reproduces 2048×2048 at the default 2048 giving one and printing no tile figure, and 2049×2049 giving four and printing four. **The arithmetic is now observed at five counts rather than at two boundary points — 2, 4, 1024, 225 and 53,284 — and an area rule is refused because it disagrees at three of them, giving 977, 215 and 53,081.** Label: **observed in a test**, the unequal-sided case included: one recorded run grew a single side, 2048×2048 to 2048×2049. **The reason this clause gave for the threshold being open — *every size tried grew both sides at once* — is false and is withdrawn; the unobserved-case label goes with it.** **The threshold at which a picture is split into tiles is still open, and no threshold is written here or anywhere below.** A frame the grid counts as several tiles that Blender treats as one, or the reverse, makes the plan total wrong by the difference, and B5 is what absorbs a wrong plan ([Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 8).

**B3. The first bar is the renderlet in hand, against its planned samples, where the frame is one tile.** It is filled from R11's largest-seen count against the renderlet's planned count, and it fills and finishes once per renderlet — a frame with three active layers is three fills, which is the spec's own sentence. A short-stopping frame ends the bar where the largest-seen reading stands; the trailing zero never drags it back (R11, R12).

**B3a. The first bar's unit is per version, because the renderlet boundary is observable in only one of them. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 377.** B3 as written reads as one rule for every version — *"a frame with three active layers is three fills"* — and **R10 has already established that in 5.0 and later the view layer comes from nowhere at all.** A bar cannot fill once per renderlet on a stream that never says a renderlet ended.

- **On 4.5 the unit is the renderlet**, exactly as B3 says: the progress line names frame and view layer, the boundary is on the stream, three active layers is three fills, and the line above the bar names the layer (R10).
- **On 5.0 and later the unit is the frame.** The bar fills once per frame against **the frame's whole planned samples — every renderlet B1 counted for that frame, summed** — and **it does not reset inside a frame.** The line above it names the frame alone, which is R10's fallback and the spec's own. **What the printed count does across a frame's view layers on those versions is on record nowhere in this project, and [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 28 carries it with what breaks — added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it.** **This arithmetic does not move on the strength of that item**: it is written to survive either answer, and changing it would state what Blender does. **Narrowed 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3b): this bullet now holds only where no layer is named.** R10 as corrected that day reads the view layer's own announcement in 5.0 and later, so the renderlet boundary is on the stream there too and the unit is the renderlet, as on 4.5. The frame stays the unit where nothing names the layer. *No auditor has seen this sentence.*
- **So the reset the Design document is protecting against does not arise on those versions, because there is no reset.** Its *which of how many* fallback is a label for a boundary the stream does not carry; **the count is available from B1 and the ordinal is not**, and no ordinal is composed.
- **Nothing infers a renderlet boundary from the sample reading falling.** A restarting count would be the obvious signal and it is refused: R11 takes the largest seen for the frame in hand precisely because the latest is wrong at an ending, and R12 fixes what the trailing zero means and what it does not. **Corrected 2026-08-31:** this bullet read that a restarting count *would be modelling a stream nobody has observed saying it*, and **a restarting count is now on record — the sample number restarts inside each tile (R13), which is a tile boundary and never a renderlet's.** The refusal is unchanged and is now the stronger statement: a falling or restarting count is a tile boundary where the line carries a tile figure and an ending where it does not, and neither is the boundary this bullet is about.
- **B5 and B6 are untouched.** The plan total is B1's and is per renderlet whatever the bar's unit is; the second bar's arithmetic does not change.

**B3b. A unit that has ended takes its bar to the end, and that is a different act from reading a larger count. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 378.** B3's last sentence left a short-stopping frame's bar *"where the largest-seen reading stands"* — the 200 of 500 the Design document names as the thing to avoid.

- **The rule is: the bar never moves backwards, and never sits short of full for a unit that has ended.** The first is R11's and is kept whole; the second is new and does not touch it, because **filling on an ending is not a sample reading at all.**
- **The ending signal is R12's trailing zero on an untiled frame, used for exactly what R12 says it means there** — the frame ended short of its count — and not as a figure. **In 5.3, which prints no such zero, it is R12's `Finished` on a frame short of its count (added 2026-11-02), so the bar goes to its end before denoising and saving.** ~~**On a tiled frame there is no such signal at all**, R12 as corrected on 2026-08-31 giving the trailing zero no stopped-short meaning where the line carries a tile figure, a finished frame printing one.~~ **On a tiled frame the signal is R12's `Finished` (added 2026-11-02), in every version, because the trailing zero means nothing there and `Finished` does.** So B4's tiled bar goes to its end before denoising and saving, and it never moves back or past full: the fill is a hundred, not a figure added to the count. Where no trailing zero and no `Finished` was seen, the unit's end is the next unit beginning or the job's own ending, both of which are already read.
- **The figures are unchanged and are still shown as printed.** The bar is taken to its end; **the counts beside it still read 200 of 500**, because R11's *both figures are shown as printed* is what stops the fill becoming a claim that 500 samples were taken.
- **B5's bound is what makes this safe rather than a lie**: the bar is progress against the planned units, and *"a job can correctly end short of it or pass it"*. A filled bar says *this unit is done*, not *this unit did all the work planned for it*.

**B4. Where the frame is tiled, the first bar says what it is showing, and what it shows is tiles. Corrected 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** The tile line carries done-against-total and moves; **the sample count's unit inside a tiled frame is settled and it is the tile's — the number restarts inside each tile against the scene's own undivided maximum (R13)** — so the bar is filled from the tile figures and labelled as tiles, and no samples arithmetic is run across a tiled frame. That was already the rule and none of it moves; what changed is that it rests on an observation rather than on an unsettled unit. **And the tile figure is not what takes the bar to full**: the 2049 by 2049 run that exited 0 and wrote its picture ends on `Rendered 3/4 Tiles, Sample 0/16` (R7, R12), so a bar filled from the figures alone sits short on a frame that finished. **B3b is what fills it**, on the unit's ending rather than on a reading, and the figures beside it still read as printed — the same arrangement B3b already makes for a short-stopping untiled frame. Where a tiled line matches no grammar (R7, R3), the bar shows no percentage and the entry shows the figures it has — the spec's fallback, taken exactly.

**CORRECTED 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3c, on the build runner's ruling): the bar is not filled from the tile figure alone, and samples arithmetic is run across a tiled frame. The paragraph above is kept as the record of what it said.** It was against the Specs, which say *"A renderlet's size is plain arithmetic: its tiles multiplied by its samples. A renderlet of four tiles in a file set to 500 samples is 2000 units of work"* and *"Nothing here tries to be clever about tiles … A simple count is honest about what it is measuring"* (`Specs/Queue & History`, **Creator-stated** 2026-08-13). Filled from the tile figure alone, both bars sat at nought through the whole first tile of the Creator's test shape — 80 to 90 seconds while the tile's own count climbed 1, 257, 648 of 1,000 — observed at the desk in all four versions on 2026-10-04. The Creator said the same day that the whole-job bar is simple arithmetic over samples known at submission and must move finely even on a job of one or two renderlets. **So the part done of a tiled renderlet is the tiles finished times the samples, plus the samples of the tile in hand, over its tiles times its samples, and both bars take it: one rule.**

- **The tile count is the one Blender printed**, never worked out from the picture — the Specs' *"nothing here may work out how many tiles a picture will be cut into; the counts are read from what Blender prints"*. The samples are the renderlet's planned count on the machine (B3) and, on the desktop, which holds no per-renderlet plan, the total Blender printed beside the count (B6's paragraph of 2026-10-03).
- **The tile in hand adds its count only while it is short of the total printed beside it.** The line that counts a tile finished repeats that tile's full count, and the next tile's own count follows: `Rendered 0/4 Tiles, Sample 1000/1000`, then `Rendered 1/4 Tiles, Sample 1000/1000`, then `Rendered 1/4 Tiles, Sample 1/1000`. Label: **observed in a test** — 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 on the Creator's Mac, 2026-10-04, the Creator's 4K, 1,000-sample shape on the graphics card. So a full count beside a tile figure is never the tile in hand's, and counting it would put a whole tile on the bar that nothing has rendered. **Not observed:** whether a tile stopped short by the noise threshold repeats its short count beside the next tile's figure. If it does, a report taken on that one line counts that many samples early, at most one tile's.
- **B3b still fills a unit that has ended.** A finished frame's trailing `Rendered 3/4 Tiles, Sample 0/16` still reads three tiles of four, and the unit's end takes the bar to full, as above. **That end is `Finished` where it is printed (R12, added 2026-11-02).**
- **B1's total and the whole-job figure count the same units.** B1 counts each renderlet as its tiles times its planned samples. B6's finished part counts ended renderlets whole in those units, and the renderlet in hand as this fraction of its own units. B1's tile count is B2's grid, because the total is worked out before Blender prints anything; the fraction reads the printed figure, so a grid that disagrees with Blender's tiling misprices the total, which B5 absorbs, and never the fraction.
- **What does not change:** the bar is still labelled as tiles, the figures beside it are still the tile pair and the tile's own sample pair as printed (R11, R13), and a tiled line no grammar reads still shows no percentage.

R11's *never fed to a samples bar* and R13's *computes nothing across them* are corrected with this paragraph, and [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 19 closes. *No auditor has seen this paragraph.*

**B5. The second bar is the whole job against the plan total, presented as progress against the plan, and it survives the plan being wrong by saying what it is.** Adaptive sampling can finish a correct job short of the total; B2's assumption can misprice a frame; both are the spec's own *a total is a plan, not a promise*. The bar's claim is bounded to the plan — it is progress against the planned units, and a job can correctly end short of it or pass it.

**B6. The second bar advances on completed frames, in plan units.** A frame that counts as already there under `Job files and outputs` C12–C13 contributes its every renderlet's planned work, whole; a frame not yet complete contributes the first bar's current renderlet progress and nothing for its unstarted layers. The unit reconciliation Row 64 demands is exact and rests on a Blender write rule this document carries as **Creator-stated (2026-07-24)** — the Glossary's **Output** entry, which is neither a joined answer nor a row-file quotation, and the label is stated here because the reconciliation is called exact on the strength of it: a frame's outputs land only once every view layer active on that frame has finished. **The label is the end of the trail, stated here for the first time 2026-08-23.** The three Glossary entries that follow **Output** all carry *"to be confirmed at Blender Research"* — **Regular output path**, **Compositor**, **File output node** — and they are the only three in that file that do; the **Output** entry carries no such words, and `Glossary.sources.md`'s entry behind it names the Creator and no publication. **no research question covers this write rule, in any version or on either machine**, so nothing later confirms or overturns it and a reader must not expect a research answer behind it. **Breaks if it is wrong:** the reconciliation is not exact and a frame counted whole by B6 can contribute plan units for work that never landed; **what holds:** B5's plan-not-promise presentation, and the first bar, which reads the stream rather than the reconciliation. And *already there* is per-file and refuses empties — so frames are the granularity at which finished renderlets are knowable, and the bar counts renderlets through frames without pretending to see inside an unfinished one.

**B6's finished part is worked out on the machine, and the desktop draws it over B1's total. Added 2026-10-03, out of step 10c's piece 6a account §6, on the build runner's ruling of that day; no auditor has seen it.** B1's total is the machine's (B1), and the desktop holds no per-frame plan to set against it, so **the render worker carries one more figure in every report (R5), beside the total: the plan units of the frames finished so far** — every frame of the range the attempt does not ask for (B7's complement), and every frame of the attempt's own list up to and including the newest one whose save has been announced (P1a's figure; frames are worked through in the order given, so that is the list's finished prefix), each frame counted through B1's own renderlets and B2's arithmetic. **The second bar is that figure over B1's total, both from the machine**, presented under B5. **It is never the total divided by the frames**: view layers switch on and off over the range (I6), so one frame's units are not another's. **What this costs, stated rather than left for a reader to find:** the frame in hand's share — B6's *"the first bar's current renderlet progress"* — is not carried, so the second bar moves when a frame's save is announced and not inside a frame; a job of one long frame reads nothing on its second bar until that frame is saved. The first bar is untouched and still shows the frame in hand.

**CORRECTED 2026-10-04: the paragraph above kept less than the spec promises, and the frame in hand's share is carried.** Found by the Creator's note 13 on the test copy — *"Overall progress doesn't show anything. A renderlet's progress does though"* — on a job of one frame, whose second bar sat at nothing for its whole render, exactly as the paragraph's own last sentence said it would. **The spec is plain** (`Specs/Queue & History`, *A rendering job adds what is happening now*, **Creator-stated** 2026-07-24 and 2026-08-13): both bars count samples and count the same thing, and the second is *"the same sum over every frame-and-view-layer pairing the job will render"* — so the work inside the frame in hand counts, and a figure that moves only when a frame is saved is a narrower promise. B6's first paragraph already said so; the ruling of 2026-10-03 traded it away. **So the render worker's finished figure is**: the plan units of the frames finished so far, as above; **plus, for the frame in hand, the planned units of its renderlets that have ended** (B3b's ending — the next unit beginning, or the stopped-short mark), **plus the first bar's fraction of the renderlet in hand times that renderlet's planned units**. That frame's share is capped at its own planned units, and **the figure never moves backwards**: it is never less than the last figure the worker sent, so a count restarting at the next layer, or a frame stopped short, cannot pull it down. It travels in the same report (R5), the desktop still draws it over B1's total and computes nothing, and B5's plan-not-promise presentation is unchanged. **What still rests on Blender's printed counts**: the frame in hand's share is read from the same lines the first bar reads (R11), so where they print nothing the share is nothing and the figure moves when the frame is saved, as before. The paragraph above is kept as written, as the record of the ruling it was. *An Architecture clause that kept less than the spec promises is a plan fault put right here; Rule 10 does not reach it. No auditor has seen this paragraph.* **Added later on 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3b): the stopped-short mark ends a renderlet only where the layer is named.** Each layer's count opens with `Sample 0/N` (R11's paragraph of that day). Where the layer is named — every version under Cycles, since R10's correction — the readings start over at each layer, the zero lands on a fresh renderlet, and the mark means what R12 says. Where no layer is named, a frame of several renderlets counts its readings as a part of one renderlet, the smallest, and the mark does not finish the frame, since it may be a later layer's opening zero; the frame is whole on its save. Label for the opening zero: **observed in a test**, 2026-10-04, all four versions on the Creator's Mac. *No auditor has seen this sentence.*

**Added 2026-10-17, the Creator's eleventh notes, note 1: a frame of the attempt's list also counts as finished once a later frame of that list has begun**, because frames are worked in the order given (P1a); the save announcement still counts too, whichever comes first, and the figure still never moves backwards. Found on a 90-frame job whose only output was a video: no save is ever announced for a video (§22 item 11, *what an encode prints*, is permanently unresolved), so the paragraph above held the second bar at one frame in ninety — 1% — for the whole render. A frame beginning is read from the same printed lines the first bar reads (R11). *A hole in B6 and P1a — nothing said how the second bar moves for a job that announces no save — put right on the build runner's word; no auditor has seen this paragraph.*

**B7. A re-run picks its bar up from the reckoning, and falls back where trust does.** The carried progress is the plan units of every frame this attempt does not ask for — the complement of the attempt record's frame list against the range — computed on the machine, at the attempt's start, by `Job files and outputs` C1's own reading, not remembered from the desktop. That complement is **not** the set the reckoning counted already there, and is one frame short of it: D7's list carries C17's last frame asked for, re-rendered anyway though the reckoning counted it there. The carried figure is that frame's renderlets low, which is the truthful reading of work this attempt will do again and is not corrected. Where that cannot be trusted — a video-shaped job, which has no already-there machinery at all (D9), or no previous attempt record readable (C20) — the bar shows only the work still to do, which is the spec's stated fallback. **Which of B6 and this clause owns a frame both could count is stated here rather than left open — added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it: the already-there set owns it, and the carried figure covers only frames that set does not hold.** The two sets are very nearly the same on a re-run — this clause says so itself, that the complement is not the set the reckoning counted already there and is one frame short of it — and neither said which owned the overlap, so a frame in both was counted twice. **B6's set is the authoritative one** because it is a reckoning over what is actually on disk, computed by `Job files and outputs`' own reading and unable to double-count itself, and because B6 carries a recorded write rule behind it that this clause does not.

**B8. Fact J's case uses the spec's fallback and the fallback's own trigger is the unanswered part. Rewritten 2026-09-02.** This clause read *"Until it answers … where the answer, when it comes, marks a file's set untrustworthy"* — written as though a round were on its way, which [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 4 forbids by name. The plan needs the active view layers per frame before the job runs; that is answered for a keyframed switch in all four versions on both machines and unanswered for a switch driven or changed by a script inside the file — and whether such a file can even be told apart by the reading is the same unanswered question. **It left this stage for Blender Research, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that there will be no further Blender research: it is permanently unresolved, no round is coming, and nothing here waits on one.** So, for every file: **the total is computed from the reading's evaluated per-frame set**, and **no file is routed to the fallback**, because the condition that would route one — a set the reading cannot be trusted on — is the unanswered question itself, and the trigger for it will not now be established. **The spec's fallback wording stands, unchanged and unreached:** where a job's set is ever marked untrustworthy on some other ground, that job's second bar says what it is showing rather than showing a percentage — the promise's own words. **Breaks:** a driven or scripted file carries a percentage of a wrong total, bounded by B5's plan-not-promise presentation; [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 4 carries the rest of the cost. **What holds:** the bars' survival clauses, B5's plan-not-promise bound, and every other file shape. The promise is not rewritten.

**B9. No countdown to the end of a job is offered, and the unused permission is recorded here.** Row 65 is a licence with two conditions — it firms up as the job runs, and it is always an estimate — and the research closes the ground under the first: no running figure covers a whole animation in any version, the whole-job total is a plan adaptive sampling legitimately undershoots, and Blender's own estimate covers the frame in hand and comes and goes. A countdown built from elapsed time and counted frames would be a confident figure Smoozy cannot stand behind on a machine billed by the hour, which is the exact harm the spec's *no promise* clause names. So: the entry shows Blender's own per-renderlet estimate as Blender's and as rough (R6), and nothing wider is offered. This is the row where *nothing is offered* is the answer, recorded as the answer rather than left as an unused permission.

---

## 7. Readings taken from the machine

**Constraint:** Row 66 — the whole card's usage against its capacity, and the disk beside it; both are facts on the entry and neither may be narrowed to the render. Facts 43, 28 and 46 ([Facts](Facts.md); [Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md); Fact 28's mount reading is [Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md)).

**G1. The card reading is per card, card-level, taken by the supervisor through the driver's own per-card query, unprivileged, on request.** Per card: identity, total, used — the observed route returned exactly that, with the idle card reading zero at the same instant, which rules out any machine-wide figure. Card-level and never process-level: the two differ by driver context not attributed to the process, and the row's own reason stands — the two opposite endings a full card gives turn on who filled the card, which a reading of the render alone would answer neither way. The count is read as a count and never as a parsed total — the per-card query returns the value once per card and a whole-output parse reads `22` on a two-card box.

**G2. Free is not total and the entry never conflates them.** 483 MiB of each observed card was already held by the driver at idle. The entry shows used against capacity as read; capacity is the server list's — `Servers`' — and the live usage is this document's, per the spec's own split.

**G3. On a container the host-inherited routes lie and are not used.** `/proc/driver/nvidia` and the PCI scan are the host's unfiltered view; card presence and readings come from the driver's own answer, selected by the detected machine kind — `Rented-server runtime` V6 entire, cited. A reading taken from a container is not a reading of the container, and nothing here forgets it.

**G4. The disk figure is the available figure at the root's mount, read per-path, and shown as of its moment.** Free space is a choice of mount — 51 filesystems on one plain reading, one of them the host's own disk showing space the rental may not use — and the mount that matters to a rendering job is the one its outputs fill: the root of `Job files and outputs` 1.1, under which every job folder sits. The reading's mechanism is `Server protocol and transfers` §9.1's; the promise's reach is `File Browser`'s reading, which the spec ties this figure to and which this document does not exceed. Nothing is cleared, warned about or decided from the figure — §11.4 — and a writability test is never a signal.

**G5. Both readings travel the health-readings route and are period-held.** Taken on request, never on a schedule (V2); carried through `Servers`' connection to the entry; each carries its moment and its clock (D5, V5); nothing durable is written from either — except that `Servers`' Row 28 remembers hardware on its own terms, which is that document's and not this one's. Fact 43's remainder — two renders on two cards, one render spanning both, cross-login attribution — is carried at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 7 and bites nothing here, because the figure shown is card-level and attribution is never claimed.

**G5a. The requests G5 leaves as *on request* are two, and there are no others. Added 2026-09-02, per the disk-fill scenario replay's finding N-e.** G5 said the readings are taken on request and never on a schedule, and **no document named a request that takes them**: `Rented-server runtime` V2 makes every health reading on request and names only the Telegram worker as its exception, `Desktop app and delivery` [P10d](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery)'s closed set excludes a health reading by name, and `Servers` H15 is the hardware reading rather than these. As written every clause was satisfiable by never taking one, so Row 66's figures could stand promised and never shown.

- **The two, and the list is closed.** With **`Servers` [Q1](Servers.md#10-the-first-connection-in-order-rows-36-and-35) step 7's queue reading**, on every primary connection, after the installation identity confirms — one request per connection, carried on the same connection as the reading it rides. And at **the user's own act of opening a job's entry**, once per opening.
- **Each is a reading the user's own act already causes**, which is `State, identity and recovery` [§8.4](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#84-nothing-is-polled-to-withdraw-a-record)'s third moment in its own words. **No watcher, no timer and no schedule is added**, so V2 stands unqualified and `Desktop app and delivery` P9's *nothing on this side polls* is untouched.
- **Neither recurs, and the entry says the figures as of their moment.** P10d's closed set is what repeats inside a standing period and a health reading is excluded from it by name, so an entry left open shows the figures it was given, with the moment and the clock they were taken on (D5, V5, G4), until one of the two acts above happens again. **Nothing on the entry ticks and nothing re-reads to keep a figure fresh.**
- **Where no connection stands, no reading is taken and the entry shows none.** Absence, which R4 already makes an ordinary state of the entry rather than a fault, and which N17 already covers for an entry whose machine is gone.

---

## 8. The ending, and the words

**Constraint:** Rows 67, 68, 69. `Rented-server runtime` §11 owns the captured streams, the exit and the closed set of four collection outcomes; M20–M22 own the ending's ground; Row 286's presentation is `Desktop app and delivery`'s. Nothing below re-derives any of it; what is decided is what the entry does with it.

**E1. The ending is decided by the render having ended, and the entry's state comes from records alone.** A job's state comes from the operation record, the execution group's validation and the reaped exit status, never from either stream (M20). A group that is gone with no reaped status is an ending with no status, and an ending with no status is failed (M21) — the entry moves to failed the same as any other, and nothing stays *rendering* because nothing was printed. The reverse also holds: a stream that has gone quiet is not an ending, and the entry stays *rendering* while the group validates — a run that sits forever is a live run the user can stop, and no timer of Smoozy's ends a render, because a legitimate render has no bound and a timer would decide for the user. The limit the joined answer demands — *anything waiting on a run to finish needs a limit of its own* — is on waiters, each with its own deadline (C16), never on the run.

**E2. The entry's four endings map from recorded outcomes and from nothing else.** *Stopped* ← the outcome **left stopped** (L11); *given up* ← the outcome **given up** (D12); *finished* and *failed* ← the render kind's own ending, whose classification is `Rendering`'s to define and is owed there — with two rules already fixed for it by closed documents that this document reads as constraints: an ending with no status is failed (M21), and a successful exit status is never read as *everything was written* (M22) — **twenty separate endings report success, six of them ending at 0 with an empty output folder** (corrected 2026-08-31 out of the repaired Blender research; this read *twelve*, and the six are why M22's rule is the sharper fact rather than the count) — and the exit number never names a cause, 137, 134, 135 and 139 each arriving from unrelated events (`Rented-server runtime` X13, M23). **One limb of `Rendering`'s classification reads the completeness statement: a render that ended at 0 having written none of what the job owed is *failed* (`Rendering` K14b; added 2026-11-02, outside any round; no auditor of this document has seen it).** The entry still maps from the recorded outcome and from nothing else.

**E3. *Finished* is a statement about the render's ending, never about the outputs, and the completeness statement stands beside it.** The count coming up short is C27's statement, named per path (C28); `Download` measures arrivals against the count; the entry never converts exit 0 into *everything was written* and never converts a short count into a failure the render did not report. The two are shown as the two facts they are. **Limited 2026-11-02, a hole (*Reports 1*, *Reports 12*): a count that found nothing at all of what the job owed is no longer only a short count.** It is `Rendering` K14b's *failed*, decided on the machine at the ending, and the entry maps from that outcome as from any other and converts nothing itself. **The reason line *All frames rendered.* stands on *finished* alone** (`Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md`, *A clean finish*), so it no longer stands over nothing. ~~Over a partly written job it stands as it did: the ruling left that job as it is.~~ **Reversed 2026-11-02, the 5.3 integration's piece 5, the build runner's ruling, a hole under Rule 8: the line does not show where the count says only part of what was owed was written.** The outcome stays *finished*, and only the words change: *3 of 4 files written.*, C27's files present of its files expected, with a file at zero bytes not present, as C27 keeps it. **Where there is no count at all, because the inventory withheld itself or the walk failed, the line stands as it did**: no count says anything against it. **The count reaches the desktop on the note's answer** (the fields of N3's paragraph) and is kept beside the record in the same act as the ending, so History and a restart read one thing. No auditor of this document has seen this.

**The run behind that refusal, recorded here 2026-08-31 out of the repaired Blender research: neither a full sample count nor the word *Finished* means a picture reached disk.** One render prints `Sample 600/600`, then `Finished`, then `Cancelled`, exits 1 and leaves no file. Label: **observed in a test**. So *Finished* is not merely a statement about the ending rather than the outputs — it can stand on a run that wrote nothing, beside a full count and beside a stated cancellation. Nothing in this document reads either as evidence of a written file: the completeness statement is C27's walk (H7), R11 forbids converting the printed pair into completion, and the ending's own words are kept and never mined (E10).

**E4. The silence is stated, and which silence it is comes from the collection outcome, not from the sink.** *The render printed nothing* and *nothing of what the render printed reached Smoozy* are the same on a naive entry and are not the same event; §11.3's closed set of four — *collected*; *collected, and the stream was empty*; *collection incomplete*; *never established* — is what tells them apart, resting on two different records rather than on the sink. The entry carries the outcome per stream per attempt and `Desktop app and delivery` 16.3 presents each as its own sentence; an empty panel is never what the user meets. This document adds nothing to the mechanism and takes nothing from it.

**E5. Some endings never arrive at all, and the entry is honest about that shape too.** Two observed 4.5 cases print a message and then sit for ever; they read as live work, because they are live processes, and the stop is the user's route — E1's rule. This document does not manufacture an ending for them, and the idle machinery never fires while a render operation runs. **The occasion is named 2026-09-01**, scenario replay *The provider reboots the machine* finding 3: `What Build inherits` item 2(a) has since established that **an engine named on the launch line whose add-on is absent does not exit at all on 4.5**, so this is a cause a builder meets on a first job rather than two curiosities — and **D4's re-run is the machine's own act**, so the case recurs on attempt 2 with nobody connected. `Rendering` K15a and K15b hold the whole of it; the mechanism here is unchanged and nothing a builder builds moves.

**E6. A dropped connection is not a stop, and no clause of this section fires for one.** The render carries on unwatched; the entry, when next readable, shows whatever the records say. The launch arrangement that makes this true — the render started detached — is `Rendering`'s Row 19, owed there; this document's half is that nothing here reads a connection's end as an event about a job.

**E7. Everything Blender said is kept because the sinks are the record, and the entry is where the user reaches it.** Both streams, captured apart by the worker into sinks the supervisor made, retained verbatim as a prefix under M4a's finite ceiling, unedited, kept until the job's machine-side record ends, with any loss stated by M13's `collection incomplete` outcome — `Rented-server runtime` M1–M8 including M4a, M13, R1, R2, taken entire. The marker is part of the raw record; the shell's own lines on Linux are in it too, unedited, because the record is the stream and not a digest of it.

**E8. The live commentary and the kept words are one capture serving two promises.** While the job runs, the desktop reads the sink's current bytes over the connection, on request, as a period-held reading shown on the entry — reading material for display, never state. **The read recurs while the commentary is on screen, on `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d's live-reading sequence, and its repetition stops when the commentary is not** — added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R1; that recurrence is what makes the spec's *read as it arrives rather than arriving in one lump at the end* true, and *on request* includes that sequence's passes. After the ending, the same sinks are what history carries. **The desktop learns where the sinks are from the queue reading**: for each running job the answer names the attempt's two sinks and their current lengths, so the desktop composes no machine path, and each read asks only for the bytes past the length it last read. *Added 2026-10-02, out of step 10c's map of the Creator's notes on 0.1.8 (note 7), on the build runner's ruling; no auditor has seen it.* **The entry's own three lines are on screen too.** A rendering entry showing the ordinary stream's newest three lines (`Desktop app and delivery` M13b's narrowing) is commentary on screen for this clause, so its read recurs on the same sequence while the entry is drawn and stops when it is not; it reads the ordinary stream only. Its first read takes the stream's last 4 KB rather than the whole, and each read after takes only the new bytes. *Added 2026-10-03, out of step 10c's map of the Creator's notes on the test copy (note 12), on the build runner's ruling; no auditor has seen it.*

**E9. The history record takes the words in at reconciliation, and from then on they survive the machine.** §7 step 4's fill-in names *the machine's own words*: on a connection that learns a job's ending, the desktop fetches both captured streams and the per-stream collection outcomes into the job's history record, whole or not at all (M11), kept apart, unedited. **The fetch covers every attempt of the job that carries a capture record, never the ending attempt alone — added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R17.** Two sinks and two outcomes per attempt (M19, E11), each attempt's pair fetched whole or not at all; so a re-run job's entry holds what the interrupted attempt said up to the interruption beside the ending attempt's own words — the record of whether the render was healthy when the machine went down, which would otherwise be readable only while connected to that machine and die with the rental. The history record is user-held, so the words live as long as the entry — the measured cost is **about 5 kB per frame on the ordinary stream under 4.5, about 3.7 kB under 5.0**, and a measured zero on the complaints stream, so a ten-thousand-frame job is roughly 50 MB at the higher of the two, spent on the user's own history under their own control, and nothing expires it. Where the machine is gone before any connection learned the ending, the words are gone with it and the entry says it learned nothing — the spec's own boundary.

**E10. Nothing reads the words.** Not sorted, not grouped, not mined for a cause — no version hands out a failure in a form a program could act on, and four wordings exist for one failure (M24, C26). Smoozy keeps them and shows them. The three things observed *at the loop* rather than read from the sink are E13's device line, E13a's colour-substitution line and P1a's frame-save announcement, each an observation of bytes in passing, not a reading of the record. *(Two until 2026-09-02, when E13a was added.)*

**E11. A re-run's words are the new attempt's own.** Two sinks and two outcomes per attempt, never merged (M19); the entry reaches each attempt's record, and history keeps what it fetched per attempt.

**E12. The device line is taken only from a job whose recorded outcome is *finished*, and the entry says *not known* everywhere else. Corrected 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** This clause read that the line *exists only for a finished job* — a closing report printed after the last sample and before the frame is written, so *a job stopped part-way never produces it* — and that sentence was the whole ground for trusting a line found on the stream. **It is false.** After a stop, **5.0 and 5.1 print the whole closing block, 5.2 prints a cancelled line and no block, and 4.5 prints nothing**; and **5.2's own fix list calls that block on exit fake, with wrong numbers.** Label: **observed in a test**, with that version's own publication behind the last part. **So a device line observed on a run that did not finish is not evidence, and it is never read**: the entry states the job's devices only where the line was observed on a job whose recorded outcome is *finished* (E2), and says the devices are not known for every other ending — a stopped run that printed a block included. The rest of the clause stands unchanged: every observed result is Cycles' own, so nothing establishes an EEVEE job names anything; and the settings read back afterwards can name a card while the processor did the work, so nothing is ever guessed from them. Where it can be had the entry states it plainly; where it cannot the entry says the job's devices are not known — both halves are the spec's own words, kept.

**E12a. What was *asked for* is a different fact from what did the work, Smoozy holds it for every job on every ending, and the entry states it whatever the ending was. Added 2026-09-02.** E12 is right about the line and wrong about what the entry is therefore left with. **The ending on which the answer matters most is the one E12 leaves silent**: a job that failed for want of memory is a job the user must decide about — submit again on a machine with more of it, or move the render off the cards — and the entry as E12 leaves it says the devices are not known. **They are not unknown. They were chosen, and the choice is Smoozy's own record.**

- **The fact and where it already lives.** The resolved device choice — cards or processor — is `Render Settings` D1's payload entry, composed at submission from the user's own answer or the file's own value where they gave none. **Two copies already exist and neither is derived from a reading**: in the manifest on the machine (`Job files and outputs` M1's field list), published with the job's material before the job record exists (Q3); and on the desktop in the local job record as part of the kept setup, which is what *submit again* opens the page from (`Rendering` A1) and which N10's merge rule leaves alone — no reading fills it and no reading overwrites it. **So it survives the machine** (N10–N11, C11), which the device line does not, and it needs no fill-in, no connection and no stream. This is the same two-copy shape N10a already uses for the Blender version.
- **What the entry says, and the two are never one field.** The entry states **what the job was asked to render on**, on every ending and on a job still running, in words that name it as the ask. It states **what did the work** only where E12 permits — the line observed on a job whose recorded outcome is *finished*. **Neither substitutes for the other and the entry never merges them.** Where the line is not permitted, the entry says the devices are not known, exactly as E12 requires, **beside** the ask rather than instead of it. Wording and placement are Design's.
- **Why the ask is not evidence of what ran, stated here so nobody promotes it.** `Rendering` L21a measured a machine whose every card reported itself enabled while the backend selector was off; L24a has a device argument misspelt by one character swallowed in silence, the file's own settings deciding, the render going to the processor and exiting 0; and E12's own second half has settings read back afterwards naming a card while the processor did the work. **So a job asked for the cards can render on the processor and say nothing about it**, and that is precisely why the two facts are stated as two. **A builder that fills the device field from the ask has broken E12**, and a reader that infers the device from the ask has made the mistake L21a exists to forbid.
- **What it costs, and what it does not.** Nothing is read, nothing is computed, no store is added and no launch changes. The one thing gained is that a memory failure's entry names the silicon the job was pointed at, which is what a re-submission turns on. **It is not a told line** and adds none: [17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)'s one told line is the ending and its *does not carry* list now excludes the ask for the same reason it excludes the device line.
- **Only a Cycles job states the ask. Added 2026-11-02, the build runner's ruling that only Cycles names a technology (`Rendering` L21), the live pass on real production files; no auditor has seen it.** Under any other engine the device choice decides nothing: the render page draws no such pair, no device goes on the line, and the file's saved Cycles device is a value nothing reads. An EEVEE job seen on the card read *Renders on CPU*. So the entry states no ask where the engine in force is known and is not Cycles: the user's pick, else the file's own as the entry holds it. **Where it is not known**, as on an unread job, the row stays as it was. **The record is untouched**, so *submit again* opens as it did, and a job that printed a device line still says what did the work (E12).

**E13. The line is observed at the worker's own copy loop, as a step of the ending, and carried with the outcome.** Asking every render for the line, and the switch that produces it, are `Rendering`'s Rows 10 and 11, owed there — including the version split: 4.5 reaches it only through a Cycles debugging switch writing to the complaints stream, the newer three through an ordinary logging switch on the ordinary output. This document's half: the render worker observes the line as the bytes pass its copy loop on whichever stream the version's route prints it — the same discipline as M28's marker observations, never a mining of the sink afterwards — and records it in the attempt's record, where step 4's fill-in carries it to the entry and into history. Where `Rendering` fixes the route otherwise, its ruling wins; the observation point moves with it.

**E13a. The colour-substitution line is observed the same way the device line is, on both streams, and the entry states what the machine did not have. Added 2026-09-02.**

**Constraint, and it is a spec ruling rather than a row.** `Preparation/Specs/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` settled on 2026-09-02 that **no module supplies the machine's colour management configuration**, and that what closes the hole is the telling: *"where a render's file asks for a colour space or a view the machine does not hold, **the job's entry says so** — that the machine did not have what the file named and that Blender rendered with its own in its place"*, with *"Nothing new is built for it"*. The entry is this document's, so the observation and the record are this document's; the wording and the placement are Design's and `Desktop app and delivery`'s ([Section 20](#20-what-this-document-does-not-own)).

- **The mechanism is E13's, taken whole and not re-decided.** The render worker observes the line as the bytes pass its copy loop, records it in the attempt's record, and §7 step 4's fill-in carries it to the entry and into history. Never a mining of the sink afterwards (E10), never a second channel (E14), never a reading of state (R1) — the render's ending is unchanged by it in every case.
- **Both streams are watched for it, and no stream is asserted.** Which of the two carries the line **was never recorded**, and the spec says so in its own words — *"Blender prints in two places while it runs — its running commentary and its complaints — and which of the two carries this line was never recorded, so both have to be watched for it."* The worker already copies both streams (`Rented-server runtime` M3) and E13 already observes on whichever stream a version's route prints its own line, so watching both opens no read, no channel and no cost. **`Rendering` L7 is the ground and it is stronger than a convenience**: *"both streams are captured, always"*, because which stream one of Blender's own messages lands on is not fixed even without an ask. **Nothing here decides the stream and no clause below may be built on one.**
- **What is on record about the line, and it is what the match is built from.** A colour-space name or a view the active configuration does not hold is **thrown away and replaced, the render is not stopped, and lines are printed for it** — **three of them, in 4.5 to 5.2**: the scene view not found and a default set; the image colour space not found and a default to be used in its place; and a warning that the current value matches no entry in the list. Label: **observed in a test** (`What has to travel with a file`, *Dependencies 15*; the head note names this as one of its two exceptions to the routing rule). **The 4.5 difference is a window difference and not a stream one** — that version alone shows nothing on screen — so a render started with no window prints the lines in each of those four.
- **The line shapes are read per version by R2's rule and by no rule shared across the boundary**, the printed message format being one of the things that parts at 4.5 against the three newer versions; a line that matches no grammar yields nothing and is kept anyway, by R3's. **The exact printed text of the three is on record for none of 4.5 to 5.2**, which is what [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 27 carries.
- **So this clause stands unbuilt, and it stands unbuilt permanently rather than pending a round. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it.** No match exists, none can be written for 4.5 to 5.2, and the one 5.3 allows is parked (the next bullet), so the observation never fires and the entry never carries the telling — item 27 as corrected states it. **The clause is kept rather than struck** because everything else in it is right and would be right the moment a line shape were ever recorded; what must never happen is a line shape written from memory to make it fire.
- **5.3's lines are on record, and they do not fit the three above. Added 2026-11-02, the 5.3 integration's piece 6; no auditor has seen it.** From a file a little different, on the 5.3.0 Beta on the Mac alone, it printed `color_management | WARNING sequencer colorspace "sRGB" not found, will use default instead.`, `color_management | WARNING image colorspace "ACEScg" not found, will use default instead.` and `bpy.rna          | WARNING current value '-1' matches no enum in 'ColorManagedInputColorspaceSettings', '(null)', 'name'`, and the view was replaced with **no line printed**. Label: **observed in a test** (*Dependencies 15*). Which stream carries them is not recorded, and 5.4's text has not been read. **A match for 5.3 could now be written, and the build runner's ruling of 2026-11-02 keeps it unbuilt**: the text comes from a beta on a Mac and may still move. It is parked in Open Threads for after the release re-read, and until then a 5.3 entry says nothing, as the four before it do.
- **The entry states two facts and infers none.** That the machine did not hold what the file named, and that Blender rendered with its own in its place — the spec's own pair. **It is never an ending, never a failure and never a warning about the picture**: the render finished, E2's four endings are untouched, and nothing here says what the frames look like, the spec's own sources labelling that step **inferred**. Where no such line was observed the entry says nothing at all, and **absence is never evidence that no substitution happened** (item 27).
- **R6's closed list is not opened**, and R6 now names this line among its exclusions for the same reason it names the device name: this is not a reading the running-render reader produces.

**E14. Rows 68 and 69 are answered together deliberately**, because the device line is bytes on the same captured streams: one capture, one loop, one fill-in, no second channel.

---

## 9. The machine going down, and coming back

**Constraint:** Rows 70–75. Fact 1 at the promise level; `State, identity and recovery` §6's reconciliation with its four dispositions and two prohibitions; `Job files and outputs` §10's completeness machinery, cited and never re-derived.

**D1. The queue survives in order because the order is data.** The line is durable job records with sequence keys (Q2, Q4); reconciliation reads them and starts nothing whose durable state forbids it. Observed at the promise level: a real kernel panic, proved unclean by the next boot's filesystem recovery, left the held job held with its hold byte-identical, the running job at attempt 2 with `re-run once` journalled, and the line read back complete — and the container reproduced the shape by a different arrangement, on a provider where a clean restart and a crash are the same event.

**D2. A job expected to resume that neither starts nor gains an ending is one of exactly two named states, and both are told apart honestly.** A recycled or reimaged machine: the installation identity check reads absent or different, nothing on it is Smoozy's, and every machine-derived value against the row is disqualified — the job's fate is *no ending Smoozy ever learned* if its start had been learned, and, if it had not, an entry saying Smoozy never heard whether it started ([C9](#11-one-job-two-records-two-lifetimes)'s second way in), which is the spec's own answer for a machine that is gone. *(Corrected 2026-09-18, item C1, Creator-stated: this read* and no trace if it had not*, which assumed a start that was never learned was a start that never happened. From this side the two cannot be told apart once the machine is gone. No auditor has seen this.)* A machine whose state mount filled: where the supervisor is down, the machine reads as the supervisor-unavailable condition and the repair is `Server Setup`'s and `Desktop app and delivery`'s trigger (Q8); where it lived through the fill, it answers throughout and the ending is held pending under `Rented-server runtime` S14 until its write lands *(the second half added 2026-08-22, R10 — this named only the down case)*. The promise's own escalation clause was written for an unanswered Fact 1; Fact 1 is answered, and what remains is these two, neither silent.

**D3. Order is preserved across the interruption without a rule of its own**: the interrupted job's record still holds the lowest live key, so the re-run precedes the rest of the line by the same admission rule as always (Q4).

**D4. The re-run is the machine's own act, from durable state, and nothing waits on anybody.** §6 step 2: desired state *should run*, group gone, attempt count 1 → disposition **re-run once**, the count of 2 written durably before the new attempt starts, journalled. The attempt reads the immutable manifest — a re-run reads, never recomposes (M2) — and the remap material sitting in the asset area beside it; a re-run that cannot read the remap material its manifest names must not render, which is `Dependency Upload & Remap`'s constraint handed to `Rendering` and honoured here as a queue fact: such an attempt fails rather than rendering the artist's own recorded locations.

**D5. The interrupted render itself is gone and no version resumes inside a frame** — picking up is a fresh render that skips what is already on disk, which is the joined answer's own sentence and the whole reason the reckoning exists.

**D6. The re-run's frame set is handed over as an explicit list of frames, composed by the machine's reckoning from integers, and Blender's own skipping is never used.** The spec offered two forms; the constraints leave one. Blender's skip test is bare existence at one path — an empty file counts as done, and file output nodes are not reached at all — and the Creator's ruling that a zero-byte file never counts is a rule Blender's test cannot deliver, so walking the range on Blender's judgment cannot be taken. The scattered list is available in every version, taken in the order written, mixing frames and stretches — and its cost is closed by C3: a malformed list renders nothing and still reports success, so the list is composed from the reckoning's own integers and never from text anybody typed or parsed.

**D7. What the list contains is `Job files and outputs` §10's and is cited, not restated**: the frames not already there under C12–C13, plus the last frame asked for that is there, re-rendered anyway (C17–C18), in the previous attempt's asked order; where no previous attempt record can be read, the whole range (C20). The reckoning runs on the machine, as a step of the render worker before launch, needing no connection (C1) — which is what makes the automatic re-run possible at all.

**D8. Row 73 closes as unresolved, and the fallback is already written into the promise.** Fact 44 is not posable today: the power of a rented machine cannot be cut over the network and the register forbids putting it in a rental batch, and the remaining route — a guest machine killed at the hypervisor, which never touches the Creator's own Mac's power — is with the Creator, the blocker being what is installed rather than what exists ([Facts](Facts.md), Fact 44, corrected 2026-08-22). *(Reworded 2026-08-29: this read "Fact 44 is unposable: no machine this project can reach can have its power cut", which the register withdrew on 2026-08-22 — the clause asserted a permanent impossibility where the register names an install as the blocker. The status is unmoved: still not answered, still not scheduled.)* The nearest reachable case — a kernel-level reset, all four versions, one rented Linux box — left no frame's contents at all, including a frame reported saved seconds earlier, with the surviving directory entries differing between two runs of the same test.
