The spec's fallback stands: render the frames again rather than trust a file Smoozy cannot vouch for, and which frames that covers is [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md)'s. What a true power cut could add beyond the reset — a short-but-not-empty file that C15's limit counts as there — is named at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 3 with what breaks. Not a stop: the promise stands weaker, not unsupported.

**D9. A video job gets none of the skipping, and the switch is thrown before the render, from the recorded reading.** The video shape is a field of the reading (I19–I23), known at Pre-load Settings or at job start, carried on the entry either way (N9). Where any destination is video-shaped: the reckoning does not run and the attempt covers the whole range (C6, I22); the re-run replaces rather than adds, so nothing is cleared first; the second bar shows the whole range again (B7).

**D10. Nothing reads a video file's size, ever.** Its size does not move while the render runs on one record and grew stepwise on another — the two topics disagree, neither is chosen, and the joined answer's rule is that the size must not be read as a sign a render is getting on. §1.3 rule 5 forbids the reading anyway; this document adds the entry-level consequence: a video job's entry carries no on-disk progress figure of any kind, and no last-frame figure (N8).

**D11. A closed-off split file survives and the re-run still covers the whole range** — the artist's own splitting setting is [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md)'s condition, and nothing here reads the closed files as progress.

**D12. Re-run once, not forever, and the count survives the crash it counts.** §6's table: *should run*, group gone, attempt count 2 → **given up**, the disposition and the recorded outcome written together, journalled; the machine moves on by Q4. The count lives in the operation record and is advanced durably *before* the second attempt starts — which is why the very crash it counts cannot lose it, and why the observed panic came back at attempt 2 with `re-run once` already journalled. The bad case the rule exists for — a scene that takes the machine down every time it loads — costs two attempts and then a *given up* entry, a telling, and a machine that moves on.

**D13. The disk-full form cannot loop either, and for the blunt reason.** Where the state mount is full the count cannot advance — and nothing runs at all: a supervisor that cannot start starts nothing, and one that lived through the fill admits nothing, Q4's trigger being an outcome **written** and every record write refusing (`Rented-server runtime` S14; *widened 2026-08-22, R10 — this named only the cannot-start case*). The failure is total and loud rather than a loop on a paid machine (Q8, [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 1). The one harm the *given up* rule guards is not reachable by either route.

**D14. The supervisor finishes an outstanding act on the line from its own record before the admission point may start anything. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it.** At its start, and before Q4's admission point may start or drop a single record, the supervisor completes an outstanding clear from L23's record and an outstanding reorder or block move from L24's, in that order, and clears each record when its last unit is done. **This is what makes L23 and L24 worth writing at all**: without it the record changes nothing across a crash, which is the case that matters most — a supervisor killed between two removals comes back, finds the line half-cleared, and starts a job the user asked to have cleared. A record whose completion is itself refused leaves the record standing, the act outstanding and nothing started, which is Q8's disk-full answer applied here and not a second one.

---

## 10. The note, the figure, and what the read fills in

**Constraint:** Rows 76, 83, 87. The per-job note is [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#12-on-each-rented-server) §1.2's own store, owner `Queue & History`; Fact 40 answered it in full including the disk-full form, with a null ending for a job that has not ended rather than an invented one.

**N1. The per-job note is one durable record per job, in the package area, written by the supervisor, and it is the read surface an entry's lasting facts come from.** It holds: the **label**, whole and authoritative (Y8); the **times** — the moment the render started and the moment it ended, on the machine's clock, each written at the event that makes it true; the **ending**, with outcome and detail — and a **null ending for a job that has not ended**, never an invented one; the **attempt count and re-run marks** — interrupted and re-run, and from which frame the re-run proceeded, which is the first frame of the re-run attempt's list; **given up**, where it was; and the **last-frame figure** of N6. It is created in the same act as the job record (Q3), with the label and a null ending.

**N2. The note is written by the single writer in the same act as the record that makes each fact true, which is what keeps one fact from having two disagreeing homes.** The started time lands when the render operation's attempt starts; the ending and its time land when the recorded outcome is written; the re-run marks land with §6's dispositions. Every write is journalled, so a reader learns of it by cursor rather than by comparing two readings (E1 of §4).

**N3. The reason a render failed is the note's pointer into the attempt's captured record, not a diagnosis.** The note carries the ending's classification, the exit status verbatim (M22 — recorded, handed on, never a claim), and the reference to the attempt's capture record with its two collection outcomes; the words themselves stay in the sinks (E7). Nothing in the note is mined from a stream.

**What the note's ending carries to the desktop, and it is two things more than N3 names. Added 2026-09-27, outside any round, out of step 10c of Build.** N1 gives the ending *"outcome and detail"*, and E9, E13 and E13a have the desktop learn what the machine recorded at §7 step 4's fill-in, but no clause said that any of it travels on the note — and as built, the note-reading answer carried the outcome, the times, the exit status and the attempt and nothing else. So a failure's reason could not reach the desktop, and on step 10c's seventh passes every finished job read *not known* for what did the work on all three providers, though the machine had observed the line each time. **So the note's ending carries, and the answer that reads the note sends, the reason the machine recorded with the outcome** (`Rendering` K14's, in the words the machine wrote at the ending) **and the device line as the worker observed it at its own loop** (E13), verbatim. N3's rule binds both: the reason is the machine's own record of why the ending was classified as it was, and the line is an observation of bytes in passing (E10); neither is mined from a stream, and the words themselves still stay in the sinks. The desktop keeps the reason and shows it with the failure. **And a third, added 2026-11-04, the launch film's first job: whether the attempt ended before any render began**, `Rendering` K14's *before the render instruction*, which the machine knows and the outcome word does not say. It is sent only where it did, and absent reads as *a render began*, so a note from an older machine program keeps the words it always had. The desktop keeps it on the record at version 60 (`State, identity and recovery`'s one forward-only list), and `Design/Queue & History`'s *Failed before rendering.* rests on it. *The record's other signs were read and refused*: no exit status and two streams never established misses a job Blender was launched for and stopped in its launch material, and `render_start_learned` reads 1 on the film's own job. It states *what did the work* from the line only on an ending whose recorded outcome is *finished* (E12), and never from the ask (E12a).

**N4. The times obey D5 entire.** Both moments are the machine's own clock; the duration an entry shows is computed from those two readings and never across clocks; the desktop's clock is presentation only. The clock's stability over a render's length carries Fact 92's qualifier — nothing was disciplining the one rented clock this project has watched, and a container has no clock of its own — named at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 10, which also names the second reading that would make a step visible and says why it is not taken here.

**N5. Fact 40's disk-full half is what makes the note trustworthy exactly when it matters, and its limit is stated.** With the filesystem at a genuine zero every read answered normally and truthfully, before the refused writes and after them, and no read reported success from nothing — the case the sources file called the most likely to fail when a job needs it. The limit: while the state mount is full, the *writes* refuse, so an ending that occurs then is an ending the note learns only after space returns — at the supervisor's restart, or at `Rented-server runtime` S14's pending-write landing where the supervisor lived through the fill (Q8; *widened 2026-08-22, R10*). The note never holds a guessed ending in the meantime — the refused write leaves the null ending standing, which is the truthful state.

**N6. The last-frame figure is computed at the job's ending, on the machine, by the ending's own step, and written into the note.** For every ending short of a clean finish, the ending's step takes the same one walk and completeness statement as Row 54's reading (H7 — C7, C27) and records **the last frame, in the job's own asked order as the manifest records it, that counts as already there** under C12–C13 — across attempts, and whichever attempt asked for it, so a re-run's own frame list never narrows the figure to that list's complement. C17's consequence is kept explicit: the last frame asked for on the previous attempt is re-rendered and is short or absent while it is, so it may drop out of the figure — the rule working rather than a loss. The figure is stated in frames, never renderlets — whether a whole job's renderlets can be counted is not established and this figure must not lean on it, per the spec's own words. It is what was true when the job ended; *carry on from where this stopped* reads the machine again before using it, which is `Rendering`'s Row 20, owed, shown against this figure as its fallback where the machine cannot be read. **And what the figure is, is a boundary the action applies C17 to rather than a frame to start after — stated here 2026-09-02, out of the disk-fill scenario replay's finding N-c.** `Rendering` A4, corrected the same day, opens the carried-on range **at** the last frame that counts as already there, exactly as C17 re-renders it on the machine's own attempt, because that frame is the one Smoozy cannot vouch for under C15's limit. **The two arithmetics are one and must not diverge**: a page opened after this figure would leave the machine re-rendering a frame the user's own action had already written off. Nothing about the figure itself moves — it is computed and stated the same way — and the reading it feeds is that document's.

**N7. Where the ending's own step never ran, there is no figure, and the entry says so.** A machine that died mid-render and never came back leaves a job with no ending Smoozy ever learned — no figure, no times past the start, and the actions that read the figure not offered, with the reason stated. An interrupted-then-re-run job's figure is the *final* ending's; intermediate interruptions leave their marks (N1) and no figure of their own.

**N8. C15's limit and the video case are carried on the figure, not hidden under it.** A file short but not empty still counts, so a machine that went down can leave a figure vouching for a frame with a hole in it — the two signals where that bites are the count coming up short and what the machine printed, and nothing further is claimed. **Which frame that can be is narrower than this clause read, and it is never the boundary frame — corrected 2026-09-02, out of the same finding N6 and [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 3 already carry.** The figure's own frame is re-rendered whatever it looks like: `Job files and outputs` C17 re-renders the last frame asked for on the machine's own attempt, and `Rendering` A4 opens a carry-on's range **at** that frame rather than after it (N6). **So the frame most likely to be short is the one frame neither route skips**, and what C15's limit can leave uncorrected is a short frame *behind* the boundary — one written whole-looking earlier in the run — which is a narrower case and the one both signals are for. This clause read as though the boundary frame were the case, which was the sharper-sounding half and the one already covered. Backing the figure off by a fixed number was raised and rejected by the Creator's recorded ruling — the observed loss reached every frame in the run, so no fixed number covers it — and is not resurrected here. A video job has no figure at all (D10), and its entry says the interruption cost is the whole range.

**N9. What the render's file read produces reaches the entry by the two standing routes and no third one.** The recorded reading — scene name, picture size, the six statements, the folders, the collision record, the writes-nothing mark, the video shape, the main output, a destination recorded as not mappable with its reason — is composed on the machine, journalled as an event when written, and sits in the job record directory for §7 step 4's fill-in (H8, H9 of `Job files and outputs` §9). The entry learns it on the next connection however long that takes — or, where a connected period is standing, from the same journalled event on `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d's recurring read-forward; *nothing polls and nothing is pushed* is that clause's and §8.4's sense. It is a second thing the machine records against a job beyond the note, and it is *that document's* store, not a new one of this document's — the spec's two-store shape lands in the existing inventory. **One more field rides it from 2026-10-04: the job output folder's path and its case, where the render worker chose them** (`Job files and outputs` [6.6](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P21) — *and, from later that day, whether the chosen fallback folder was passed over (P23)*.

**N9a. Where a re-run found the file changed, the entry says so, and says what became of the frames that were already finished — whether they were rendered again or reused. Added 2026-09-02, answering Row 516, against the ruling of the same day in [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md), which settles the telling and names this document as its owner, and against `Job files and outputs` R14 and its [Section 14](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#14-unresolved) item 9, which route it here and compose none of it.**

**Corrected 2026-09-04, out of a code round, and the correction entered at the spec and came down.** This clause said the entry *"says that frames finished under the earlier arrangement were rendered again rather than reused"*, flat, because the spec said it flat. **That holds only where the arrangement moved**, and a file edited in a way that moves no destination — a material, a light, the camera — yields the same arrangement, so the earlier attempt's frames are found where the check looks and are reused. **The entry was therefore built to tell the user something that had not happened**, on the common case. `Job files and outputs` R14 carries the correction's ground and C12a is the fact that separates the two.

- **Both facts are read, and neither is computed here.** `Job files and outputs` [R13](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#44-the-file-identity-and-when-a-recorded-reading-goes-stale) writes **a statement that the file changed** into that attempt's record, beside the fresh reading and the fresh map, and keeps the previous attempt's map beside it; **[C12a](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover) writes beside it the statement of whether that attempt kept any frame an earlier one had written**, derived from the frame list the reckoning composed against the inventory and from nothing else. [M9](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#34-the-attempt-records-beside-it) is the durable half — attempt records are on the machine before the render begins, each map named by the attempt that used it. Its [10.2 C7](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#102-the-reading-the-reckoning-is-made-against) makes the already-there test read **the map in force for the attempt being started** and no other, which is what makes both outcomes a recorded consequence of that test rather than an inference. **The entry states the two records and derives nothing**, and in particular it never reads the maps itself to work out whether the arrangement moved.
- **It reaches the entry by N9's route and no new one.** The statement sits in the attempt's record in the job record directory, journalled as an event when written, and the entry learns it at §7 step 4's fill-in on the next connection however long that takes — or from the same journalled event where a connected period is standing. This is E13's shape for the device line applied to a second attempt-record fact, not a second route. **That is what makes it survive nobody being connected**, which D4 requires: the re-run is the machine's own act from durable state and nothing waits on anybody. Once filled in it rides the history record and survives the machine, like the words of E9; **where the machine is gone before any connection carried it the entry never learns it and says nothing**, which is N13's rule — absent is drawn as absent and never as a claim that the file did not change.
- **It is said, never asked.** Nothing here is a control, a prompt, or a decision put to the user: no hold is placed, §3's held-job machinery is not reached, and no attempt waits on an answer. The re-run follows an interruption with the user possibly away, so a question would either hold a machine billing by the hour or be answered by a default, which is silence with a prompt in front of it. The user's own routes — submitting again and *carrying on from where this stopped* — are untouched and are what a user who wants to step in already has.
- **It is per attempt and not per job.** A job may have more than one attempt with more than one map, each named by the attempt that made it (R13), and the entry carries the statement against each attempt whose record holds it rather than collapsing them into one claim — the same per-attempt shape E9's fetch already has.
- **No count of frames is stated either way, and that is a bound rather than an omission.** C7's walk is against the map in force for the attempt being started alone, so nothing counts what stood finished under the earlier arrangement; a number would want a second walk of the earlier attempt's map, which no clause anywhere takes and which this document does not add. **C12a is a plain yes or no for the same reason** — it says that a frame an earlier attempt wrote was kept, never how many. The entry states the fact and the cost in words. **The words are Design's**, as every other entry wording here is, and there are now two of them to word rather than one.

**N10. The merge rule: Smoozy's own record wins where it carries a fact, and the reading fills what it lacks.** A job submitted again from a history entry carries the old job's scene name as Smoozy's own record, with no read — that name is never overwritten by a later reading's; the picture size is not carried and fills in from the read, exactly as the spec splits them. Everything only a read can know — the six statements, folders, collisions, the main output — lands from the reading; from then on *show outputs* opens the main output, or the job's own folder where the walk declared none (B4–B8 of that document).

**N10a. Each answer the record carries says whether it was the user's own or resolved for them, and the Blender version is the one that turns on it. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Render Settings](Render%20Settings.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 23.** The local job record and the history entry keep, beside each such answer, **whether the user made it as an override or whether Smoozy's automatic match resolved it**. **The version is the case that needs it**: `Render Settings` Y15 loads a page from a history entry by taking the entry's answers as store entries, **and takes the version pick among them only where the entry records it as an override**; otherwise the automatic match runs afresh for the machine in hand. **Without the distinction there is no honest option**: keep every pick and a version resolved for a machine that no longer exists rides into the store, so the automatic match never runs and the page offers a build the new machine may not have — worst case a same-numbered build at another path; or drop every pick and the user's own deliberate answer is thrown away with it. **The distinction is a field of the record and never a reading**: it is written at submission from which side supplied the value, and no later reading changes it. N10's merge rule is unaffected — this says how an answer was arrived at, not who wins where two exist.

**N11. The entry states what was known when nothing was read, rather than a blank or a guess.** No scene name — submitted rendering whichever scene the file was saved showing; a percentage only where the user typed one; no pixel size at all. Those are the spec's own sentences; what this document adds is their ground: they are fields of the local job record written at submission, so the entry reads them with no machine and no read.

**And one field this list lacked: the `.blend` file's path. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R4.** The local job record carries, written at submission, **the root `.blend` file's machine path as the submission named it** — the same copy-at-submission shape as N12's fields, and for the same reason: the manifest holds the path and the manifest dies with the machine, so without the copy an entry whose machine is gone cannot name the file the job rendered, and finding that file again on a new machine is the user's memory rather than Smoozy's record. It is Smoozy's own record — no reading fills it or overwrites it (N10) — and it is absent on a record written before this rule, drawn as absent (N13).

**N22. The submitted-with-no-scene-named mark survives the read that supplies the name, because the mark and the name are two fields and the read fills only one. Added 2026-08-25, per Row 494.**

- **The field.** The local job record carries, written at submission with the provisional record (§7 step 2's write, which N16's moment already joins), **the mark that the submission named no scene** — present exactly where the job was submitted with no scene in hand (`Render Settings` F5's no-scene composition, Y10). It is a frozen note about the submission's moment, N15's own shape and the fifth on that set's terms; one presence field, holding no scene name of its own.
- **N10's merge rule is untouched, and it is what made the field necessary.** The record lacks a scene name, so N9's recorded reading fills the name in when the job starts — that filling is correct and stands. But after it, nothing on the record told a scene the user chose from a scene the read supplied, so the mark could not be drawn, could not be reworded to name the scene, and could not stay into history. The mark is Smoozy's own record: no reading fills it, none overwrites it, and no later read of any kind removes it.
- **The two draws are composed at the draw from the two fields, stored never** (N20's shape). Where the record's scene-name field stands filled, the mark draws naming that scene — the design's *submitted with no scene named; Blender rendered the scene the file was saved showing*, named; where no read has landed, it draws as N11's own sentence. Wording Design's.
- **It stays into history on N15's terms** — it rides the record history is (C10, C11), survives the machine's destruction and the server row's removal, and goes only with the entry (X1) — and it is **absent on a record written before this rule, drawn as no mark** (N13's rule, N15's).
- **An adopted no-scene job takes the mark from the manifest carrying no scene name**, which is the machine's own record of the same submission fact — `Rendering` L15's *a job submitted without the file ever having been read carries no scene name*, S6's unnamed branch — carried in by step 4's fill-in at A7, the one route by which the fact is not already on the desktop (L20's shape). The manifest is immutable, so the fact survives on the machine however late the adoption comes.

**N12. Two more fields of that record, and the entry cannot be drawn without them. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 320.** The local job record carries, written at submission beside the fields N11 names, **the server's label** and **the server's badge hue**, both **as they stood at submission**.

- **They are copies and are never read back from the server row.** [Servers](Servers.md#61-one-durable-store-and-one-row-per-server) 6.1 D3 gives the row a colour field whose *Ends at* column reads **the removal**, and D5 states outright that *"History records are keyed by the job identity and never by the server row, which is why a removal leaves them exactly where they are."* **So an entry that read the row would have nothing to read the moment the server was removed** — which is the exact case both the spec and the Design sentence single out.
- **A copy is what the spec asks for and not a convenience.** [Queue & History](../Specs/Queue%20&%20History.md): *"**An entry keeps the server's name as it stood at submission**, and removing that server does not take it. The name is part of the setup the entry carries."* **A rename therefore does not reach history**, which follows from *as it stood at submission* and needs no rule of its own — and it agrees with `Servers`' own statement that a rename changes no connection detail and ends no period.
- **The hue is carried for the same reason the name is, and the Creator's own sentence is why it is not cosmetic.** *"A job in history is placed by its colour before its name is read"* (**Creator-stated**, 2026-08-10). A history row with no colour is a row the user cannot scan, so the value has to survive the removal exactly as the name does.
- **They are the entry's alone, and the guarantee is about composition, not the absence of a field. Narrowed 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R4** — this read *"the entry holds a label and a hue and no server identity of any kind beyond the one D5 already keys on"*, which contradicted `Servers` [W32](Servers.md#117-the-edit-warning-and-where-its-knowledge-comes-from-row-317)'s *"each names the server it was submitted to"* and misread D5, which keys history records by the **job** identity. **What the record carries**: the local server identifier the job was submitted under, written at submission with the provisional record (`State, identity and recovery` §7 step 2) — it is what step 2's resolution asks the machine about and what `Servers`' edit warning counts by (W31). **What is guaranteed**: nothing composes a live server list from entries, nothing matches a row to a server by a label or a hue, and **a removed server is never reconstructed out of an entry**. **Once the row is gone the identifier dangles, and a dangling identifier reads as nothing**: nothing is reconstructed from it — N17's draw-time lookup finds no row and reads that as the whole answer *(reworded 2026-08-22 with N17; this said "it is never resolved against the store", whose sense was never a bar on the lookup but on making anything of one that finds nothing)* — and the entry's server is drawn from the copied label and hue alone, which is what the copies are for.
- **N10's merge rule is untouched.** Both are Smoozy's own record and no reading fills either, so a later read never overwrites them; a job submitted again from a history entry takes the **current** server's label and hue, because it is a new submission on whichever server the user picked.

**N16. The submitted moment, and it is the one figure on the entry taken from the desktop's clock. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 381.** N1's note holds the started and ended moments on the machine's clock; **submission happens on the desktop and the machine was never told when.**

- **The local job record carries the submitted moment, on the desktop's clock, written when the provisional record is written and before the submission is sent** (`State, identity and recovery` §7 step 2, whose write this joins).
- **D5 permits it in its own words** — *"the desktop's clock is used for presentation and for nothing a promise rests on"* — **and nothing rests on it**: no ordering, no timeout, no reconciliation and no decision anywhere reads it. It is drawn and nothing else.
- **It is never subtracted against anything.** N4's *how long it rendered* stays the note's two machine-clock readings and only those. **The four times are four moments; exactly one duration exists on the entry and it is machine-clock-only.** A gap between *submitted* and *started* is not computed, and where the two clocks disagree the entry shows two moments that do not line up rather than a number that is wrong.
- **It is absent on a record written before this rule**, and absent draws as absent — the mockup's own no-ending line already shows the shape, *Ending unknown — submitted 4 Aug 21:40*.

**N15. Two more fields, both frozen notes about a moment that has passed. Added 2026-08-21, per Rows 379 and 380.** [Design](../Design/Queue%20&%20History.md) fixes that a mark *"stays on the entry into history"*, and two of its four marks had no field anywhere to live in.

- ***Dependencies were not checked before submission*** **is one state of the mark, not the mark itself — widened 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R8.** The local job record carries, written at submission, **the record state the missing-assets evaluation found at that moment**: `Rendering` [W2](Rendering.md#5-the-missing-assets-warning)'s three — *no standing at all*; *a standing carrying the replaced-since-check or disturbed mark*; *a standing keyed to a Blender version other than the one the submission names* — or that a complete standing satisfying its W1 stood, under which no mark is drawn. One field wide enough for the state, never a boolean: a single bit worded as the first state would assert the one thing W2 forbids — *"the warning is never allowed to say nobody checked where one stands"* — against exactly the job whose file was checked and then replaced, and the frozen mark is bound by the same bar. The wording of each state is Design's. **Nothing new is computed**: `Rendering` [W1](Rendering.md#5-the-missing-assets-warning) already evaluates exactly this *"at the moment of submitting, from the check standing alone, with no read of the file and no read of the machine"*, and this is that evaluation's result written down instead of only acted on.
- **It has to be a copy and cannot be the standing itself.** `Dependency Upload & Remap` V10's check standing is keyed to *(root file's machine path, machine under its installation identity)* and is **disqualified with everything else machine-keyed** when the machine is recycled. **The standing is a live record about a file on a machine; the mark is a frozen note about a job, kept for ever.** Nothing re-checks it and no later check changes it.
- ***It carries on from an earlier job.*** The local job record carries, written at submission by the action that made the job, **the earlier job's identity and its label as it stood** — the same copy-at-submission shape as [N12](#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in)'s, and for the same reason.
- **The label is copied because the link can outlive its target.** Deletion is unconditional and leaves a tombstone (X1 to X5), so **the earlier entry can be gone while the mark still names it.** With the label copied, the mark reads whole and **the way to that entry is simply not offered**; with an identity alone it would be a name Smoozy could not read.
- **Both are Smoozy's own record, so N10's merge rule leaves them alone** and no reading fills or overwrites either. **Both are absent on a record written before this rule**, and absent draws as no mark at all, which is the design's own *shown only when it is true*.
- **Section 17's persistence list is what these join.** The disturbed-file and simplify marks were already named there as persisting into history unchanged; these two now persist on the same terms, and the four marks are one set. **A fifth joined 2026-08-25 — N22's no-scene mark, on the same terms — and the five are one set.**

**N14. One further field, and it is the only place the file's own values ever reach an entry. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 319.** The local job record carries, **per setting the payload carries an override for ~~and no others~~**, the **file's value for that setting as the reading in force at submission gave it**. `Render Settings` [Y1a](Render%20Settings.md#91-what-the-payload-is) composes it, because that page is the only place holding both objects at once and both are gone soon after. **Corrected 2026-10-07 with Y1a (the Creator's second notes on the test copy, piece 16): the frame range, the sample count, the percentage and the engine are carried whether or not the payload overrides them**, so the entry draws the file's own value, in the file's face, on a setting left alone, where a reading stood. Until then those rows read *not known* on a job submitted with the file read.

- **It is not in the payload and not in the manifest**, so `Render Settings` Y1's prohibition on a file value re-sent as an override is untouched: this never reaches a render, a machine, or a launch.
- **N10's merge rule does not touch it.** No reading fills it and no reading overwrites it — the value is *what the file said when this job was submitted*, and a reading taken afterwards is a different fact about a different moment.
- **Absent is a value here too**, on N13's rule: a job submitted with no reading standing carries no companion, and the entry draws the file's side as absent rather than showing a value from anywhere else.
- **A job submitted again from a history entry composes a fresh one** from the reading in force at that submission, exactly as it composes a fresh payload.

**N13. Where the record predates this rule, the entry says nothing rather than guessing.** Added 2026-08-21. There is no route from an old entry to a hue, and reading the server row for one would be exactly the fault N12 exists to prevent. **A missing label or hue is drawn as absent** and never as a default colour, which would place a job by a colour that was never its server's.

**N17. What each of the entry's machine-reaching controls reads to decide whether it is offered, composed at the draw and cached never. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R5.** The spec's rule is the constraint: an entry outliving its machine keeps its setup and loses its reach, and Smoozy says so plainly instead of offering an action that cannot work. **The fact is supplied by the entry's own copied server identifier (N12), looked up against the server store at the moment the entry is drawn** — a dangling identifier reads as nothing (N12), so a destroyed-and-removed machine's entry resolves to no row — and by nothing cached: the offering is re-composed at every draw, and no connection is opened and no machine is read to decide it.

- **Where the lookup finds no standing row**, the reach-back controls — *show outputs*, the whole-job download, and *carry on from where this stopped* — are not offered, and the entry states that the machine is gone, wording Design's. Adding the same physical machine again changes nothing here: the new row has a new identifier, the entry's copied one still dangles, and N12's *a removed server is never reconstructed out of an entry* holds at the draw too.
- **Where a row stands**, each control's own clauses already answer the disconnected and could-not-check cases — carry-on opening on the kept figure and saying it could not check (`Rendering` A9), the download's and *show outputs*' own refusals — and this clause adds no second rule over them: what it fixes is the no-row case, and that the offer is a read at the draw rather than a judgment cached from any earlier moment.
- **Two acts are not reach-backs and are not withheld by any of this**: *submit again*, which opens the page from the kept setup with no machine in it and acquires one by the user's own pointing (`Rendering` A1, A1a) — the spec's own exception, named there as the common case; and deletion, whose local half is unconditional and whose machine half is already a recorded debt (X1, X4). **A third joins them at N18.**

**N18. The entry's two shortcuts to the files, and only one of them reaches a machine. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 433; second and fourth bullets corrected 2026-08-29 at the stage close, per Row 509 — the attribution added 2026-08-29 at the re-gate, the header having explained the two corrections by [Download](Download.md#11-where-downloads-land-row-204)'s clause widths alone and named no row, where every other dated clause here names its own; `Download` [H11](Download.md#11-where-downloads-land-row-204) and its §15 items 24 and 25 answer the same row from the other side and close on these two bullets. Each bullet says in place what changed — both were this document quoting [Download](Download.md#11-where-downloads-land-row-204)'s clauses at the width they carried before H7 was narrowed and H7a's second empty-set reading was added. No answer here moves and nothing below is re-decided.** They sit together on the entry and are composed from different records, and the asymmetry is the answer rather than a defect of it.

- **The server-side shortcut is *show outputs*.** What it opens is N10's — the recorded reading's main output, or the job's own folder where the walk declared none — over the manifest's recorded path verbatim (Y8). Whether it is offered is N17's: it is a reach-back, withheld with the whole-job download and carry-on where the entry's copied server identifier resolves to no standing row.
- **The computer-side shortcut is *show a job's downloaded files*, and it is handed a set of landing places, never a folder.** The set is [Download](Download.md#11-where-downloads-land-row-204) H5 to H8's, consumed whole and not re-decided here: the distinct folders drawn from the arrangement's *fetched* entries and from the manual request records enumerating under that job's output folder, in the order each was first written to, **composed when the control is used and never cached**, **one place and it opens, and where the set holds more than one the control opens every place in it and picks none**, a place that no longer exists kept in the set and stated as one Smoozy cannot open. **Corrected 2026-08-29 at the stage close.** This bullet read *"every place offered and none picked"*, which is the wording [Download](Download.md#11-where-downloads-land-row-204) H7 carried until that day; H7 was narrowed there to the Creator's own act — `Preparation/Specs/Queue & History.md`'s *"where there are two it opens them both rather than picking"* — and the wider wording admits a chooser the spec does not grant. **What is left to Design is only how several openings are presented and announced**, on H7's own unchanged bound: no place is hidden. `Download` H9 assigns the control to this entry and the rule behind it to that document.
- **It is not a reach-back, N17 never withholds it, and no clause of this document may.** Both records behind the set — the arrangement record with its per-file reckoning, and the manual request records — are **user-held** in the local record (`Download` §12's lifetimes, R1, M1), and neither is a copy of a machine state the machine still holds, so §3 D4 is untouched. So the set composes with nothing connected, on an entry whose server row is gone, and **whatever the job's ending was** — the spec's own three conditions on this control, kept by construction rather than by a rule of its own.
- **Where the set is empty, which of two readings the entry states is [Download](Download.md#11-where-downloads-land-row-204) H7a's, and both are carried here.** **Widened 2026-08-29 at the stage close**, this bullet having carried only the first of the two — so a builder reading this document's entry clauses alone was told an empty set means nothing of the job came home, which is false of the second case and is the case the spec Creator-states a different sentence for. On H7a's own test, consumed whole and not re-derived here: **where the set is empty and no manual entry under the job's output folder published**, the entry states that **nothing of the job came home**, rather than offering a control that does nothing — the spec's own sentence; **where the set is empty and manual entries under the job's output folder did publish**, the entry states instead that **what came home was picked out file by file and went wherever the user sent each of them**, and **those destinations are not offered** — `Download` H5a's component-count test keeps them out of the set, a folder holding three frames of a job not being the job's folder. Either reading is the same shape as N17's stating of a machine that is gone, and neither costs a reading or a new record: both are the composition's own result. Wording is Design's.
- **Deleting the entry takes both shortcuts and neither set of files.** X1 removes the local record and everything riding it, the arrangement record among them (`Download` R3), so nothing remains to compose a set for; X2's erase reaches no job output folder; and nothing on the user's computer is touched by any of it. That is the spec's own *what is untouched is named as well* — the frames on the machine, and whatever already came home.

**N19. Which Blender the entry keeps is a pair, not a version, and which copy of it `Server Setup` reads is the machine's. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 448.** The promise says the entry records *the version*; **a version does not name an installation, and an installation is what `Server Setup` removes** — Fact 45 planted five builds five ways on one box, one of them answering 3.0.1, and two builds of one version answer alike.

- **The identification rule is `Server Setup` [B13](Server%20Setup.md#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s and is cited, not re-decided**: a Blender on the machine is identified by its **executable's path**, and the submission payload's version-pick entry carries **the picked build's path and its answered version** together (`Render Settings` [Y1](Render%20Settings.md#91-what-the-payload-is), always present). This document keeps the pair wherever it keeps the version.
- **Two copies, both written at submission and neither re-derived.** On the machine, in the manifest — `Job files and outputs` M1's field list and M1a's form — published with the job's material before the job record exists (Q3). On the desktop, in the local job record as part of the kept setup, **Smoozy's own record**: N10's merge rule leaves it alone, no reading fills it and no reading overwrites it, and N10a's field says whether the version was the user's own override or the automatic match's answer.
- **`Server Setup`'s removal warning reads the machine's copy, and this document supplies which records it is read off.** The live set is the machine's job records with state *waiting*, *held* or *rendering* (Q2, [17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)), and each live job's recorded build path is in that job's manifest, in the job record directory its queue record references (Q2's fourth field). **Never the desktop's copy:** a live job the local record does not name has none (Q6, A2), and Row 96's warning names every live job. The predicate over those paths and the warning's words are `Server Setup` [B14](Server%20Setup.md#55-taking-a-blender-off)'s.
- **What survives the machine is the answered version, and the path is kept without meaning.** The version keeps meaning against a different machine's installations — `Render Settings` B2 matches on the release line and Y15 loads the entry's pick only where N10a records it as the user's override. **The path does not, and nothing matches it against another machine's list**: it is the record of which build this job ran with, machine-scoped exactly as N12's server identifier is, and on an entry whose machine is gone it reads as nothing — nothing resolves it, nothing composes a list from it, and no installation is reconstructed out of an entry.
- **Nothing reads a machine to keep the pair true.** A build removed, moved or renamed after submission changes no entry: the pair states what was picked at that submission, not what stands on the machine now (§1.3 rule 5). Whether the search can find that path again is `Server Setup` B10–B12's reading with its own limits; the entry's record stands either way ([Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 21). What the entry **shows** of the pair is Design's, and no clause here requires the path on a screen.

**N20. The carry-on mark's two draws, the way it offers, and the direction it does not hold. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 449.** [N15](#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in) already fixes the field — the earlier job's identity and its label as it stood, written at submission by the action that made the job, Smoozy's own record, a copy because the link can outlive its target, absent drawing as no mark. What no clause said is which record each list's row draws it from, what decides the way to the earlier entry, and what the one-to-many direction costs.

- **One field serves both lists, because the queue row and the history row are two presentations of one job** (C10). The mark is on the local job record, which stands from submission (`State, identity and recovery` §7 step 2), so the queue row draws it by the same join L20 uses for a group heading: the local record's copy, joined to the period-held reading by job identity, composed at the draw. **A1's queue reading carries no carry-on field and the machine holds none** — the mark is Smoozy's own note of Smoozy's own act, never a desired state (D1, D2), never in Q2's closed set, and nothing about it reaches a machine.
- **The way to the earlier entry is a lookup at the draw, on the copied identity, against the local record alone** — N17's shape, and for N17's reason: no connection is opened, no machine is read, and nothing is cached from an earlier draw. **A standing record means the way is offered; a tombstoned identity and an identity no record names read alike as not offered** (X1, X5), and the copied label is what leaves the mark whole either way (N15). A deleted entry is never reachable through a mark, which is Row 82's promise holding at one more surface.
- **The other direction is held nowhere and needs nothing.** The earlier job's record is not written to when it is carried on from — no back-reference, no count, no list of the jobs that name it — so *the earlier entry is not changed by being carried on from* is kept by there being no write at all, and *can be carried on from more than once* costs no store: each new job carries its own one mark and several marks may name one identity. Deleting the earlier entry therefore takes nothing of theirs (X1 reaches its own record alone), and nothing anywhere counts them.
- **A job carrying the mark is an ordinary job below Section 4**: the mark plays no part in the line, in admission, in any ending or in any figure. The figure the carry-on action started from is N6's and is not this mark (Row 83).

**N21. The disturbed-file mark is a copy on the job's own local record, written with the telling, and it is not composed from the record it is copied from. Added 2026-08-23, per Row 450.** **Constraint:** the reading is `File Browser` [W9](File%20Browser.md#84-the-telling-row-196)'s and its source is that act's §9.4 outcome record, which is **user-held** — it ends when the user removes the record of that action (`State, identity and recovery` §11.1) — while the entry's mark must stay into history unconditionally. So the mark is a copy and never a composition over that record, which is N15's own distinction: the source is a live record about an act, the mark is a frozen note about a job.

- **The field.** The local job record carries, **per disturbance**, the disturbed member as the record spells it — the path the containment test matched, `File Browser` W5 — the act intended, and the composed reading: *disturbed*, or *may have been disturbed* where the act's outcome was never learned (W9, taken whole and not re-derived here). **Entries are appended and never replaced**: one job's files may be touched more than once and by more than one act, and the spec's *says which file it was* is per disturbance. Wording is Design's.
- **It is written with the telling, so no disturbance can stand with no mark.** The copy lands **in the same durable local write as `File Browser` W8's telling** — both are records in the local record and §9.3 makes the pair one unit — and W8's refusal, the act not attempted where the telling could not be written, therefore covers the mark by the same write. The reading is completed once, when the act's per-job outcome is learned, and **the mark is removed where none of that job's own units took effect**, which is W9's rule kept from this side. ~~**W8's write does not name this field today, and that line is owed there rather than written here**: routed to `File Browser` §8.4, that document unedited, and [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 22 names what a refusal breaks.~~ **W8 names it, 2026-10-02** ([Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 22, closed).
- **What it survives.** It is Smoozy's own record, so no reading fills or overwrites it (N10) and nothing machine-derived is disqualified with a recycled machine (`State, identity and recovery` §2.3). It sits on the record history is (C10, C11), so it stays on the entry into history, outlives the machine's destruction and the server row's removal, and goes only when the entry is deleted (X1). **It is not `Dependency Upload & Remap` V13's check standing and must not be confused with it**: that standing is keyed to a file on a machine and is subject-held (K10); this is a note about a job, kept for as long as the entry.
- **It changes nothing about the job.** No queue state — Q2's closed set does not move — no desired state (D1, D2), nothing written to the machine, and the admission point never reads it. A job marked this way runs, and where it fails the entry already says the likeliest reason.
- **A job the local record does not name carries no mark and needs none.** `File Browser` W5's per-job set is composed from records the desktop holds, so an untracked job is named by no warning and there is nothing to mark; adoption enters a record from that moment (A6), and disturbances after it are ordinary.

**N23. A job held for the driver and released after a swap that missed the bar carries a mark saying so, a copy on its own local record written before the release is sent. Added 2026-10-11, the Creator's ruling that a job needing the newer driver is held back and the user is told (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's rulings on OptiX and the driver, 2026-10-11.md`, its last section). No auditor has seen this text.** `Server Setup` R1c releases such a job however its swap's run ended, and where the driver did not reach the bar the job then fails as `Rendering` §9 item 3 says. The mark is what keeps that failure from being unexplained, and it follows N21's shape.

- **The field.** The local job record carries the driver's version and the job's Blender version as they stood when the release was decided. Wording is Design's, following what that Blender's row stops (`Rendering` L23a, added 2026-11-02).
- **Written by the desktop before it sends that job's release**, in one durable local write. Where the write is refused the release is not sent and the job stays held, so no job is released without its mark.
- **What it survives** is N21's: Smoozy's own record, so no reading fills or overwrites it; it stays on the entry into history and goes only when the entry is deleted (X1).
- **It changes nothing about the job.** No queue state, nothing written to the machine, and the admission point never reads it.
- **Only a job that was held carries it.** A job sent once `Servers` H11b's statement stands was never held, and the server's card and its Blender table had already said so (`Server Setup` B12b).
