## 11. One job, two records, two lifetimes

**Constraint:** Rows 77, 78, 81. D4's three exceptions and no fourth; §7 step 3's cursor; T18's boundary; T22's durable in-app records.

**C1. The catch-up notice is composed from exactly the events §7 step 3 applies, and nothing else ever composes one.** On a connection, after the installation identity confirms, the desktop reads forward from its durable event cursor and applies each event idempotently; everything applied in that pass is the catch-up account (T18) and posts no notification. The notice names what those events say about jobs this desktop tracks on that machine: finished and when, failed, interrupted and re-run from a given frame, given up, a held job dropped and why (H11), **a waiting job dropped for a Blender removed under it, named with the version it was submitted with (L22) — added 2026-08-23, per Row 447** — a job that simply rendered to the end named like any other, because it is the ordinary case. *Design presents the endings as a count in the Queue heading and names only the dropped jobs (H11, L22); the record and its composition are unchanged. Added 2026-10-12, Creator-stated, the Creator's ninth notes (2026-10-11), note 3; no auditor has seen it.*

**C1a. The account is composed from the applied events, not from the connection, so it is composed the same way whether or not a period broke. Ruled by the architecture runner 2026-09-02 under the Creator's delegation of the same day, as a change to what the user gets; written here and nowhere else.** C1 keys composition on *a connection*. Nothing else in this document composes an account, and inside a standing connected period events are applied by the read-forward that `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d repeats from the same cursor (P3, N9) — **the same cursor, the same idempotent application, the same events, and no account at all**. So the user who leaves Smoozy open overnight came down to nothing to read, while the user who shut the lid came down to one grouped account of the same three endings. **Being attentive produced the worse morning, and that is the fault this clause removes.**

- **The trigger is the application of events, whichever pass applies them.** §7 step 3 on a connection and P10d's read-forward inside a standing period are one mechanism read at two moments; **everything applied by either pass is the catch-up account** (T18), on C1's own list of what the events say and with C4's gap rule unchanged. No new pass, no new store, no schedule and no watcher is added — the two passes already exist and already move the cursor.
- **One standing record per machine, added to rather than replaced.** C2's record is already singular per machine, written before the cursor advances, standing until the user's own dismissal. **Events applied while an undismissed record stands are composed into that record**; a fresh record is composed only where none stands. That is C2's *composes a notice only of what is new, or none* read against the pass rather than against the connection, and it is what makes the overnight case one account of three endings instead of three accounts of one.
- **The composition is identical in both cases because the records are identical.** Both passes apply the same journal events to the same local job records; nothing about the account is derived from how long a connection lasted, when it opened, or whether it broke. **No clock is involved** — C3's rule, kept — and there is no session, no window and no quiet period anywhere in this.
- **Nothing about the telling moves, and it is not this document's.** The account still posts no notification (T18), and the per-ending notifications the desktop fires from the job-ending records are `Desktop app and delivery`'s finished and failed kinds reading L18 and T11, untouched here. **This clause does not suppress, group, delay or hold any of them**, and it draws no screen: what the account looks like, whether it folds, and what dismissal does stay Design's, exactly as C2 and C2a already say. Whether a notification fired for an ending the standing account also names is that document's question and is routed to the runner, not answered here.
- **What it costs.** A record now stands and grows for a machine the user is watching, so a user who reads each ending as it lands still finds an undismissed account naming those endings and dismisses it. That is the same act they already take after a reconnection, and the alternative is the silence above.

**C2. The notice is a durable record of Smoozy's own composition, written before it is shown, which is what *told once* rests on across a restart.** Applying step 3 writes the notice's content into the local record as part of the application, before the cursor advances past the events it names (E3 — the cursor moves when the work the event asked for is done, and composing the account is that work). A crash between advancing and showing loses the showing and not the record; the next run shows the standing notice. A second connection in the same session finds the cursor past everything already told, so it composes a notice only of what is new, or none. The notice is T22's durable in-app record and its lifetime class is **subject-held on the server row** (§11.1), matching W5's mark: it is about one machine's journal, it ends with the row, and dismissal is the user's own act on Smoozy's own record. How it is shown and what dismissal does are Design's, per the spec's own pending.

**C2a. One message spanning several servers is a presentation over the standing records and never a record of its own. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 382.** [Design](../Design/Queue%20&%20History.md) promises *"One message, not a burst"*, grouped by server under headings carrying each server's badge (**Creator-stated**, 2026-08-13), and C2's record is per machine and ends with the server row.

- **C2 does not move.** The record stays singular, per machine, subject-held on the server row, dismissed by the user's own act. **Nothing spanning servers is stored anywhere**, so there is no second lifetime to reason about and no record that outlives a row it is about.
- **The message is *every standing, undismissed notice record at the moment it is drawn*, grouped by server.** One group per record. **A connection to a second server later in the same session adds its group to the same message** rather than raising a second one, because the message is composed at draw time and the earlier record is still standing.
- **Dismissing the message dismisses every record behind it**, each on its own terms — which is what makes *told once* survive a restart per C2, unchanged, and what stops a dismissed message reappearing because one of its groups was never dismissed. **Dismissing one group dismisses that record alone. **Changed 2026-10-12, Creator-stated, the Creator's ninth notes, note 3: opening History takes every ending off every standing record; a record left naming nothing is dismissed, and one still naming a dropped job stands until that job's line is dismissed, which takes that one name off it.** The message as one dismissable whole is gone with the banner. No auditor has seen it.**
- **The heading's count is composed at draw time from the same set** — jobs across records, servers as records — and is stored nowhere. *(From 2026-10-12 the drawn figure is the count of endings by kind — finished, failed, stopped, given up — across every standing record, composed at draw time and stored nowhere.)*
- **A server removed while its group stands takes its record with it**, per C2's own lifetime, and the message is one group shorter the next time it is drawn. **Nothing is left naming a row that is gone.**
- **What the message looks like, whether it folds, and what dismissal looks like stay Design's**, exactly as C2 already says, ~~and the Design document's own `[PENDING]` on surviving dismissal is untouched by this.~~ **and Design has answered them** (`Design/Queue & History`, *The catch-up notice*: it folds, it does not survive being dismissed, and *Dismiss* asks first where a line names a job that left no entry). *(From 2026-10-12 it is a count that opens History and is cleared by opening it, with one line per dropped job whose Dismiss asks first; the fold is gone.)* *(Corrected 2026-10-03: that marker was closed at Design and this pointer was stale; out of step 10c's map of the 38 promises, P7. No auditor of this document has seen it.)*

**C3. The point-since is the event cursor and nothing else.** Durable on the desktop against the server row and the installation identity it was taken under; never advanced on receipt; a new installation reads from the beginning of its own journal, never from a cursor taken under a dead one (§2.3, E2). No clock is involved, deliberately — T19's rule, kept.

**C4. A gap is stated, never smoothed.** Where the journal's oldest retained sequence number has passed the cursor (E5), the notice says the account is incomplete rather than presenting what arrived as the whole story — §7's own closing rule, applied to the one surface this document composes.

**C5. Where the machine is gone before the next connection, there is no notice, and the entries say what they know.** Its note and journal went with it; the tracked jobs that had started rendering read *no ending Smoozy ever learned*; nothing is invented to fill the silence.

**C6. Row 78 is D4 applied, and no fourth exception is created.** The last-known queue reading is **period-held**: process memory for the connected period, never durable, gone when the period ends — and the period is `Servers`' connected period, which runs until the user disconnects or the application closes, standing through a drop. So a dropped-connection server shows last-known entries for the rest of the run; a fresh launch knows nothing until it connects; and there is no durable cache anywhere that could be shown hours stale, which is the failure the promise exists to prevent — prevented by construction rather than by care.

**C7. A last-known entry carries the moment it was taken and says it is not updating — the marking is composable because the reading carries its own moment, and its trigger is the break declaration, never the period.** Every reading arrived stamped with its moment and clock (D5, R5). **The stamp and the not-updating statement appear at the moment `Servers` declares the break and tears the connection down (`Servers` [K3 and K5](Servers.md#21-the-break-reporting-mechanism))** — the same declaration that ends `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d's live-reading sequence, which is what was updating the entry — **and they stand until a later connection's `Servers` Q1 step 7 takes a fresh reading and starts a fresh sequence.** Not the first failed run of attempts, and not the period ending, which drops the record entirely (C6). **The mark also reaches a reading held while a connected server's [§7](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#7-reconciliation-on-the-desktop) has not completed** — a connection sitting on §2.3's fourth branch (`Servers` [Q19](Servers.md#10-the-first-connection-in-order-rows-36-and-35)) holds a reading taken on an earlier connection, and nothing is updating it — **because the mark is about the reading's age, never about the connection's state.** *Re-keyed 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R2: this clause keyed the mark on the entry outliving its connection, which named no moment and left a builder three candidates — the declared break, the first failed run, the period ending — while `Servers` H1 keyed the same mark on the period, so a dead reading could present as live.* The entry shows that stamp and the not-updating statement — wording Design's — and never its live readings as current. `Servers`' removal warning reads this same period-held record; where Smoozy holds none, no warning appears, whatever the reason — the spec tolerates every one of them by name. **`Servers`' edit warning is a second reader and it does not read this record. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Servers](Servers.md#16-unresolved) §16 item 19.** It reads the **durable job records** of C8 and C11 instead, which are Smoozy's own notes rather than a copy of a machine state, so §3's D4 is untouched and the warning is reachable when this period-held record is not. **The two warnings therefore differ in reach on purpose: the removal warning has nothing to read once the period has ended — a server disconnected by hand this run, an application closed and reopened (`Servers` S11, and W19's last row) — and the edit warning still warns there, from the durable records.** *Corrected 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R2: this sentence read "the removal warning is silent between runs of attempts", which contradicts `Servers` W19's third row — between runs of attempts a period stands, the record is held, and the warning warns from what Smoozy last knew, saying so. The reach difference is real and sits at the period's end, not between runs.*

**C8. What survives the period is exactly the three exceptions and this document's own notes**: the history record (Smoozy's own note, not a copy), the remembered server state (`Servers`' Row 28, subject-held, owed there), the tombstones, the event cursor, the catch-up notice record and the idle-spoken mark — each Smoozy's own record of Smoozy's own acts or D4's named exceptions, none a copy of a machine state the machine still holds.

**C9. A job is in history from the moment it starts rendering, and the moment is the machine's, learned late.** The render-start is journalled on the machine (N2); the desktop learns it at step 3 or sees it live, and from that learning the local job record — written at submission, provisional until the machine confirmed it — is presented in history as well as in the queue. The started moment shown is the note's, on the machine's clock, never the moment Smoozy learned it. **Narrowed 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's note 11 on the test copy, on the spec's change of that date: the record half of this clause stands and the list half moves.** The local job record is still written at submission and still takes the learned start, and the started moment is still the note's. **What changed is the history list: it presents a record once its ending is learned (C11), or once C9's second way in below has marked it; a record whose start is learned and whose ending is not is presented in the queue alone.** So the two lists no longer overlap on screen, and nothing else in this section moves. *No auditor has seen this paragraph.*

**One further way in, and it is not a learned start: a record whose render start was never learned, whose server is gone. Added 2026-09-18 from step 10b's code round *jobs, history and downloads*, item C1 — Creator-stated, 2026-09-18; no auditor of this document has seen it.** Two moments carry it, and they are the only two at which this side knows the machine that held the job is gone: **the server row is removed** (`Servers` [D17](Servers.md#65-what-a-removal-reaches-row-41)), and **§7 step 1 of [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#7-reconciliation-on-the-desktop) takes the absent or present-and-different branch** for that server — what D2 below calls a recycled or reimaged machine. At either, every local job record of that server whose render start has not been learned — provisional or confirmed alike — is **marked *start never heard*, durably, and not removed**; from that mark it is presented in history. **The mark is not a learned start**: the render-start flag stays false, no started moment is invented, and the entry reads its ending as absent and says that Smoozy never heard whether the job started — wording Design's. **The ground is the spec's**: a job that rendered unwatched and a job that only waited are indistinguishable from this side once the machine is gone, and the spec keeps both rather than dropping both, accepting that a job which never ran can appear. **It is an entry like any other from then on** — X1 deletes it with its tombstone, and nothing can fill it in, because nothing is left to read. **No absence, of any length, sets the mark** (`Whole app`'s *never claims to know which it is*): a server that simply does not answer leaves the record exactly as §7 step 2's third branch leaves it.

**C10. The two lists are two presentations of two records with one identity, not two stores of one fact.** The queue entry is the period-held reading of the machine's live state; the history entry is the local record. While both hold the job, the machine is where the live half is read from and Smoozy's record is what survives the machine — the spec's own division — and reconciliation is the standing machinery: events forward from the cursor (step 3), fill-in of what the local record lacks (step 4), never a merge rule invented here. **While this run's own submission act is in flight, a job whose provisional record is written is drawn in its server's line as *Submitting…* until the machine's row replaces it or the act removes the record; it is the act's, not a reading of either record.** Where the act ends not knowing whether the machine took the job, the row goes and what the queue already shows for that case takes over. *(Added 2026-10-10 on the Creator's seventh notes, note 6: a submitted job reached the queue only once the machine answered. No auditor has seen it.)* **Runner's ruling the same day:** the row is drawn from the moment the act starts — the press of *Submit* — not from the provisional record's writing, since the act reads the file on the server and runs the engine gate first and the Creator asked for the job *"immediately upon submitting"*; ~~a refusal before the record is written removes the row and the panel says why, as for one after.~~ **An act that ends with a job not in the machine's line leaves its row in the line, reading what the panel says — *Not submitted.* and why — with a cross that clears it.** It goes when the user clears it, submits that file to that server again, or the run ends. The panel says it too where it is open, but it is never the only place, because the user may have left it. This holds for a refusal before the record is written and for one after, and for an act that failed part-way, such as files not placed; the act not knowing whether the machine took the job keeps the handling the sentence above gives it. *(Changed 2026-10-19, out of the live pass on Vast for the thirteenth and fourteenth notes: the line went while a job was sent, the act ended "files not placed", and the row went with nothing said anywhere the user was looking — `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The faults the live pass found — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md`, fault 1. The struck words said the panel alone tells it, which it does only while it is open on that file. No auditor has seen it.)*

**C11. When the job ends it leaves the queue and the history entry takes the ending Smoozy learns** — from watching, or from the note on a later connection — and an entry whose machine never came back keeps the job with no ending Smoozy ever learned, shown as such, for good. **So does an entry C9's second way in made** — a job whose start was never learned, whose server is gone — shown as one Smoozy never heard whether it started, for good. *(Added 2026-09-18, item C1; no auditor has seen it.)*

---

## 12. The idle half hour

**Constraint:** Row 55. T9 and T14 — the idle kind is this document's reading and its half hour; D5 — no arithmetic across clocks; the notification itself is `Desktop app and delivery`'s.

**W1. A machine is idle when it is connected, no render operation is running, and its line holds nothing that can start** — an empty line, or only held jobs, which cannot start until a person answers (U5). *A job held for the driver cannot start until the desktop releases it, and counts the same way ([H12](#3-held-jobs)). Added 2026-10-11, the Creator's ruling that a job needing the newer driver is held back.* Untracked work counts as work: a machine rendering a declined job is busy, not idle, and the wait's stated cause is [Section 13](#13-reading-a-machine-and-adoption) A4's. **A transfer still running delays nothing here — added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R13**: an auto-download arrangement still bringing a finished job's outputs home is not a render operation and holds nothing in the line, so it neither delays the statement nor moves W2's quiet moment, and a builder adds no transfer condition to either.

**W1a. *Idle* is a present condition throughout this document and no figure says when a machine will become one. The mockup's *idle in 12 min* on a server heading is refused rather than supplied. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 424.** W1 to W6 produce the moment a machine **has** gone quiet and how long it has been quiet, and nothing anywhere produces the moment one **will**. [B9](#6-the-two-bars-and-the-countdown-that-is-not-offered) already declines the strictly smaller figure — a countdown to the end of one job — and the wider figure is refused on B9's ground plus three the smaller one never had to meet.

- **What the figure would have to be, stated so the comparison is arithmetic rather than assertion.** The remaining time of the render running now, **plus** the whole duration of every job in that machine's line that can start, **plus** whatever a person does with the held ones. B9's declined figure is the first term alone.
- **The first term is B9's, unchanged.** No running figure covers a whole animation in any version, the whole-job total is a plan adaptive sampling legitimately undershoots, and Blender's own estimate covers the frame in hand and comes and goes ([R6](#5-the-reader-of-a-running-render)).
- **The second term has no operand at all, and that is where the wider figure is strictly worse.** A job that has not started has no elapsed time, no counted frames and no estimate of Blender's. The only quantity this stage holds for it is [B1](#6-the-two-bars-and-the-countdown-that-is-not-offered)'s plan total, which [B5](#6-the-two-bars-and-the-countdown-that-is-not-offered) already bounds to *progress against the plan* with *"a job can correctly end short of it or pass it"*. Turning plan units into a duration needs a units-per-second rate for that scene on that machine, and **nothing in this stage measures, records or supplies one.**
- **The line is not a fixed quantity either, so even a perfect per-job duration would not close it.** Held jobs cannot start until a person answers (W1, U5), so a line holding one has no computable end. The user may add, reorder, hold or remove work at any moment ([Section 4](#4-acting-on-the-line)). And **untracked work counts as work** (W1, [A4](#13-reading-a-machine-and-adoption)): a machine rendering a declined job is busy, Smoozy holds no manifest, no plan total and no reading for that job, so any forecast is short by an amount it can neither bound nor state.
- **The harm B9 names is larger here rather than smaller.** B9's ground is *"a confident figure Smoozy cannot stand behind on a machine billed by the hour"*. A wrong countdown over one job is one job's error; a wrong countdown over a whole line is that error summed over every job on it, presented on the one heading that stands for the machine and therefore read as the machine's own statement about when it stops costing money.
- **What the heading carries instead is what this document already produces**: the present readings of the machine and its line, and W1 to W5's idle statement, which says a machine **has** been idle and for how long. **The mockup value is unsupported and goes back to Design to be parked** — `Preparation/Design/Mockup/About this mockup.md` already rules that where a drawing and the documents disagree the drawing moves, and nothing is repaired here. This is B9's own closing rule at the machine's scale: the heading where nothing is offered is the answer.

**W2. The quiet moment is the machine's own, read fresh, never remembered, and the rule is general with the list as examples.** It is the moment, on the machine's clock, of **the most recent recorded event that left the line without startable work** — a recorded ending, a clear's outcome, a drop's outcome, a cancel's removal, whichever is latest. The list does not bound the rule: an attempt that placed a hold at the start of its render and ended without rendering has the render kind's own recorded outcome for such an attempt (U6) and dates a stretch like any other. It is read off the machine's records on request while connected; no copy of it is held durably (D4 — it is exactly an observed machine state the machine still holds).

**Where no such event exists on this machine, the floor is stated rather than left to a builder.** A prepared, connected machine with an empty line and no work ever submitted is idle by W1 exactly and has no recorded event to date, so W3 would have no operand and the machine would either never speak or the builder would invent a moment. For that stretch alone: **the stretch is keyed on the installation identity with a null opening-event identity, and the quiet moment is the earliest event the machine's journal retains, on the machine's clock.** The key and the moment are split deliberately, because a later reader will otherwise reach for one anchor to serve both. Not the supervisor's start and not a fresh run identifier: a machine idle across a reboot would open a second stretch and speak twice, against W5's *once per idle stretch, across restarts*. Not the earliest retained event as the **key** either: it moves as the journal's cap drops the oldest entries (E5), with the same consequence. W5's mark already carries the installation identity beside the event identity, so this costs no new field and no new store, and the key is stable across both reboots and the cap. The moment the machine takes work and falls quiet again, the general rule above supplies a real opening event and W5's *new opening event, new stretch* applies unchanged.

**W3. The elapsed half hour is computed on the machine's clock alone.** Overdue is machine-now minus quiet-moment, both taken from the machine in one reading. A connection that finds a machine which went quiet an hour ago finds it long overdue and says so at once, rather than starting the wait over — the promise's own sentence, kept because the quiet moment is the machine's and not the connection's.

**W4. While connected and short of the half hour, the desktop schedules a re-check and re-verifies against the machine before speaking.** The remaining interval is machine-clock arithmetic; the desktop's own timer only schedules the re-check, and the claim is re-taken from the machine's readings at that moment — so no promise rests on the desktop's clock, and drift costs a re-check, never a wrong statement. Disconnected, nothing fires: an idle machine Smoozy is not connected to goes unremarked, which is the spec's own limit.

- **It is not a second timer, and the admission is now cited from this side. Added 2026-09-02, routed from `Desktop app and delivery` [P10e](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery).** [P10a](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery)'s two admitted shapes do not reach this re-check — it fires with nothing under way, an idle machine being its whole subject, and it does work rather than redraw, because it re-takes the claim from the machine — so under P10b's last sentence read alone a builder would not build it at all, and the one thing standing between the user and a machine billing for nothing would never fire while Smoozy stayed connected. **P10e admits a third shape for it and names this clause as its one instance**, on three parts all of which this clause already states: the wake-up is scheduled from a durable fact already held (W5's mark and W2's event, both records rather than a countdown), it re-verifies against the machine before it speaks (the sentence above), and it dies with the connected period (*disconnected, nothing fires*). **Nothing of this clause moved when the admission was written**, and the citation is here so a builder reading P10b alone finds the route back. This is the shape `Servers` Q19's last bullet and N2/N6's cadence already use for P10a, applied to P10e.

**W5. Speaks once per idle stretch, across restarts, by the idle-spoken mark.** The mark is one small record in the local record, subject-held on the server row, carrying the installation identity and the **event identity of the recorded event that opened the stretch** (W2's event). Before speaking, the mark is looked up; present for this stretch means already spoken. A machine that takes work and falls quiet again has a new opening event, a new stretch, and speaks again. The mark is Smoozy's own note of Smoozy's own act — like the event cursor, not a copy of machine state — and it is one of the two record kinds [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 13 hands to the store-inventory seam.

**W5a. What *once per stretch* bounds is the telling and nothing drawn. The heading's standing statement is not bounded by the mark and is redrawn as often as the surface is. Added 2026-09-02, settling the reading W5 and [W1a](#12-the-idle-half-hour) left open between them.** W5 reads as though it bounded the whole of what this document produces about an idle machine, and [W1a](#12-the-idle-half-hour)'s closing bullet has the server heading carrying *W1 to W5's idle statement* as what stands there instead of the refused figure — a surface, not an event. Read together, a builder gating the heading on the mark would draw the idle statement once and then draw nothing, on a machine that is still idle, which is the one thing W1a promised the heading would carry.

- **The mark gates one act: speaking.** The telling is `Desktop app and delivery`'s notification kind (T14, [17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)), and it is Smoozy's own act at the user rather than a reading. The mark is looked up before that act and written by it; nothing else reads it. **A second stretch, a second telling** — W5 unchanged.
- **The heading is a composition over W1 to W3's readings, redrawn like any other reading.** Whether a machine is idle (W1) and how long it has been quiet (W2, W3) are taken from the machine while connected, period-held, and the heading states them for as long as they are true. That is [17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)'s own first test, already applied to the idle reading: it is a reading and not Smoozy's own act, so it is drawn at the screen's rate and never at the user's. **The mark has no place in it**, and a heading that went blank because the notification had already fired would be stating *not idle* about an idle machine.
- **So the two clauses are about two different things and neither bounds the other.** A machine idle for six hours is told about once and says *idle for six hours* on its heading throughout, the figure moving as W3's arithmetic moves. **A restart changes neither half**: the mark survives it (W5), and the heading is composed fresh from the machine on the next connection, which is C6's period-held rule and not an exception to it.
- **Nothing is added and nothing moved.** No second mark, no store, no schedule — the heading's redraw is `Desktop app and delivery` [P10a](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery)'s drawing against a published moment, and the re-check that produces a fresh reading is W4's, admitted at P10e.

**W6. Fact 42 is answered for Vast.ai and moves nothing, as this clause always said it would not.** **CLOSED 2026-08-21 by reading Vast.ai's own billing page** — *"You are charged the base active rental cost for every second your instance is in the active/connected state"*, at a rate quoted in $/hr (read 2026-08-20). The rate is hourly, the charge is per second. Settles Vast.ai only; the blanket form is what is refuted, and the spec's sentence is corrected. Nothing about the feature moved. What it broke is the spec's stated *reason* for the half hour and not one clause of the arrangement — a machine doing nothing costs money however it is billed. **Updated 2026-08-21**; this clause used to close it as unresolved for want of a provider's page nobody had read. [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 2.

---

## 13. Reading a machine, and adoption

**Constraint:** Rows 79 and 80. Fact 40 answered in full; §7 steps 1, 5 and 6; §2.2's rule that all adoptable work carries an identity this desktop issued; `Servers`' Row 43 is the other side and is owed, not answered.

**A1. The queue reading is one request, on demand, after the installation identity confirms, and it returns the machine's whole working state.** Per job: its state, its admission point standing, its operation, its unreleased hold — the shape Fact 40 answered — plus the note (N1) and the machine's own history copy's count and entries. It is a reading, period-held, stamped with moment and clock; it is never taken before §7 step 1 confirms the installation, and on the absent or different branches nothing on the machine is read as Smoozy's at all. **The request recurs within a connected stretch on `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d's live-reading sequence — added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R1**: the same one request, one at a time, period-held as ever, repeated while a surface showing that server's queue or its running entry is on screen and not otherwise. *On demand* names who composes the request and includes that sequence's passes; this document still holds no schedule of its own. **From 2026-10-31 the reading carries two more things, for `Telegram` V1d: per job, its current reporting choice where `Telegram` V1c's record value is written; and once, the machine's phone's choice — value, operation identity and Telegram's `date` *(and Telegram's update identity, which orders two choices of one `date`: added 2026-10-31 by the server-half builder, no auditor has seen it)* — where one is held.** The desktop writes each tracked job's choice into its own record, so the Queue's control shows what the machine holds after a change made from the phone, and takes the phone's choice as `Telegram` V1d says. Both are absent on a machine that holds neither, and an older desktop ignores them. *Added 2026-10-31, the Telegram discussion of that day (`Specs/Telegram.md`, *One choice for every job, from the phone*). No auditor has seen it.*

**A2. The reading reaches untracked and declined work because the store does not distinguish them** (Q6). What distinguishes them is the desktop's local record: identities it names are tracked; identities it does not are untracked.

**A3. Where a machine cannot be read, Smoozy says it could not check, and never connects silently.** The supervisor-unavailable condition (§8.3) is raised by the request not being answered **with the transport shown to have carried it — `Server protocol and transfers` [C17a](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#46-per-waiter-deadlines)'s verdict, consumed rather than retaken: a request left unanswered inside a break `Servers` then declares raises nothing**; the queue shows the condition rather than a guessed line; a partial account is stated as partial (§7's closing rule). **Narrowed 2026-08-29, out of the scenario replay of *The connection drops while rendering*.** This sentence read unconditionally, that document's C17 read the opposite way, and between them a request left unanswered by the user's own dying line — one is ordinarily in flight when the line dies under `Desktop app and delivery` P10d's live-reading sequence — could raise a machine verdict standing beside the connection-down statement for the whole outage, with nothing to withdraw it while no request could be composed. `Preparation/Specs/Whole app.md` forbids exactly that: *"A lost connection is a fact about the connection, never a verdict on the machine."*

**A4. Declined work is sayable as a fact without being shown as jobs.** The queue states, from the reading, that the machine is busy with work the user chose not to track — the fact of it, the count of it, never the jobs themselves — so a tracked job sitting at the top of the queue and not starting has a stated cause. Nothing durable holds it (C6); each connection reads it fresh.

**A5a. The offer is unfiltered, and no pre-check asks whether the current login could act on the work. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Servers](Servers.md#16-unresolved) §16 item 6.** A5's one offer per machine covering everything at once already admits this reading; it is stated from this side so a builder does not add a filter. **The ground is `Servers` 11.3's**: a refusal is discovered by trying, not predicted, so a pre-check would be a guess that could withhold work the user can in fact act on. What follows a refusal is that document's.

**A5. The adoption offer is §7 step 6, composed from the reading, one per machine, covering everything at once.** The set is every job identity on the machine the local record does not name — less any identity a history tombstone names, which is not offerable work but an owed erase (§10.1's own rule: the next connection to a machine holding that identity removes it). Declining writes nothing durable, so the next connection that finds the work offers again — the spec's *declining is not final*, delivered by the absence of a record rather than by a schedule.

**A6. Accepting is atomic entire: every offered job enters the local record in one durable write, under the identity the machine carries.** All or nothing is the offer's own shape (§9.2 test 1 — the promise is about the offer, not about members); the identities are kept, never reissued (Y4); each adopted record is written **confirmed**, not provisional — the work demonstrably exists on the machine.

**A7. An adopted job's entry shows what the machine's records hold, and that is more than the spec's floor.** Untracked work was once submitted from this desktop, so the machine holds its manifest — scene, frame range, Blender version, settings payload — its note with the label and times, its attempt records, its reading where one was taken. Step 4's fill-in carries all of it to the entry; *whatever the machine can be read for and no more* is exactly the fill-in's reach. Where a record is unreadable, the entry states the absence.

**A8. Adoption writes Smoozy's note at adoption, and history behaves from there as if the note had been written at submission.** The local record entered at A6 is Smoozy's own note from that moment; a job already rendering enters history at once (C9); a job that ends later takes its ending by the ordinary routes. Every guarantee the document rests on holds for the adopted job by construction: its folder was named for its identity when it was first submitted, *no job ever writes over another* stands on the identity that never changed, and re-runnability is the machine's own reconciliation, which never needed Smoozy — the spec's conditional dissolves into the ordinary machinery, and where a machine-side record is damaged, C20's whole-range rule is the floor.

**A9. Nothing about adoption restores a deletion.** The tombstone exclusion in A5 is what keeps Row 82's promise across the one route that could quietly break it.

---

## 14. Deletion

**Constraint:** Row 82. §10.1's tombstone is built and is not re-invented; Fact 41 is answered in part and the remainder is this section's problem; the spec's own rule — *deleting history destroys the record, not the renders* — bounds what the erase may reach.

**X1. Deleting an entry removes the local record and writes the tombstone in one atomic local act.** The tombstone is §10.1's: keyed by the job identity alone, holding the deletion moment and whether the machine-side copy has gone, refusing step 4's fill-in unconditionally, kept for the life of the local record. Removing the record removes everything subject-held on it — the fetched derivatives among them (§11.3). The two writes are one unit (§9.3): a record cannot be gone with no tombstone, and a tombstone cannot exist for a record still standing.

**X1a. A first-frame pair drawn as one History entry is deleted as one: X1 over each of its two records. Added 2026-10-27 for the nineteenth notes, note 7, Creator-stated: "The first-frame check is not bundled with the job it's connected to clearly enough."** Where Design draws a check and the range job whose local record names it (P5a) as one entry, its deletion runs X1 on both records — one user act, atomic within each record, each with its own tombstone, as X6 is over a machine's entries. While a held or line job's record names a check as its dependency, that check's record is not deleted alone, because the waiting entry reads its previews from the check (P5a). No auditor has seen it. *(From 2026-10-29 the check writes into its range job's output folder (`Job files and outputs` P18a), so the check's *Writing to* and *Files on the server* open that one folder, and deleting the check's record removes only the files the check's own inventory names. The twenty-first notes, note 6. No auditor has seen it.)*

**X2. What the machine-side erase reaches is enumerated here, exactly, and it is the record — never the renders.** The owed erase removes, under the deleted job's identity: the machine's own history copy; the per-job note; the queue job record where one still stands; **the hold record naming that identity as its subject** — `Rented-server runtime` U22's third ending, *its subject is removed and the record goes with it*, and R1's retention row, which ends a hold record with its subject's machine-side record by the same rule this enumeration already cites for the operation and command records — accounted as its own unit in X3's enumeration, its outcome gating the tombstone's flag exactly as the others do, because a hold record left with no release outcome written reads as unreleased to `Rented-server runtime` G21's commit check and G23 then blocks every package commit on that machine; the operation and command records (R1's rule — they end with their subject's machine-side record), **the erase's own command record excepted, which is the record of the act and is never among the records the act removes**; the capture records **and both captured streams**; the job record directory entire — manifest, recorded reading, attempt records, launch material, machine-side derivatives (`Job files and outputs` §11 — the record directory ends when the job's record is gone); and the remap material at `remaps/<job identity>` (`Dependency Upload & Remap` U2). *Added 2026-10-16, owed piece 11, on the build runner's ruling of that day: **a job's remap material is erased only where no other job's manifest still names it**, because `Telegram` Y1's *try it again* copies the failed job's manifest with its reference to that material, so the job's material is its own location and the one its manifest names, the last job naming a shared material takes it, and a material kept this way leaves its unit done; no auditor has seen this sentence.* **It does not touch the job output folder or anything in it**: the rendered frames are files of the user's, and the spec says in as many words that deleting history destroys the record and not the renders. Where the frames go is the user's own act through `File Browser`, or the machine-reaching server removal, or the machine's destruction.

**X3. Fact 41's remainder is the gap between that enumeration and what the one observed removal reached, and the erase is specified to close it.** The observed removal ended the history entry, the note, the collection record and the queue entry, journalled, touching no other job — and left the captured streams, a rendered frame and the launch material on disk, fully readable, under the removed identity, with the machine reporting nothing about them. The frame staying is correct (X2); the streams and launch material staying is the shortfall. So the erase is one command with a §9.4 outcome record in the local record, holding **the enumeration of X2's units durable on the desktop before the first unit is attempted** and each unit's outcome as it becomes true — partial across the material kinds, atomic within each directory removal. **Removal of a unit already absent is recorded *done*, never *failed***, because the erase must be able to run against a machine where earlier units already went. The tombstone's machine-side flag is set only when every unit is done, and the tombstone keeps its one flag and grows no enumeration of its own (§10.1). Anything less leaves the debt standing, and **the remainder is taken by a new action with a new operation identity, composed from the outcome record's enumeration** (§9.5 — no unit is retried inside its action), at the next connection to a machine holding that identity. That the widened erase completes as specified has not been observed — one removal per box, not repeated, streams not included — and [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 5 names what breaks.

**X4. A deletion made while disconnected is remembered as a deletion, and only as one.** The local record goes at once; the tombstone is the something that survives — one identity in size, meeting no user surface, which is how *nothing anywhere shows the job* and *it does not come back* hold together. The next connection to a machine holding that identity runs the erase as a recorded debt (§10.1).

**X5. Filling in never brings a deleted entry back, and neither does adoption.** Step 4 is refused unconditionally by the tombstone; step 6 never offers a tombstoned identity, because §10.1 makes such work the erase's and A5 keeps it out of the offer. There is no third route by which a machine's holdings become an entry.

**X6. Wiping a machine's history is the per-entry deletion over every entry for that machine, tombstones and all** — one user act, partial across entries, atomic within each, each with its own tombstone so no later connection restores any of it.

**An entry in progress has no per-entry deletion, so the wipe passes over it.** That is an entry whose job has started, whose ending Smoozy has not learned, and whose server row still stands — read at the draw, as N17 reads it. Design gives it no *Delete*, because deleting it would take the record of a job still running on the machine. It is cleared like any other once it has an ending or its server row is gone. *Added 2026-10-01, from Build: `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — twelfth-pass fix piece 5b, clearing history keeps jobs in progress.md`, on piece 5's finding (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Twelfth-pass fix piece 5 — what landed.md`, For the runner, item 1). No auditor has seen it.*

**X6a. Clearing the whole of history is X6's act over every machine's entries, and the two boxes' listings are compositions at the draw. Added 2026-08-25, per Row 497.** The act across machines is the spec's own third — *"clear the whole of history at once — the third of those added 2026-08-24"*, **Creator-stated** — so nothing here grants it; what is arranged is how it holds over machinery deletion already has.

- **The act.** The per-entry deletion of X1, over every history entry the local record holds, every machine's alike, tombstones and all — one user act, **partial across entries and therefore across machines, atomic within each entry's record-plus-tombstone unit**, each deleted entry with its own tombstone so no later connection restores any of it. That is X6's own shape scoped to the set rather than to one machine, cited and re-decided nowhere; *for that machine* is read off the copied local server identifier (N12) for both acts, the per-server act taking the open entry's. An interrupted clear leaves the remaining entries standing and drawn, and the act is the user's to take again; nothing resumes it and nothing needs to, each deleted entry's tombstone and debt being complete on its own.
- **The machine halves are the standing per-entry debts, and nothing spanning machines exists.** Each deleted entry's owed erase is recorded against its own tombstone (X1, X4) and run at the next connection to a machine holding that identity (X3, §10.1); a machine never connected to again never pays, its tombstones stand for the life of the local record, and no record, no unit and no outcome spans two machines. **That is how *afterwards nothing in Smoozy shows those jobs were submitted* holds while erase debts stand unpaid**: the sentence is about what Smoozy shows, and it holds at the local act — no entry, no fill-in (X5), no adoption offer (A5), no way through a carry-on mark (N20) — while the machine-side residue is a recorded debt and never a showing, exactly as a single deletion made disconnected already is (X4).
- **An entry whose copied server identifier dangles is deleted like any other.** The local half is unconditional and reads no server row (X1, N17); the tombstone is written with its machine-side flag unset; and where a machine — the same physical one under a new row included — is ever connected again holding the identity, §10.1's own rule makes the erase that connection's work (A5's exclusion).
- **Both boxes' figures are composed at the draw, over the local records alone, and stored nowhere** — C2a's shape, a presentation over standing records and never a record of its own. The per-server box counts the entries the list draws for the open entry's server; the all-history box lists those entries grouped **on the copied local server identifier** (N12), each group drawn under the copied label and hue and counted by its entries. **That falls on N12's own side of its guarantee, kept rather than skirted**: no live server list is composed — the groups are history's own entries under names history itself carries; no server row is consulted, matched or reconstructed — a dangling identifier still keys its group and the copies are what draw it, which is what the copies are for (N12, N13); and nothing offers, connects to or claims a server out of any of it. An entry predating N12's fields groups on the absent value and is stated plainly (N13). Tombstones are covered by the act and counted in no figure, meeting no surface (X4).
- **X6's pass-over holds for both acts, and both figures count only what goes.** An entry in progress is neither deleted nor counted, and each box says in one line that it is kept. The server-row lookup that decides *in progress* is N17's, and it decides nothing else: the groups, their labels and their hues still read the copies alone, as above. *Added 2026-10-01, with X6's paragraph on the same grounds. No auditor has seen it.* **Narrowed 2026-10-04 with C9: a running job's record is no longer in the list either box acts on, so neither box says it is kept.** The pass-over itself stands — the record is still the running job's, and neither act takes it.
- **What neither act touches is X2's own line**: no job output folder, no render, and nothing on the user's own computer — the boxes say so in Design's words, and the erase each deletion owes reaches the record material X2 enumerates and never the renders.
- The boxes' shapes, their wording and their named buttons are Design's.

**X6b. Clearing the history of removed servers is X1's deletion over every entry whose copied server identifier resolves to no server row. Added 2026-10-24 by the design pass for the eighteenth notes, ruling 10, Creator-stated, note 13: "History should have one more button — delete jobs from servers that are no longer in Smoozy."** The set is read at the act, over the local records alone, with N17's lookup and nothing else: an entry whose copied identifier dangles is in it, and every entry whose server row stands is not. **The act is X6a's shape scoped to that set** — partial across entries, atomic within each entry's record-plus-tombstone unit, each entry with its own tombstone — and **it reaches no machine at the act**: no row stands to connect through, and nothing is opened or asked. Each tombstone's machine-side flag is written unset, exactly as X6a's dangling-identifier bullet already has it, so a physical machine that is ever added again under a new row and still holds an identity pays that erase at its connection (§10.1, A5's exclusion) and fill-in never brings an entry back (X5). The box's figures are composed at the draw over the same set, grouped on the copied identifier with the copied label (N12) and counted by entries; nothing is stored. X2's line holds: no render and nothing on the user's computer is touched. The box's shape and words are Design's. No auditor has seen it.

**X7. The residue was checked against the third escalation and the check is recorded.** After a deletion with the erase complete, what remains on a machine the user pays for is the job output folder — which the spec names as staying, names in the delete confirmation as staying, and gives the user their own routes to. The record-material residue the throwaway left is exactly what X3's enumeration exists to remove, and where the erase has not yet run the tombstone carries it as a recorded debt rather than a silence. Nothing survives a completed deletion that the spec does not tell the user survives. Not an escalation; [Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator) carries the check.
