## 15. The previews

**Constraint:** Rows 84 and 85. `Job files and outputs` §8 settles the derivative set — made once, on the machine, both readers reading the same set — the candidate list, the rungs, the two picks made once and never recomputed; §11.3 makes the fetched copy subject-held. What is left here: when a running job's preview refreshes, what triggers the fetch, where the fetch sits in the transfer order, and the second choice's per-version name.

**P1. Production is dense: one derivative per finished frame's chosen candidate, produced on the machine by the preview worker, at low priority, cancellable, never blocking a render.** The set and the route are B22–B29's; the production policy is this document's: as frames complete — P1a's trigger — the supervisor queues preview work per frame's candidate — B24's declaration at the arbitration point, admitted under A3 and A8's guaranteed share, displaced by an exclusive render under A6 and re-queued at the next admission, in frame order. **No finished frame is passed over.** A job rendering faster than the worker keeps up (B24's measured 2.4 seconds with the cores taken makes that ordinary) leaves a backlog bounded by the frame count and by nothing else, and the backlog is worked off rather than dropped: the spec's promise is that the user moves through a job's finished frames, and a set with holes does not let them — P1a's re-derived prefix and P1b's ending catch-up are the working-off, so a displaced, missed or failed production costs a delay and never a hole. Production needs no connection, which is what lets `Telegram` read the same set with Smoozy closed. What the dense set costs is at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 18 and the check is at [Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator).

- **One copy per View Layer, not per frame alone. Added 2026-10-10, from the Creator's seventh notes, note 4; no auditor has seen it.** Where a frame's main copy (P10) is made from a multi-layer file — or, where it is not, from each layer's own destination (`Job files and outputs` B31a's last bullet) —, the worker also makes one copy for **every other View Layer in that frame's set** — item 5's set read with the job's choices, exactly as `Job files and outputs` B26 step 1 reads it — **whose picture that same file holds**, found by the same rungs and by that layer's name (B19, B19a). B26's multi-layer route reads `<view layer>.Combined` and nothing else, so a layer's copy is its Combined, and a layer whose Combined the file does not hold has no copy and owes none. Every other source — an ordinary picture, a file output node's picture, the latest image — gives one copy per frame, as before. *(Changed 2026-10-12, Creator-stated, the Creator's ninth notes (2026-10-11), note 2: where the main copy is not made by the multi-layer route, each other view layer in the frame's set gets its copy from a destination only it feeds, by `Job files and outputs` B31a, and a layer with none has no copy; the sentence before stands for a frame where no such destination exists. No auditor has seen it.)* **Each copy is one production and one Blender start** *(two or three where its first write weighs over `Job files and outputs` B26a's byte cap, all inside the one production; added 2026-11-02, no auditor has seen it)*, queued in frame order with the main copy first and the frame's other layers after it in the set's order; P1a's and P1b's *no published derivative and no production standing* is read per copy, so a missing layer copy is owed and queued exactly as a missing frame is. **The cost multiplies by the layers:** B24's 0.8 to 2.4 seconds per copy (B26's multi-layer route measured 0.5 to 1.4), times the number of View Layers each frame holds — so a five-layer job makes up to five times the copies, and the backlog P1 already allows grows by the same factor, still bounded by frames times layers and worked off rather than dropped.

**P1a. *As frames complete* has one trigger, and it is a record of Smoozy's own observation, never a reading of the folder. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R25.** The render worker observes, as the bytes pass its own copy loop, **each frame's save announcement** — the stream's own after-write signal, printed in the words the version prints (*When a render ends*: a part-written file is announced as saved in exactly the words a whole one is, so the line proves the write ended and never that the file is whole — a limit the candidate rules already absorb, an empty file never being a candidate). The observation is the worker's own act at its loop, the same discipline as M28's marker and E13's device line, outside R6's closed list of the reader's readings, so §1.3 rule 5 stands untouched. It is taken per version's own grammar, recorded separately per R2's rule — and the start-of-render frame announcements the newer grammar carries are not the signal: a frame announced is a frame begun, and only its save ends the write. What the worker holds from it is one figure, **the newest frame of the attempt's list whose save has been announced** — frames are worked through ascending, one at a time, in the order given, so the figure marks the list's completed prefix — reported to the supervisor on R5's own bounded reporting and re-established across a supervisor restart by the same W12 route. **On a report, the supervisor queues preview work, in frame order, for every frame of that prefix whose chosen candidate has no published derivative and no production standing — queued or running.** So a production that failed — for want of room on the mount or otherwise — leaves its frame owed and queued again at the next report and at P1b's write, never retried inside its own action (§9.5), never on a timer, and never on a free-space reading; and a missed or delayed report costs a delay, never a hole. A frame the candidate rules exclude — a video destination, an interrupted job's last frame asked for — is excluded here too (P9, B20): this clause queues the same work P1 queues, and only fixes when. Where the stream prints nothing (R4's two cases), no report ever arrives, no per-frame trigger fires, and P1b is the route the set is produced by — the previews then arrive at the ending rather than during the render, which is the truthful reading of a stream that said nothing.

**P1b. The ending's write is the catch-up, and it is what makes *no finished frame is passed over* true against every route a per-frame trigger can miss. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 items R25 and R18.** In the same pass in which a recorded outcome that ends a job is written — whoever writes it, the worker path or reconciliation, which is H7's own shape — the supervisor reads the completeness reading carried with that outcome and queues preview work, in frame order, for every frame it counts already there whose chosen candidate has no published derivative and no production standing, under P1a's same exclusions. This is a read of records Smoozy wrote — the carried reading, and the derivative operations' own records — never a walk of this clause's own: the one walk behind it is the ending step's, already taken (H7). What it recovers, by construction: the frames of a stream that printed nothing (R4), frames whose reports a supervisor restart lost, an interrupted first attempt's frames at the second attempt's ending, and every production that failed for want of room — including where the mount was so full that the production's own outcome could not be recorded, since the condition reads what stands, not what failed. **Where even the ending's write is refused for space, nothing here fires until that write lands**: when it lands is Q8's and N5's ground — after space returns, by the supervisor's restart or by `Rented-server runtime` S14's pending-write landing *(the route §3.3 supplies, named 2026-08-22 when R10 closed)* — and the catch-up rides the write itself, so the backlog's recovery needs no trigger of its own beyond the ending's. [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 1 names what breaks while nothing can land.

**P2. What the running entry shows is the newest derivative the set holds, and that is a rule about display and refresh rather than about production.** B31 hands the *when* here, and it binds the **fetch**: at most one preview fetch for the running job's live preview is in flight, taking the newest derivative the set holds at admission. So a job rendering faster than the connection keeps up shows the newest finished derivative on the running entry rather than every frame in turn, which is a preview and not a viewer — and the frames it passed over are in the set, produced by P1 and fetched by P4's ordinary per-derivative route behind it.

- **Added 2026-10-10, from the Creator's seventh notes, note 4; no auditor has seen it.** The newest derivative the live fetch takes is the newest frame's **main copy** (P10); that frame's other View Layer copies, like every passed-over frame's, come by P4's ordinary route behind it. **What the entry shows is the newest frame only while the user follows it**: stepping to another frame shows that frame from the copies already held, and stepping back onto the newest frame, or *Show latest*, follows again. The stepping is the desktop's alone and reads only held copies — no fetch, no request to the machine. **Added 2026-10-23 for seventeenth notes 5–7:** the typed signed frame number also reads only held copies; Enter selects it and disables following even at the newest frame, until *Show latest*. Missing or invalid numbers leave the picture and following unchanged. History’s *Show last* selects the last held frame. Enlargement reuses the same copy and selection, makes no additional fetch, and retains following/selection across entry refreshes. This extends the arrow-only selection described above; no auditor has seen it.

**P3. Each derivative's production is journalled as its operation's outcome, which is what the desktop's fetch triggers on.** No polling and no push: the desktop learns of new derivatives by reading forward from its cursor while connected, and at step 3 on a fresh connection. ***While connected* is `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d's live-reading sequence — added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R1** — whose read-forward repeats for the whole connected stretch; before that clause the words named a route no clause anywhere ran. *No polling* is P10b's and §8.4's sense — a standing timer or a condition watcher — and that bounded sequence is stated there not to be one. **The published event carries the source `Job files and outputs` B31a records for a copy that names no View Layer, in a frame of more than one — the regular output path, or the File Output node by name — written only where present.** *Added 2026-10-17, the Creator's tenth notes, note 2: the desktop names such a main copy by where it came from and lists it first. No auditor has seen it.*

**P4. The desktop fetches what it learns of and holds it subject-held on the job record.** One fetch per derivative, through the transfer contract — staging, integrity, publication last — partial across derivatives, atomic within one. **CORRECTED 2026-08-22 by the Creator's ruling.** This sentence used to read: ~~*"The fetched copy is cleared when the job record is deleted and at no other moment (§11.3): never on age, never on a cap, never because the machine is still reachable."*~~ **What it says now: the fetched copy is cleared when the job record is deleted, or when it has been held longer than thirty days (§11.1's age ending, §11.3), whichever comes first — never on a cap, and never because the machine is still reachable.** **The period is thirty days and it is set — ruled 2026-09-02 under the Creator's standing delegation, at `State, identity and recovery` §11.1 R16, measured from the moment the copy was published on the user's own computer, on that computer's clock (its §11.2 R19). Corrected here 2026-09-02: this clause said *the ruling's period* against a period nobody had stated, and the ending is now buildable.** Nothing of this document's half moves with the figure: the fetch, the holding and P4a's no-re-fetch rule are unchanged, and no clause here reads the number from anywhere. A frame's derivative replaced on the machine (B29 publishes over the name) is journalled and re-fetched, replacing the held copy. **Across a server's jobs, the running job's newest main copy (P2, P10) is fetched first and each ended job's newest main copy next, before any other View Layer copy or passed-over frame (added 2026-10-27, the Creator's nineteenth notes, note 10; no auditor has seen it).**

**A failed desktop fetch stays owed and is tried again on the next live read-forward pass, even if that pass learns no new event. Added 2026-10-23, the Creator’s seventeenth notes, note 13; no auditor has seen it.** Each fetch sequence asks for a derivative at most once, so a failure cannot run an immediate retry loop. The next existing `Desktop app and delivery` P10d pass supplies the next opportunity; no timer or extra read is added. Only owed fetches are considered: held copies, productions settled absent, and copies cleared by age are untouched.

- **The key is (job, frame, View Layer). Added 2026-10-10, from the Creator's seventh notes, note 4; no auditor has seen it.** A derivative is one per job, frame and View Layer on the machine and on the user's computer alike, so two layers' copies of one frame never publish over each other's name and never replace each other's held copy. **The View Layer part is B31a's recorded name, and absent where B31a records none** — a copy carrying no name is the frame's only copy, keyed by (job, frame) with the layer absent, which is every copy held before this rule. Each copy's record also carries **whether it is the frame's main copy** (P10) and **its place in the frame's set** (P1), both written on the machine with the production, so the desktop orders a frame's layers and finds its main copy without reading anything else. The age ending, the deletion and P4a apply to each copy on its own.

**P4a. A copy cleared by age is not fetched again, and that is a rule this document owes the ruling. Added 2026-08-22.** The fetch triggers on reading an event forward from the cursor (P3), and the cursor has long since passed the event for a derivative old enough to be swept, so nothing re-fetches by construction — and nothing is added that would. **The clearing queues no fetch of its own**, on a machine still reachable or otherwise: a preview cleared on age and fetched again would spend the user's connection on the same picture for as long as the job record stood, which is the unbounded state the ruling replaced wearing different clothes. So the picture is gone from that entry for good, whether or not the machine that made it still exists — and where it still exists, the machine-side copy is untouched by any of this and P8's clearing triggers are unchanged.

**P5. Catching up is the same trigger on the next connection, which is the spec's own sentence.** Frames rendered while the user was away produce derivatives while away; the connection's read-forward finds them; the fetches queue. Previews already held show with nothing connected, from the held copies.

**P5a. First-frame purpose survives in the existing job records. Added 2026-10-23 for seventeenth note 2.** At pair submission, record the check purpose and exact frame on the check job, and its identity plus the submitted range/list on the held job’s existing desktop record; these are descriptive job metadata, not a second hold. Read the machine’s existing hold dependency to recover the relationship after reconnect, and use the dependency’s previews for the approval entry while all approval commands target the held job. History retains the check purpose with its ordinary job record. Missing legacy metadata is unknown; do not infer a check from a one-frame range or hide a History record. The machine’s hold remains the sole approval authority. No auditor has seen it.

**P5b. The frame text the user typed is a field of the local job record, and the entry shows it. Added 2026-10-24 by the design pass for the eighteenth notes, ruling 6, Creator-stated, note 8: "queue and history should show it the same way."** At submission, where the user typed a range or list and Render Settings accepted it (`Render Settings` F1), the desktop writes that accepted text into the job's local record beside the kept setup, in the same write as the record's other submission fields (N11, N12) — one value per job, a scene-job of an all-scenes submission carrying the group's typed text where the range was typed for the group. **It is presentation only**: it is never parsed again, never counted, never compared with what the machine reports, and never sent to a machine; the payload's parsed form (F3) stays the only source for counts, progress, the first-frame pair's range (P5a) and *Carry on*. **Queue and History draw it exactly as stored wherever the entry, its row or the waiting first-frame question names the job's frames**; an entry whose record has no text — a job submitted before this, one adopted from a machine (A5), one submitted from another computer, or one whose range was the file's own — draws the numbers form from the kept setup, as before. *Submit again* hands the text to the page it opens (`Render Settings` F1); *Carry on* composes its own range (`Rendering` A4) and hands none. Deleting the record deletes the text with it (X1). No auditor has seen it.

**P6. The preview fetch declares its transfer class: below every user-asked class and below auto-download, never starved.** Preview traffic is the one transfer nobody asked for by name, so it ranks last — behind manual downloads, uploads and auto-download alike — and T10's no-starvation guarantee is what keeps *catching up the way an auto-download does* true on a busy line. The relative order among the other classes is Rows 189's and 207's, declared at `File Browser` [6.2 O4–O5](File%20Browser.md#62-the-order-and-the-shares-row-189) on 2026-08-20 with this declaration taken as its lowest rank — the constraint the struck Row 86 left behind, honoured. *(Reworded 2026-08-29, routed by the `File Browser` gate: this read **its fifth and lowest rank**, true of the five classes O4 listed and no longer true — `Rendering` §7 declares a submission class against that list, which makes the preview fetch the sixth. The ordinal is dropped rather than re-counted, because it will age out again the moment a seventh class is declared and O4 exists to let later documents declare them; **lowest** is what this clause actually fixes and it holds at any count. **The same sentence's *the other four classes* aged out on the same fact and by the same cause** — it counted O4's list as five — and is dropped to *the other classes* on the same preference; the routing named the ordinal alone, and this second figure is reported to the runner rather than left standing in a sentence being corrected for exactly it.)*

**P7. *Throwaway* is true only on the machine, and the two lifetimes are cited, not re-decided.** The machine-side copy can always be made again while the machine exists and may be cleared once the fetched copy exists or the record is gone; the fetched copy is not throwaway, because the ordinary end of a job is the machine being destroyed and then the copy can never be made again. A finished job keeps its preview because the fetched set lives with the history record — **for as long as the age ending leaves it there, corrected 2026-08-22 by the Creator's ruling; this clause carried no qualifier before that date, and the spec's own sentence is corrected on the same date rather than narrowed here**; that picture is what a finished-job notification attaches (T15), and nothing about any of this brings a rendered file home — the derivative is a viewing-format small copy, and a preview and a download stay different things.

**P8. The Open Threads seam about a never-fetched machine-side derivative narrows to a machine never connected to again.** This document fetches on learning (P3–P5), so a derivative nothing ever fetches is one produced after the last connection a machine ever gets; its clearing trigger is then the job's record going or the machine's destruction, which is the ordinary end. Recorded as the seam's reach, not repaired.

**P9. The rungs are applied as `Job files and outputs` B17–B19 states them, and the second choice is written per version off the manifest.** The beauty pass first — **Combined**, one name in every version; the diffuse colour pass second — **`DiffCol` in 4.5, `Diffuse Color` from 5.0**, so the rung reads the manifest's Blender version to know which name the file carries; the latest image last, where the promise gives way rather than breaking. An empty file is never a candidate; the last frame asked for that is present is not a candidate on an interrupted job; a video destination is never a candidate (B20).

**P10. Inside a multi-layer file the preview reads the Combined of the file's first view layer.** B19 picks by name — Combined under the view layer it came from — and where several layers put several Combineds in one file, the first view layer is the pick, matching what the same job written as ordinary images would have shown, which holds the first view layer alone. The spec's own pending marker on this case names Design as the other possible settler; this is the architecture's answer and Design may restyle its presentation, not its pick — a pick recomputed or restyled per opening is what B15 forbids. The second choice's unestablished purpose — no research ever looked at a separate pass for what a failed texture leaves — costs the fallback's value and nothing else ([Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 9).

- **That pick is now the frame's main copy, not its only one. Added 2026-10-10, from the Creator's seventh notes, note 4; no auditor has seen it.** The pick above, and B26 step 1's choice of layer, stand unchanged and are made once (B15); P1 adds a copy beside it for each other View Layer the file holds at that frame. ~~**The main copy is what everything outside the preview's layer choice reads**: the live fetch (P2), the finished-job notification's picture (T15), and `Telegram`'s *send a frame* (its I2, I4) — none of them ever reads another layer's copy, so nothing there changes.~~ **`Telegram`'s frame pictures and its *send a frame* read every copy of the frame**, in their place in the frame's set (P1 and P4: the main copy first, then the frame's other layers in the set's order), each with its own name (P10a). **The live fetch (P2) and the finished-job notification's picture (T15) still read the main copy alone**~~**, and so does the contact sheet (`Telegram` M2)**~~**. The contact sheet reads every copy of its chosen frames, one album per View Layer (`Telegram` M2).** *(Changed 2026-10-31, Creator-stated, the seventeenth notes, note 10, and their yes of 2026-10-30. No auditor has seen it.)* *Changed 2026-10-18, Creator-stated, the fourteenth notes, note 7: "have Telegram send all view layer previews with their names." The struck sentence's `Telegram` half was the plan writer's choice of 2026-10-10, not the Creator's ruling; its other half stands in the sentence before this note. No auditor has seen it.* **On the desktop the preview opens on the main copy**, and where the user has picked a View Layer, a frame holding that layer's copy shows it and a frame not holding it shows the main copy and says the picked layer did not render there; the pick is kept for the entry and comes back on a frame that holds it (the Creator's ruling, 2026-10-10). **The ruling's *first layer* is the main copy's layer**: B26 step 1 already picks the first layer that feeds the destination and renders at the frame, and the regular output path — the only multi-layer source B26 makes copies from — is fed by every layer the file switches on, so no second notion of *first* is introduced.

**P10a. The heading over the preview names the view layer the picture came from, read off the derivative's own record, and names the frame alone where none is carried. Added 2026-08-25, per Row 496 — the entry half; the machine half is `Job files and outputs` B31a's.**

- **The name is B31a's**: recorded on the machine with each derivative's production, only where the file holds more than one view layer, established only where the picture's choosing names a view layer, and carried as the file's own bytes. It arrives by the routes the derivative already travels — journalled with the production event P3 triggers on, held beside the fetched copy on the job record (P4), carried by step 4's fill-in where a connection learns the job late — no new route and no third one.
- **Where the copy carries no name, the heading names the frame alone** — a single-view-layer file, a picture whose choosing establishes no layer (B31a), or a derivative produced before the rule — absent drawing as absent (N13's rule). Nothing composes a name from anything else, and nothing reads a second record to supply one.
- **The heading's name and the progress line's are two facts and are never merged.** R10 and B3a name the view layer being rendered **now**; this names the layer in the picture; after the first layer the two differ on one screen, which is the design's own reason for the promise. Nothing recomputes either: the pick is B15's, made once, and Design may restyle the heading while the name it draws stays the record's (P10).
- Wording and placement — *Frame 147 · Reflections* — are Design's.
- **Added 2026-10-10, from the Creator's seventh notes, note 4; no auditor has seen it.** Every copy carries its own name by the same rule, and **the name is now drawn by the View Layer dropdown rather than the heading**: the dropdown lists the names of the copies held for the frame shown, in their place in the frame's set (P4), with the copy shown in bold; it is absent where the frame holds one copy. The frame number sits between the frame arrows, and the heading reads *Frame preview* — Design's wording, the drawing's. The two facts stay apart exactly as above: the progress line names the layer rendering now, the dropdown the layer in the picture.

## 16. Declarations — atomic or partial, arbitration, and the one termination contract

**Declared per action against [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#9-the-atomic-or-partial-boundary) §9, with the test that decided each. Nothing below is decided at run time.**

| Action | Partition unit | Test | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| **writing a job's queue record and its per-job note at submission** | the pair | §9.3 — the note is unsafe as an absence, because N2 gives it no second creation moment, so a job record with no note loses Rows 76 and 83 silently and A7's adopted-job reading finds a job with no note | **atomic** — the two are staged and brought into being by a single publication step in the shape `Job files and outputs` M5 already uses, never by ordering and never by care. The note is **not** folded into the queue record's store: §1.2's inventory names them separately and it is a closed document's. §9.6 applies — the unit is written as atomic and this document says so — and the filesystem ground is `Job files and outputs` [Section 14](Job%20files%20and%20outputs.md#14-unresolved) item 1, cited and not restated (Q3, N1) |
| **cancel of one waiting or held job** | the job's leaving the line | §9.2 test 1 — the promise is about the job, and nothing smaller | **atomic** — one durable removal of the job record (L1); the material cleanup is `Job files and outputs`' declared partial action, sequenced after, and can restore nothing |
| **reorder of one job** | one job record's key | §9.2 test 1 | **partial across records, atomic within each rewrite, made whole by a durable record of the act** — L24's record carries the whole intended key assignment and is written before the first key moves; each record's own rewrite is one durable write; the record is cleared only when the last moved key is written, and §9 finishes an outstanding one before the admission point may start anything (L6, L24, D14). *(This read **atomic** — one durable rewrite of sequence keys — until 2026-09-05, when a code round found it unbuildable rather than merely unbuilt. **The move spans several job records**, this store keeps one file per record (Q3, Q6), and [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#9-the-atomic-or-partial-boundary) §9.6 P8 settles the ground: the two-file half of Fact 78 is answered and negative — no single filesystem operation updates two independent files atomically. The old reading is kept here so a later reader does not restore it.)* |
| **group block move** | one member's key | §9.3 — a half-moved group could render in a mixed order, so the block's intended order is one unit; **what makes it one is the record rather than the write** | **partial across records, atomic within each rewrite, made whole by a durable record of the act** — the same L24 record, carrying the whole intended key assignment including the gathered block's, written before the first key moves (L7, L24, D14); a member no longer waiting is named in the outcome as not moved. *(This read **atomic** — one durable write — until 2026-09-05, and it was wrong for the reason the row above gives: the block is several job records and this store is one file per record.)* |
| **stop-and-clear** | the ordered pair | §9.3 — the stop is unsafe unless the clear succeeded | **one unit across the pair**: clear first, one durable write emptying the line (L14); where it fails the stop is **not attempted**, naming the clear (L16). The desktop-side outcome record (§9.4) holds both halves' outcomes durably before the first is attempted (L17) |
| **the clear inside it** | one covered record's removal | §9.2 test 2 — a half-cleared line read by the admission point is the failure the order exists to prevent, and **what shuts that window is L23's record rather than one write** | **partial across records, atomic within each removal, made whole by a durable record of the act** — L23's record names every identity the clear covers and is written before the first removal; each identity's outcome is written as it becomes true; the record is cleared only when the last covered record is gone, and §9 finishes an outstanding one before the admission point may start anything (L14, L15, L23, D14). *(This read **atomic** — every covered record removed in one write, or none — until 2026-09-05, when a code round found it unbuildable rather than merely unbuilt. **The clear spans every covered job record**, this store keeps one file per record — Q3 makes the record's existence the fact, Q6 puts untracked records in the same store, and [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 5 forbids reading a file's size as state — and that document's §9.6 P8 settles that no single filesystem operation updates two independent files atomically. The old reading is kept here so a later reader does not restore it.)* |
| **deleting a history entry** | record-plus-tombstone | §9.3 — a record gone with no tombstone is a deletion a later connection undoes | **atomic locally** (X1) |
| **the owed machine-side erase** | one material kind | §9.2 test 1 — the promise names what the erase reaches, per kind | **partial across the enumerated kinds, atomic within each removal**; the tombstone's flag set only on all units done (X3). **The §9.4 outcome record sits in the local record**, its units X2's enumerated kinds — the hold record among them — durable before the first is attempted, each carrying done, failed or not attempted; an already-absent unit is *done*; the remainder is a new action composed from this record (§9.5) |
| **adoption accept** | the whole offer | §9.2 test 1 — the offer is one per machine, all or nothing | **atomic entire** — every record entered in one local write (A6) |
| **applying catch-up events** | one event | §4 E3–E4 — each applied idempotently, cursor advanced after each application is durable | **partial across events**; the notice's composition is part of each application (C2), and a gap is stated (C4). **The §9.4 outcome record is short and is stated rather than left to the builder**: its units are the events the pass reads forward over, enumerated from the cursor before the first is applied, each carrying its own outcome, the action's own outcome derived from them |
| **fetching preview derivatives** | one derivative | §9.2 test 1, and the transfer contract makes each atomic | **partial across, atomic within** (P4). **The §9.4 outcome record is short too**: its units are the derivatives the pass learned of, enumerated before the first fetch is attempted, each carrying its own outcome, the action's own outcome derived from them |
| **writing the idle-spoken mark** | the mark | §9.2 test 1 | **atomic** — one small write (W5) |
| **clearing history — one machine's (X6) or the whole of it (X6a)** | one entry's record-plus-tombstone | §9.2 test 1 — each entry's deletion is X1's declared unit, and neither act promises anything smaller than an entry or anything that spans two of them | **partial across entries — and, for the whole of history, across machines — atomic within each** (X1); an interrupted clear leaves the rest standing and drawn, the act the user's to take again, and each deleted entry's machine half is its own recorded debt (X4) |

**Arbitration (Rented-server runtime A1): every operation this document owns on the machine is a command that starts no worker.** A cancel, a clear, a reorder, a block move, an answer, an erase — each is a command record on the supervisor's path (C4, §5.1), takes no contended resource, and needs no resource class, priority or cancellability declaration. What this document does own at the arbitration point is **A9's ordering**: what waits behind the exclusive render is ordered by the machine-local queue's sequence keys and by nothing else (Q4). The workers this document's rows lean on are others' kinds — the render (`Rendering`'s), the preview (`Job files and outputs`' B24 declarations) — and this document adds no kind of its own.

**The preview kind's termination contract, stated because this document is what starts that kind running (P1–P2) and no document has stated one.** Per X11's four things: the termination is delivered to the **recorded external process** — the Blender the worker drives — at **any moment**, cancellation being B24's own declaration; the worker reaps it and exits, so **the worker outlives every member of its group**, which is what keeps X1's leader-only recording and X9's leader-gone-means-group-gone sound for this kind; the cleanup owed is the sweep of the derivative's staging path, so a killed production never publishes a partial (B29 publishes by rename, and an unpublished staging is **`Server protocol and transfers` S28**-shape swept material); and the displaced operation's outcome is its own — cancelled — never a failure of the work that displaced it (A6). **This is a contract for a kind whose declarations `Job files and outputs` owns, and that document is closed: the contract is recorded here and handed to the runner as a seam** ([Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 14).

***S28* used to stand there with no document in front of it, and the paragraph around it made that the worst possible place for a bare marker. Corrected 2026-09-04, raised by the builder writing this document's rules half at Build step 7.** Every other marker in this paragraph and the one above it is `Rented-server runtime`'s — X11, X1, X9, A6, A1 — so a reader reached *S28* and looked for it there, and **`Rented-server runtime` defines no S28 at all**: its own staging sweep is G20, which itself says *"It is the same shape as `Server protocol and transfers` S28"*. The marker meant is that one — *"Abandoned staging is removed by a record-driven sweep in each direction"* — and no other document's S28 says anything about staging (`Security and licence`'s is about a locked secret store). **A marker with nothing at the other end of it reads exactly like a working citation**, which is why this is a repair and not a tidy-up; [Conventions](../Groundwork/Conventions.md)' rule that a marker is never quoted without naming its document is what was broken. **Nothing this paragraph decides moves**: it was, and is, a *same-shape* reference and never a claim that a preview derivative's staging rides the transfer sweep itself.

---

## 17. What this document supplies, and to which document

**To `Rendering` — the queue's half of six of its rows, written so its half is a citation.** The job identity's composition and issue moment (Y1–Y3), issued before the submission is sent, with the provisional record and the ask-the-machine resolution behind its dropped-submission matching. That a job is in the line exactly when its job record exists, written after the material's publication (Q3) — its Row 2's queue-side half. The admission rule and the ordering behind the exclusive render (Q4). The stop's queue-side: *left stopped* against the kind's own ending (L10–L11), and that anything waiting on a stop needs its own deadline (L12). The dropped-connection rule's queue half: no clause of Section 9 fires for a lost connection (E6) — its Row 19's other side. The carry-on figure's source and fallback: N6's last-frame figure, computed at the ending, read against the machine per its Row 20, not offered where no ending was learned (N7). The re-run's frame-set form: an explicit integer list, Blender's skipping never used (D6–D7) — what its launch contract must carry. The ending classification it must define for the render kind, bounded by E2's two fixed rules. **Five observations its worker contract must take at the worker's own copy loop and carry** *(the fourth added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R25; `Rendering` K13 carries the matching bullet as of the same date, the routed clauses' item 9. **The fifth added 2026-09-02 with E13a, out of the spec's colour-management ruling; `Rendering` K13 carries the matching bullet as of the same date** — corrected later on 2026-09-02, this having read that K13 still lists four and owes the bullet, which stopped being true the same day it was written.)*: the live readings and their bounded reporting (R1, R5–R6), whose re-establishment across a supervisor restart is `Rented-server runtime` W12's route and not a contract of its own, so the worker's half is the re-attempt and nothing more (R5); the ending-step completeness reading carried with the outcome, for Row 54's predicate and N6's figure (H7, N6, U30); the frame's save announcement observed in passing, with the newest-saved figure reported on the same bounded reporting — P1a's preview trigger (P1a); the device line observed in passing on the version's own stream (E13) — its Rows 10 and 11 fix the ask and the route; and **the colour-substitution line observed in passing on either stream, both being captured always (E13a, `Rendering` L7)** — no ask and no switch is needed for it, so nothing of that document's launch contract moves, and the observation is recorded in the attempt record like the device line's. **This fifth observation stands unbuilt pending a recorded line shape, and permanently rather than pending a round — added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it.** The printed text of the three lines is on record in no version and the ruling of 2026-08-31 closes the route that would establish it, so there is no match for the worker to make ([Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 27 as corrected, E13a's own closing bullet). **The other four are owed exactly as before and none of them moves.** **The requirement behind the second is wider than the worker path and is stated in full at H7**: every recorded outcome that ends a job carries a completeness reading, taken as a step of that ending, whoever writes the outcome — so an outcome written by reconciliation with no worker alive carries one produced by the same C1/C7 reckoning, run on the machine before the outcome is written. `Rendering`'s worker contract owes the worker half; the reconciliation half is owned by `Rented-server runtime` [3.3](Rented-server%20runtime.md#33-reconciliation-is-the-supervisors-and-it-runs-at-more-moments-than-a-start) S11 to S13, and [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 17 records the close. And the history setup its submit-again and carry-on actions read: the entry's kept setup, version included, surviving the machine (N10–N11, C11).

**To `Servers` — the readings and the two records its rows lean on.** The queue reading's shape, moment-stamped and period-held, taken only after the installation identity confirms (A1); that a disconnected server's remembered-job warning reads the period-held record and that no warning appears where Smoozy holds none (C7) — its Row 42's stop-and-clear reads L13–L17's two ordered halves and three distinguishable results; its Row 43's untracked-work promise reads A2 and A5's offer; its Row 28's *the work is not remembered* is C6's rule, kept from this side — the queue reading is period-held and nothing durable copies it. The card capacity is its server list's; the live usage is this document's (G1–G2). **And that a history entry holds the server's label and badge hue as copies taken at submission** ([N12](#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in)), so `Servers` 6.1 D5's *history records are keyed by the job identity and never by the server row* is now true of the drawing as well as of the keying — nothing reads a server row to draw a history line, and a removal takes neither value. Added 2026-08-21 for Row 320.

**To `File Browser` and `Server Setup` — live-job use.** A job is **live** from its record's publication until its recorded outcome is written, held jobs included. The live set per machine is the job records with state *waiting*, *held* or *rendering* (Q2); what a live job depends on is the manifest's `.blend` and the per-job resolved set `Dependency Upload & Remap` K9 fixes, its remap material named inside it — read for `File Browser`'s disturb warnings, with the disturbance marks V6 and V13 carried onto the entry as fields, their wording Design's. For `Server Setup`: **which Blender a live job was submitted with is the picked build's path and its answered version together, and the copy its removal warning reads is the machine's** — each live job's manifest, reached through that job's queue record (Q2), never the desktop's copy, which a live job the local record does not name has none of. Corrected 2026-08-23 with N19, per Row 448; this named the version alone, and a version does not identify a build (`Server Setup` B13). **And what becomes of a waiting job whose Blender was removed is answered here and asks nothing of `Server Setup` beyond the act it already performs — added 2026-08-23, per Row 447**: this document's supervisor marks the live jobs `Server Setup` B14's own predicate covers, in the same act in which that removal's recorded outcome is written, and the drop fires at the admission point ([4](#4-acting-on-the-line) L21, L22). `Server Setup` §12's *writes nothing of its own into any record that document defines* stands, and no clause here reads its search. And the entry's disturbed-file and simplify marks persist into history unchanged — the disturbed-file mark's own field, its write and its lifetime are N21's. **For `Server Setup` R1b's swap alone, a job held with the reason *the driver* is left out of the live set** — it is the job the swap is for — and it stays in the set for every other use here, the removal warning included ([H12](#3-held-jobs); `Server Setup` R1c). *Added 2026-10-11, the Creator's ruling that a job needing the newer driver is held back.*

**To `Download` — the transfer triggers.** A job's render-start and its ending, each journalled on the machine and each carried to `Download` when this document learns it — live, or at step 3 — with the ending named as one of the four; what happened while Smoozy was away, handed over once per connection from the same applied events as the catch-up notice (C1); a re-run is the same job told once, its attempts distinguishable by attempt number where `Download` needs them. The count it measures arrivals against, the one folder to watch, and the video shape are `Job files and outputs`' supplies, not restated.

**To `Telegram` — reporting state.** Everything a phone says about a job is read from the same records this document defines: the journal's events (U26–U27) for endings, drops, holds and catch-up; the per-job note for label, times and endings (N1); the machine-side derivative set for pictures (P1, B22). The waiting-job answer is the one release path and the tap is `Telegram`'s (U4); the loser-is-told rule and the no-conversion rule (H4) hold for a phone exactly as for the desk. `Telegram` adds nothing of its own to any record here.

**To `Desktop app and delivery` — what its four notification kinds and its presentations read.** The idle reading and its half hour (W1–W5) for T14; the catch-up notice record and the step-3 boundary it already owns (C1–C2, T18); the job-ending records its finished and failed kinds read, group identity on the members and no group store (L18, T11); the last-known entry's stamp and not-updating statement (C7); and the four collection outcomes' entry-side standing (E4), presented per its 16.3.

**To `Reporting a problem` — the acts this document writes to the diary, and the machine endings it writes as told lines. Added 2026-08-31, out of that document's [11](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#11-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) requirement on every feature document.** This is a change to a closed, audited document made outside a round: no auditor has seen it, and Rule 7's line in the decisions record says so. J2a's three tests are applied below to every act this document performs and J4a's bound to every machine ending it consumes; **the list is fixed**, and an act absent from it is one this document does not write rather than one nobody got to.

**Three acts, and each is a write of the desktop's own rather than a reading come back.** *(Two until 2026-09-02, when the deletion's omission was re-examined and its ground found false — see the correction below.)*

- **Untracked work taken over** ([13](#13-reading-a-machine-and-adoption) A6). Smoozy's own act — the local record's one durable write, composed from A5's offer. The spec names its completion as work that takes its machine's line and *"from then on it can be seen, reordered and stopped, and it lands in history when it is over"* — rows on a screen and later in history, not a step inside something else. And the offer is one per machine, all or nothing (A5, A6), so the line runs once per acceptance and never once per adopted job. **The fixed line form:** the act's name; the moment on the desktop clock, stated as that clock (§3 D5); the server's label as N12's copy carries it; and the count of jobs the acceptance covered. **The identities it carries:** the run identity (`Reporting a problem` U7) and the server row. **What it does not carry:** the adopted jobs' identities or labels — one line per act is the third test, and each job's own entry is where it is met (A7); nothing of the reading the offer was composed from (A1), which is period-held and not the act; and no line for a decline, which writes nothing durable and is not an act on the work (A5).
- **A machine's jobs stopped and cleared** ([4](#4-acting-on-the-line) L13–L17), and L19's group-scoped form of the same request, which is one command and therefore one line. Smoozy's own act — the clear and the stop composed on the desktop as two commands with their own operation identities, marked as one ordered pair. The spec names its completion by what is left behind: the job that was rendering *"goes into history, marked as stopped"*, and `Servers` reports what worked. And it runs once per request, never once per cleared job. **The fixed line form:** the act's name; the moment on the desktop clock (§3 D5); the server's label; and which of L17's three results was recorded — could not begin at all; cleared and stopped; cleared and the stop failed or expired its waiter. **The identities it carries:** the run identity (`Reporting a problem` U7); the server row; the clear's and the stop's operation identities; and, where the group-scoped form was the act, the group identity (L18). **What it does not carry:** the cleared jobs' identities or labels (see the three subjects below); the stop's resolved subject beyond the stop's own operation identity (L13); and no second line for the stopped job's ending, which is the told line below.
- **A deletion — one entry, a machine's history, or the whole of history** ([14](#14-deletion) X1, X6, X6a). **Added 2026-09-02; this was one of the three subjects the paragraph below used to leave out, and it is in on the tests the other two still fail.** Smoozy's own act — the local record's removal and the tombstone written as one atomic unit (X1), composed on the desktop and unconditional, reading no server row and needing no machine (X4, X6a). The spec names its completion by what is left: nothing anywhere in Smoozy showing those jobs were submitted, the fill-in refused and the adoption offer closed (X5, A5). And it is **one line per user act**, never one per deleted entry — X6 and X6a are one act over a set, partial across entries and atomic within each, and the count is what the line carries. **The fixed line form:** the act's name; which of the three scopes it was — one entry, a machine's history, the whole of history; the moment on the desktop clock, stated as that clock (§3 D5); the count of entries the act covered; and, on the per-machine scope, the server's label as N12's copy carries it. **The identities it carries:** the run identity (`Reporting a problem` U7), and the server row on the per-machine scope alone. **What it does not carry, and this is the whole of why the line is writable at all:** no deleted job's identity, no deleted job's label, and nothing of the tombstones — so **the line names no job and shows no job**, which is what the deletion promise is about. Nor the erase's own units or outcomes (X2, X3): the erase is a machine-side recorded debt run at a later connection, per entry, and a line for it would be one per job by construction — it stays undeclared and the paragraph below says what that costs.

**One told line (J4a): a job's ending as the machine recorded it.** E1 and E2 decide the ending from records alone, and the desktop learns it live or at §7 step 3 — **one line per attempt that ended**, never one per journal event, per frame or per reading. **The fixed line form:** the ending in E2's own four words — *finished*, *failed*, *stopped*, *given up* — and a bounded rendering of the job's label (`Job files and outputs` N3). **The identities it carries:** J4's four, unaltered — the machine's own moment on the machine's clock, the per-machine journal sequence number the outcome arrived at (`State, identity and recovery` E1), the installation identity it was read under, and the server row — with the job identity beside them and, on a re-run, the attempt number (E11). **What it does not carry:** anything either stream said, which is kept whole on the record and never mined (E7, E10); the completeness statement and the last-frame figure (`Job files and outputs` C27, N6), which stand on the entry; the four collection outcomes (E4); the device line (E12); **the job's device ask (E12a), which is a field of the entry written at submission and never an act or an ending — added 2026-09-02 with that clause**; and no told line for a reading of any kind.

**What else was tested, and the test each fails.**

- **A reorder, and a group block move** (L6, L7) — the second test. Nothing of Smoozy's own stands afterwards: what the user sees is A1's period-held reading of the machine's own line redrawn, and the spec names no completion beyond that.
- **A stop of one rendering job** (L10) — not this document's act to write: the stop is `Rendering`'s to send, and what this document holds is the recorded outcome coming back, which is the told line above and not a second act.
- **Answering a job held on the user** (H4) — not this document's act either: the one hold mechanism and its single command path are `Rented-server runtime` §8's and the tap is `Telegram`'s or the desk's, and the completion is the job starting, which is `Rendering`'s.
- **The preview set's production and its fetch** (P1, P4) — the third test, twice over: one derivative per finished frame, one fetch per derivative.
- **The queue reading and the live commentary read** (A1, E8) — the first test: both are readings and neither is Smoozy's own act. Neither is a told line either: both recur on `Desktop app and delivery` [3.3](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) P10d's live-reading sequence, which is the screen's rate rather than the user's.
- **The machine's history copy, the per-job note and the manifest taken at §7 step 4's fill-in** (A1, N1, A7) — the first test, for the same reason.
- **The idle reading and the catch-up notice** (W1, C1) — the first test: each is a composition over applied events whose telling is `Desktop app and delivery`'s notification, and J4a's own rule is that no journal event becomes a told line by itself.
- ***Show outputs*, *show a job's downloaded files*, and the auto-download switch reached from an entry** (L1a, [17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)) — not this document's acts at all: the entry is only where they sit, and they are `File Browser`'s and `Download`'s to declare.

**Two subjects are left out on a ground the three tests do not contain, named here rather than silently omitted. This paragraph named three and its ground was false; corrected 2026-09-02, and it is the ground that was wrong rather than only the count.** A cancel (L1, L5) and the drop of a waiting job whose Blender was removed (L22) are each an act or an ending whose promise is that **afterwards nothing anywhere in Smoozy shows that job was ever submitted**, Creator-stated in the spec and rested on here at X4 and X6a, where §10.1's tombstone is admissible precisely **because it meets no user surface**.

**What the old ground said, and why it is false.** It read: *"A diary line naming any of the three would be that showing, on a surface the user reads and may send onward."* **The user never meets the diary** — `Reporting a problem` [R7](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#1-the-diary): *"No screen shows it, no setting governs it, no control clears it"* — so the first half names a surface that does not exist, and the ground as written applied a user-surface test to the one record in Smoozy that is not a user surface. **What is true is the second half alone**: the diary leaves Smoozy inside a report the user composes and sends, which is the only reason it is written at all. So the question is what a report would show, and it is answered per subject rather than for a set of three.

**The deletion is in, and it is in because omitting it bought nothing.** A deleted job's own told line — its ending in E2's four words with a bounded rendering of its label — was written into the diary when the machine recorded that ending, and **the deletion neither reaches it nor could**: the diary is append-only and its lifetime is the installation's (R7), no act of this document's clears it, and X2's erase enumerates machine-side record material and never a desktop diary. **So a report cut after a deletion already names the job.** Withholding the deletion line therefore removed no showing whatever; it removed only the explanation of why the history it names is empty, which is the exact reading a report exists to give. And the line as declared above carries no job identity and no label, so it adds no job to a report that did not already have one. **Decision changed, not merely re-grounded** — it is the third act above.

**The other two stay out, and now on a ground that holds.** A cancel and a Blender-removal drop are both of a job that **never rendered**, so neither has an ending told line and neither is named anywhere else in the diary. Both are per job rather than per act, so a line for either would be one line per job by the third test's own arithmetic, and a line for a job with no other trace **is** the showing the promise forbids — in a report, where the diary actually goes. That is a real ground, and unlike the old one it distinguishes the cases instead of sweeping them together.

**J2a's three tests weigh the act, and J4a's bound weighs the ending; neither weighs what the subject's own spec promises may survive it**, so neither decides the two that stay out, and no fourth test is invented here. **What the omission costs is stated rather than hidden:** the diary is silent about the two acts that remove a job with no other trace, and silent about the erase's own machine-side units, so a report cut after a cancel or a drop shows the line as it is and nothing about what left it. Reported to the runner as a hole in J2a rather than repaired here — narrowed from three subjects to two, and the deletion no longer among them.

**To the architecture runner, as seams rather than supplies:** [Section 18](#18-unresolved) items 22 and 25. *(Item 26 was named here until 2026-08-31, when the runner closed it as spent — Row 58's pointer had been corrected at the same re-gate that filed it.)* **Re-derived from Section 18 on 2026-08-29 at the re-gate.** This line named items 12, 13, 14, 16 and 17, every one of which is now CLOSED — 12, 13, 14 and 16 at the Architecture seam close of 2026-08-22 and 17 on 2026-08-21 — so it handed over five spent seams and named none of the three that stand. Item 24 was standing when the re-gate ran and closed in this same pass.

---

## 18. Unresolved

Each entry names what is not established, what breaks, and what holds without it. **None of them stopped this document**; [Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records what was checked and declined.

1. **Fact 1's disk-full clause — the arrangement does not run on a full state mount, and on a systemd machine does not recover by itself.** Already ruled against all three escalations by `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 12 and §17's tenth check; not re-escalated. **Breaks, here:** while it lasts, no queue act applies, no note write lands, no ending is journalled and no idle or catch-up reading advances — Rows 47, 70, 71, 75, 76 and 83 all close *with this clause named*: the line and every read of it stay truthful (Fact 40's disk-full half), every refused write refuses loudly, held jobs stay held, and the repair is `Server Setup`'s and `Desktop app and delivery`'s trigger, reached through the supervisor-unavailable condition the desktop already presents. **What holds:** everything read-side, and the desktop half entire, which shares no store with the machine. **The running-supervisor case left this item on 2026-08-22 (R10)**: what a supervisor that lived through the fill does — answering throughout, refusing loudly, holding what it could not write pending until it lands — is `Rented-server runtime` S14's, and the sentences above describe the arrangement that is down. **That half is no longer unobserved. Corrected 2026-09-02:** this sentence cited `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 25 as the unobserved half, and that item has read **ANSWERED 2026-08-25** since the Pile 3 rental — a live supervisor arrangement **does** survive its state mount filling under it on the container kind, [The Pile 3 batch](The%20Pile%203%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20a%20full%20mount%20under%20a%20running%20supervisor,%20an%20interrupted%20heavy%20frame,%20and%20an%20hour%20of%20silence.md) §2 being the answer of record, with S14 standing as written. So this item is the arrangement that is **down** and nothing else, and the running-supervisor case is answered rather than owed anywhere.
2. **Fact 42 — CLOSED 2026-08-21 for Vast.ai, and the spec's sentence is corrected.** **CLOSED 2026-08-21 by reading Vast.ai's own billing page** — *"You are charged the base active rental cost for every second your instance is in the active/connected state"*, at a rate quoted in $/hr (read 2026-08-20). The rate is hourly, the charge is per second. Settles Vast.ai only; the blanket form is what is refuted, and the spec's sentence is corrected. Nothing about the feature moved. **What is still open** is the same question for Sesterce and Hyperstack, both rented from and neither read — one page each, no machine. **This item is NOT closed by the ruling of 2026-08-31 and must not be given the permanence line its neighbours carry — noted 2026-09-02**: it wants two published billing pages read, which is neither a rental nor a Blender research round, and a reading is still a permitted route. **Breaks:** nothing; the one sentence it did break is fixed, and nothing of W1–W5 moved.
3. **Fact 44 — what losing power outright leaves.** Not posable today, and the register names the one route that would settle it as the Creator's own act *(reworded 2026-08-29 with D8; this read "Unposable on any machine this project can reach", against the register's correction of 2026-08-22)*; the nearest case, a kernel reset, left no frame's contents at all and differing surviving directory entries across two runs. **This item is NOT closed by the ruling of 2026-08-31 and carries no permanence line — noted 2026-09-02**: the one remaining route is a guest machine killed at a hypervisor on the Creator's own equipment, which is neither a rental taken to answer a question nor a Blender research round, so it is unscheduled rather than permanently unresolved and must not be written off alongside its neighbours. **What is now recorded beside it is the occasion**: a provider-initiated forcible reboot is the ordinary way a machine goes down under this arrangement rather than a corner case, and the sources file carries it against Rows 70, 71 and 73. **Breaks, and it is narrower than this item read — corrected 2026-09-02:** the already-there test can count a short-but-not-empty file a power loss left (C15's limit), so a re-run can leave a hole it cannot see — **at a frame anywhere but the boundary.** The boundary frame is re-rendered whatever it looks like: `Job files and outputs` C17 re-renders the last frame asked for on the machine's own attempt, and since 2026-09-02 `Rendering` A4 opens a carry-on's range **at** that frame rather than after it, so the one frame most likely to be short is the one frame neither route skips. This item read as though the boundary frame were the case, which was the sharper-sounding half and the one already covered. And §6's records-on-disk assumption is bounded, not proved. **What holds:** the zero-byte rule, the last-frame-re-rendered rule, the re-run-once bound, §6's prohibition on trusting any record newer than what can be read, and the spec's own fallback written into the promise — render again rather than vouch.
4. **Fact J — a whole job's renderlet total where the view-layer switch is driven or changed by a script. PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** Left the stage for Blender Research, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that **there will be no further Blender research** — so it is not parked and no round is coming; nothing below may be written as though an answer were on its way. The keyframed half is answered in all four versions on both machines. **Breaks:** the plan total for exactly that file shape, and — the sharper half — the trigger that would route such a file to the spec's fallback, since detecting the shape is part of the unanswered question. Until answered, such a job's second bar can carry a percentage of a wrong total, bounded by B5's plan-not-promise presentation. **What holds:** the bars' survival clauses, the fallback's wording, and every other file shape.
5. **Fact 41's remainder — the widened erase has never been observed to complete. PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** Watching the enumeration complete wants a rented machine to remove a job from, and there will be **no further rental to answer a question** (2026-08-31). One removal per box, not repeated; the observed removal left the captured streams, a rendered frame and the launch material readable under the removed identity. X2–X3 enumerate the erase and gate the tombstone's flag on every unit; that the enumeration completes on a real machine is untested. **Breaks:** record-material bytes surviving a deletion on a machine the user pays for, with the machine reporting nothing — the exact observed shape — wherever the erase under-reaches and its outcome fails to say so. **What holds:** the local half unconditionally — the entry is gone, the fill-in refused, the debt recorded and retried.
6. **Fact 6's survival half — unattended housekeeping over days, and more than one physical disk.** **The more-than-one-physical-disk half is ANSWERED, 2026-08-22, and only the housekeeping half is still owed — corrected in place.** One seller ships two real block devices as standard, `/dev/vda1` at 97 GB on `/` and `/dev/vdb` at 787 GB on `/ephemeral`, so a rental's second filesystem can be a genuine second device rather than a quota on the same disk; `Job files and outputs` L4 and its §14 item 6 carry it. **What replaces it here is narrower, and it is PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02:** nothing was tested about whether that large mount survives a stop, a start or a restart, and its name is the seller's claim rather than an observation — **and watching a mount over days is a rental left running, which the ruling of 2026-08-31 closes.** No seller's page settles it either, the claim being about what a particular rental does rather than what is published. **Breaks:** a job folder or record directory vanishing between connections; the entry then reads absent paths in the completeness statement and a job short of its count — visible, not silent — but the promise that outputs sit waiting to be downloaded rests on survival nobody has watched for longer than a stop-and-start. **What holds:** nothing reads presence as state, so nothing is silently wrong; the choice half is answered and inherited (Y6).
7. **Fact 43's remainder — two renders on two cards, one render spanning both, cross-login visibility in the compute-apps list. PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02** — it wants a rental with two cards and two renders on it, and there will be no further rental to answer a question (2026-08-31). **Breaks:** nothing shown, because the entry's figure is card-level and attribution is never claimed (G1, G5); named so nobody narrows the reading to the render on the strength of the process figure.
8. **The threshold at which a picture is split into tiles, and it is all that is left of this item. Rewritten 2026-08-31, out of the repaired Blender research.** The item read *every progress line on record came from an untiled picture; every tiled run was single-sample*, and **both are false**: thirteen tiled lines are on record in full (R7), and the sample number restarts inside each tile against the scene's own undivided maximum (R13). B2's unequal-sided case is observed too — one run grew a single side, 2048×2048 to 2048×2049 — and its grid arithmetic reproduces five observed counts where an area rule disagrees at three. **What is still open is which size crosses into tiling, and no threshold is written anywhere in this document. PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02** — it is a question about what Blender does, and the ruling of 2026-08-31 closes both a further research round and a rental to run one, so no threshold is coming and none may be assumed later. **Breaks:** B2's plan tile count for a frame whose real tiling differs from the grid's, which makes the plan total wrong by the difference and is what B5's plan-not-promise presentation already absorbs. **What holds:** every reading, because the condition the readings turn on is read off the printed line and never computed — a line carrying a tile figure is a tiled frame and a line carrying none is not (R12, R13, B4) — so the open threshold costs a plan figure and never a reading.
9. **The diffuse colour pass's purpose was never established** — no separate pass was ever looked at for what a failed texture leaves. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02:** looking at one is a Blender test, and the ruling of 2026-08-31 closes both a research round and a rental to run one. **Breaks:** the second rung's value as a texture check; the first choice carries the promise alone, and the rung order stands as the spec's own ruling (P9–P10).
10. **Fact 92's qualifier — nothing was disciplining the one rented clock this project has watched, and a container has no clock of its own.** **Two words were corrected here on 2026-08-21.** The generalisation was one machine: a single Vast.ai virtual-machine rental, where four readings on the box itself said `NTP=no`, `NTPSynchronized=no`, server `n/a`, **packet count 0**, and the alternative daemon absent. Ubuntu ships a time service whose own manual says it *"will step the system clock for large offsets or slowly adjust it for smaller deltas"* — `systemd-timesyncd.service(8)` as Ubuntu publishes it, read **2026-08-23**, in the same words on both releases this project has rented, 22.04 LTS and 24.04 LTS (addresses in the sources file; label **documented**, and this sentence carried neither address nor date read until 2026-08-23, the fragment reaching the document through [Facts](Facts.md) Fact 92's tightening of 2026-08-21) — so other rentals may well be disciplined; **the honest form is *the one rented clock this project has watched*.** Settling it is free and needs no rental of its own — `timedatectl show` on the next two or three rentals that come up for something else. That clock held a 0.0129 ms band over a 25-minute render. **What that band is a band of matters and was nearly misread:** it is realtime **minus monotonic**, a difference that is insensitive to frequency drift by construction and moves only when the clock is **stepped** — so it says no step occurred in those 25 minutes and says nothing about drift against true time. No drift figure can be recovered from it. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02, and the occasion is widened at the same time.** The free settlement named below is `timedatectl show` on a rental, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that **no further rented machine will be taken to answer a question** — so it is not waiting for a rental that comes up for something else, it will not arrive. *(Build may rent a machine to run the app on; that is a different act and revives nothing here.)* **And the occasion this item names is too narrow — widened 2026-09-02, per the reboot scenario replay's finding 4.** The clause below names **a container host** and nothing else, and **a reboot is the moment a machine's clock is most likely to be stepped, on any of the three machine kinds**: after one, attempt 1's and attempt 2's moments can sit either side of a step while both are *"the machine's own clock"*, which is exactly the condition D5's rule reads as safe. **The remedy the item already declines — a monotonic reading beside each realtime stamp — is still not asked for and is still not taken.** **Breaks:** the durations entries show (N4) — a stepped host clock under a container, **or a clock stepped across a reboot on any machine kind**, states a duration that is not true; the durations are Rows 76's and 381's, and the sources file records that. **The word *undetectably* came off with it**, because it is only true of the arrangement as written: a monotonic reading taken beside each realtime stamp makes a step visible, the two differences disagreeing, and makes the duration itself immune — Linux's own `clock_gettime(2)` says `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` is *"not affected by discontinuous jumps in the system time"*, and the Creator's Mac documents the same clock. **Adding that second field is a decision and is not taken here**: N1 records the two moments as **stamps a user reads** as well as as a difference, and a stamp must be on the realtime clock, so the remedy is a field beside them rather than a replacement of them. **What holds:** every ordering, which is journal sequence numbers and no clock; and D5, which at least keeps the error to one clock's.

18. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 on the desktop half by a Creator ruling: the previews accumulating on the user's own computer now have an age ending, and the unbounded state this item recorded is over.** The item was put to the Creator by the scenario-replay route as item S3 — an overnight batch leaving preview pictures on their own computer with no age limit and no size limit, clearable only by deleting the job record — and **they ruled for an age limit**, over a size cap and over leaving it unbounded. `State, identity and recovery` §11.1 and §11.3 carry the ending, §11.2 the sweep, P4 and P4a this document's half. **The period is thirty days, ANSWERED 2026-09-02, and the ending is buildable.** *(This read* **The period is not set and no agent sets it***, against that document's §13 item 12, which parked it. The Creator delegated every decision that could be delegated on 2026-09-02 and the figure was taken under that delegation: `State, identity and recovery` §11.1 R16 states it and its ground, §11.2 R19–R21 the sweep's comparison and its boundary test, §11.3 R23 the class, and `Preparation/Specs/Queue & History.md` carries the same figure in the user's own terms. Item 12 there is answered rather than parked, and no later reader should take the old sentence as a standing bar on the number.)* **What the figure costs the user, carried rather than dropped:** a picture cleared at thirty days does not come back — the machine that made it is usually gone by then and P4a queues no re-fetch — so an older job shows everything but its preview, while the rendered files themselves are untouched on both sides. **The machine half of this item is untouched by the ruling and stands as written below** — a preview backlog on the machine bounded by the frame count alone, and machine time on a job that outruns the preview worker. **And [Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator)'s declination of the disk half is superseded rather than merely dated**: the check ran there, it declined, and the Creator ruled the other way. The original entry follows, unchanged. **What the dense preview set costs is recorded rather than left to be met.** P1 produces one derivative per finished frame, so a long job leaves the machine a preview backlog bounded by the frame count alone, and the desktop one fetched derivative per finished frame, subject-held on the job record and cleared only at deletion (§11.3) — and §11.1 holds no size class and no age class that could bound either. **Breaks:** nothing promised — the backlog is low priority, cancellable and never blocks a render (P1, B24), and the fetch ranks below every class anybody asked for (P6). What is unbounded is disk on the user's own computer for a job they have not deleted, and machine time on a job that outruns the preview worker. **What holds:** the spec's own promise that the user moves through a job's finished frames, which the sparse reading could not keep and nothing later would have filled; the check is recorded at [Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator).

So the spec's *"both bars count the same thing"* is kept for an untiled frame and **stated rather than kept** for a tiled one. **Breaks:** reading the two bars against each other as one measure on a tiled job. **What holds:** the tile fraction and the plan-unit fraction agree wherever the row's own knowingly-unweighted-tiles assumption holds; neither bar claims a figure it cannot support; and B5's plan-not-promise presentation is unaffected. The mechanism is unchanged — where the tiled line cannot be read at all, B4 already shows no percentage, which is the spec's fallback. **CLOSED 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3c): B4 as corrected that day fills both bars by the same arithmetic, the tiles finished times the samples plus the tile in hand's samples, so *both bars count the same thing* is kept for a tiled frame too.** The first bar is still labelled as tiles. *No auditor has seen this sentence.*
20. **ACCEPTED COST, ruled by the Creator on 2026-08-22, and nothing is built for it: a night that keeps its connection produces no catch-up notice, and that night is the ordinary case rather than an edge.** C1 composes the notice from exactly the events §7 step 3 applies, *"and nothing else ever composes one"*; `Desktop app and delivery` T18 draws the boundary at the desktop's own reconciliation read rather than at the user's absence, and T20 has a reconnection after a drop re-run step 3, which is what makes an outage produce the notice. **So the user who queues jobs overnight and whose connection holds meets no notice in the morning at all.** Each ending posted its own transient operating-system notification to an empty room as it happened — T23: *"A post is therefore attempted and forgotten"* — and nothing survives them; the same night with one minute of connection loss anywhere in it produces the full grouped notice. The spec's own description of the notice's ordinary case is *"the user walked away from a render and came back to ask whether it is done"*, which is this night exactly.

    **The Creator was shown it as a moment — what the user gets and what they would get instead — and ruled: leave it.** Reached by the scenario-replay route as item S2. **So nothing is added and nothing is softened.** No absence-shaped trigger, no second composer, and above all no clock: T19's refusal stands, because *"An age comparison would be arithmetic across two clocks neither of which is trusted."* C1's boundary is unchanged, C2's record is unchanged, and no clause above is edited on the strength of this item — it is here to be met, not to be worked around.

    **What the user actually gets instead.** Every ending is on its entry and was on it as it happened: history holds each job with its ending, its times, how much of it was made where the ending was not a clean finish, what Blender said, and the job's frame preview, all readable the moment the screen is opened and without a connection for what Smoozy already holds. The queue shows what is still running or waiting. Nothing is lost, nothing is wrong, and nothing is silent — **what is missing is the one grouped message that says it in a sentence**, so the user reads the list rather than being told. **Breaks:** no promise. The spec keys the notice on what a connection learns, and a connection that never lost anything learns nothing; every ending it would have named is on an entry. **What holds:** C1 to C5 entire, the catch-up notice for every night that did drop, and the four transient notifications for a user who is at the desk. **And [Section 19](#19-moments-that-reached-the-creator)'s opening line no longer covers this document**: a moment from here did reach the Creator, by that route, and was ruled on.
