23. **The removed-build drop reaches a removal Smoozy performed and no other, and the mark it leaves is never re-checked. Added 2026-08-23 with L21 and L22.** No fact is missing — Fact 45 is what forces the shape: *not found* and *not there* are two readings, so the drop's ground is the recorded act and never a search. **What is not reached is two cases, both loud rather than silent.** A build removed by the user's own hand at the machine, or through the machine's software manager outside Smoozy, leaves a waiting job unmarked: it starts, and `Rendering` L14 fails the attempt before the render instruction, so the user gets a failed entry naming the version instead of a job that left no trace — a different act from the warned removal the spec's paragraph is about. And a build restored at a removed build's path by an act outside Smoozy leaves the mark standing, so a job whose build is present again is dropped; through Smoozy the case cannot arise, `Server Setup` B8 never re-occupying a removed folder. **What holds:** no job is ever dropped on a search's silence, which is the outcome Fact 45 forbids; the jobs the mark covers are exactly the jobs `Server Setup` B14's warning named before the user went ahead; a job that is not dropped fails loudly rather than rendering with a build it was not submitted with (`Rendering` L14); and nothing anywhere reads a file's presence as state (§1.3 rule 5). **One further shortfall, on the telling and not on the drop:** where the journal's cap passes the desktop's cursor before any connection (E5, E6a), the drop's event is gone, the user is never told why that job went, and a local job record stands for a job the queue draws no row for (C10) and history never held (C9); C4 states the gap rather than smoothing it, and nothing is inferred from the machine's silence (D2).

27. **The colour-substitution line's exact printed text, and which stream carries it. Added 2026-09-02 with E13a; PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED on both halves.** What is on record is that **three lines are printed in 4.5 to 5.2**, and what each of the three says in substance; **the exact text of none of them is quoted**, and **which of the two streams carries them was never recorded**. Both are questions about what Blender does, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that there will be **no further Blender research and no further rental to answer a question** — so neither is coming and nothing here may be written as though a round were on its way. **The stream half costs nothing and is closed by construction**: E13a watches both, which the worker already copies, so no answer is needed for the telling to fire. **Breaks, and it is the text half alone. CORRECTED 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen the new text.** This read *"a match built on a line shape nobody wrote down **can miss**, and where it misses the telling does not fire"*, **which describes a partial loss and the loss is total.** The printed text of the three lines is on record in no version and the ruling of 2026-08-31 closes the one route that would establish it, so **no match exists, none can be written, and the telling never fires at all, on every job, permanently.** The code is faithful and right: writing a line shape from memory is the one thing it must refuse, and this crate carries a purpose-built value meaning *on record in no version* so that nothing of that shape is ever written down as fact. So the cost is the spec's own *what breaks without the telling*, in full and for every job — an artist rendering on a machine that quietly used Blender's colour setup in place of theirs with nothing saying so. The silence is total, because absence of the line and absence of a substitution present the same way on the entry, and E13a says outright that absence is not evidence. **E13a and [Section 17](#17-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)'s fifth observation each carry a line saying they stand unbuilt pending a recorded line shape.** **What the spec promised on the strength of the telling is not this document's to rule on**; it is on the Build board as owed to the Creator, and nothing here is built or worked around for it. **What holds:** everything else, and it is more than the telling. The render's ending, the four endings and every reading are untouched — the line is an observation and never a state (R1, E2). **The material is in front of the user either way**: E7 keeps every byte of both streams verbatim and E9 carries them into history, so the three lines are in the record the entry already shows whether or not the match picked them out — the telling is a statement composed over words the user can read for themselves, not the only place the fact exists. And the shape is loud rather than quiet at the source: Blender says it out loud while the render runs, which is the whole reason the spec could close the hole with a telling and no new machinery.

The match 5.3 allows is parked in Open Threads, so the entry's silence is unchanged in every version.

28. **Whether Blender's printed sample count restarts at each view layer on 5.0 and later. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; no auditor has seen it. PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED**, on the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 that there will be no further Blender research and no further rental to answer a question. **It is established nowhere in this project and recorded nowhere** — not above, not in `Preparation/Blender Research/Question register.md`, and not in either joined answer this document may cite — so it is written here because a question carried nowhere is a question nobody can find again. **Never as work that is coming, and never with a guess in its place**: what Blender does with the sample count across view layers is not on record, and no clause anywhere may be written as though it were. **Breaks:** if the count restarts at each layer, B3a's first bar on a multi-layer frame on 5.0 and later fills to one part in the number of that frame's active layers and no further, then jumps to full at B3b's frame ending — on a three-layer frame, a bar sitting at a third for the whole frame. **What holds:** the bar never goes backwards inside a frame, which is what B3a was written to buy; B3b fills it at the frame's ending, so no frame ends with a part-filled bar; the second bar counts frames and is untouched; and on 4.5 the unit and the reading are both the renderlet, so the arrangement is right there whatever the answer. **B3a's arithmetic does not change on the strength of this item**, and the two behaviour changes a code round proposed for it were refused for stating what Blender does. **CLOSED 2026-10-04 (the Creator's second notes, piece 3b), by a controlled test the 2026-08-31 ruling does not reach — Build's own desk run, not a research round.** The count does restart at each view layer: every layer opens with `Sample 0/N` in 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 (two layers over one and two frames, Cycles on the processor and on this Mac's graphics card, the Creator's Mac). Label: **observed in a test**. And 5.0 and later announce each layer (R10's correction), so the boundary is read rather than inferred: R11 starts the reading over at each named layer and B3a's frame unit holds only where no layer is named. The *breaks* above no longer arise where a layer is named. *No auditor has seen this sentence.*

---

## 19. Moments that reached the Creator

**None from this document — and CORRECTED 2026-08-22: two of the things checked below reached the Creator afterwards by the scenario-replay route, and both were ruled on.** This section used to say *"None from this document"* and nothing else, and it was true of what this document sent. What it was never true of is what the document produced. **The two rulings, both Creator-stated 2026-08-22:**

- **The disk half of the dense preview set — ruled the other way.** The declination below covers the connection time and the disk, and the Creator ruled that previews on the user's own computer are cleared by age. The declinations stand as the record of what was checked here; they no longer describe what is built. [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 18 carries the close and names where the ending lives.
- **The catch-up notice's missing morning — ruled *leave it*, and recorded as an accepted cost.** It was never checked here at all, which is the sharper half: this section's checks as they stood at the replay — nine of them — did not include it, and the scenario replay found it. *(Corrected 2026-09-02: this read *this section's nine checks*, present tense, in a section that has carried more than nine since.)* [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 20 carries it, and nothing is built for it.

**And a third, Creator-stated 2026-08-23: Row 447's ending.** It reached them by the runner rather than from a clause written here — the targeted re-sweep of the spec judged the removed-Blender paragraph a change to what the user gets, and the Creator ruled *"drop it, leaving no trace"* over the four other endings. The spec states that ending; L21 and L22 are how it is arranged, and their own check is the last one below.

Eighteen answers were checked against [Method](Method.md)'s three escalations — and none reached one. The checks are recorded because no gate catches the third. *(Sixteen until 2026-09-02, when E13a and G5a were written and their checks added at the end below.)*

**Row 82's residue, checked hardest against the third escalation.** Bytes surviving a deletion on a paid machine, unreported — the strongest candidate in the document. Declined: after X2's enumerated erase, what survives is the job output folder alone, which the spec itself names as staying and names to the user in the delete confirmation; the record-material residue the throwaway exposed is exactly what the erase now removes, and an unfinished erase is a recorded debt, not a silence. The user meets nothing the spec does not tell them about.

**Fact 1's disk-full negative.** Already ruled against all three escalations by a closed document; not re-escalated here (Q8, item 1). The user meets the supervisor-unavailable condition and the disk-full diagnosis they already get.

**Row 65's countdown.** *Nothing is offered* is a licence left unused, not a promise broken and not a cost — the user gets exactly what the spec promises, and one fewer confident wrong number.

**Row 84's preview traffic.** Fetching derivatives spends connection time nobody asked for by name. Declined: the spec grants the copies and the catch-up *"the way an auto-download does"*; the class ranks below everything a user asks for (P6); it is not a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost.

**Row 55's idle-spoken mark.** A durable record the user never sees, keyed to Smoozy's own act; not a cost of any of the three kinds. The alternative — speaking twice after a restart, or never — is the promise broken in one direction or the other.

**Row 50's substituted label.** The folder name differing from the label is the Creator's own ruling of 2026-08-18; showing the real path where paths are shown and the full label everywhere else adds no question, no warning and no new thing to keep track of.

**Row 54's cancelled dependency dropping the waiter.** The user cancels a first-frame job and the job waiting on it goes too, with the reason stated once. That is the spec's own list — *"or it is cancelled before it ever runs"* — and the alternative, a waiter standing for ever on a dependency that will never end, is a thing that can be lost quietly.

**Row 84's dense preview set.** Producing a derivative for every finished frame rather than only the newest spends machine time and the user's own disk on work nobody asked for by name. Declined: what the user meets is the set the spec's own sentence describes — moving through a job's finished frames — and the cost falls on machine time the arbitration mechanism already bounds (low priority, cancellable, displaced by an exclusive render, never blocking one) and on disk under a lifetime that already exists (§11.3, cleared at deletion). The sparse reading is the one with a cost of the third kind: frames the user cannot reach, in a set nothing later fills. Recorded at [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 18.

**L2's *cancelling* state.** L2's ordering — machine first, then the desktop — creates a transient state the spec has no word for, and Design can only style what architecture creates. Declined: the state is a step inside an act the spec already grants, it is no place to look, nothing to type and nothing that can be lost, and the alternative is a desktop reporting a cancel done before the machine's copy is gone. The ordering stands and the presentation is Design's, as L2 says.

**L12a's *stopping* state.** Checked 2026-08-22, the same shape as L2's, and declined the same way: `Preparation/Specs/Whole app.md` itself requires that a triggered act taking a noticeable moment shows it is happening, the state is a step inside an act the spec grants, no place to look, nothing to type, nothing that can be lost — and the alternative is a stop that looks as though the click did nothing, on a job burning paid machine time.

**Rows 432, 433 and 434's answers, checked together 2026-08-23.** R15 shows a figure Blender prints and withholds nothing that was ever available; an absent estimate was already absent. N18 offers the computer-side shortcut from records Smoozy already holds and states an emptiness the spec itself words — *"Where nothing of the job came home, Smoozy says so plainly rather than leaving a control that does nothing"* — so nothing new is put in front of the user and nothing new can be lost. L20 draws a heading from an identity written at submission, adding no field, no store and no reading. None of the three is a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost. Declined.

**Rows 448, 449 and 450's answers, checked together 2026-08-23.** N19 puts a path beside a version in two records the user never reads and adds no surface, no question and nothing to keep track of; what a removal warning says over those paths is `Server Setup`'s and is unchanged by it. N20 adds no field — N15's are already written — and the way to an earlier entry not being offered where that entry is gone is the spec's own *nothing brings a deleted record back*, said rather than discovered. N21 carries a mark the spec itself grants, in the two readings `File Browser` W9 already composes and already had checked, and its removal where nothing took effect is what stops the user being pointed at a file that was never touched. None of the three is a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost. Declined. **And none of them presupposes any ending of Row 447**: N19 records which build a job was submitted with and says nothing about what becomes of a job whose build is gone.

**Row 447's answer, checked 2026-08-23 and checked hardest of the three dates, because the ruling it carries was taken over four endings that each cost the user something and an arrangement that reintroduces one is exactly what this check is for.** What L21 and L22 add beyond the ruling is a field on a machine record the user never sees, one journal event, and the telling on the route C1 already composes. **None of the four costs comes back**: no history entry and no tombstone survive the drop, so there is nothing left to look at; nothing is asked and nothing is typed; no other build is substituted; and no job is left standing on an answer. **The one thing that can be lost is the job, and that is the ruling itself** — the Creator was shown it as a moment and ruled *drop it, leaving no trace*. **Two things were checked separately and both declined.** The window between a removal and the job's turn, in which the entry reads *waiting* for a job that will not start: nothing is added to say so, because `Server Setup` B14's warning named that job to the user by name before they went ahead and the drop is what they were warned of, so a second statement is a surface no spec grants. And [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 23's restored-build case: it is unreachable through Smoozy (B8), and the alternative — re-checking the mark — is the search reading Fact 45 forbids, which would destroy jobs whose builds stand. Declined.

**N17's withheld controls.** Checked 2026-08-22 with R5's repair. Withholding *show outputs*, the whole-job download and carry-on on an entry whose machine is gone takes nothing the user had — the spec's own sentences are that the entry loses its reach and that Smoozy says so plainly instead of offering an action that cannot work — and the stating costs no new surface, the fact coming from a record Smoozy already holds. Declined.

**Rows 494, 496 and 497's answers, checked together 2026-08-25, the third hardest, because it is the one act on the screen that destroys across every machine at once.** N22 freezes a fact of the user's own submission and adds no question, no surface and nothing to keep — the read still fills the name, and the entry afterwards says more rather than less. P10a draws a name off a record the fetch already carries; an absent name was already absent, and the heading falls to the frame it already names. X6a is the spec's own third act — entered there 2026-08-24, Creator-stated — arranged entirely over machinery deletion already has: no new record, no new debt shape, and the one thing that can be lost, the whole of history at once, is the act's own stated point, its reach listed in the box server by server with each server's own count rather than discovered afterwards. None of the three is a place to look, something to type, or a thing lost beyond what the act names. Declined.

**E13a's colour telling, checked 2026-09-02 and declined.** It is a sentence on an entry the user already opens, composed from words Smoozy already keeps, about a substitution Blender already announced. **The third stop was weighed hardest, because a new sentence on an entry is a new thing to read**: it is no place to look — the entry is where the user already goes; nothing to type; and nothing that can be lost, since the words it is composed from are kept verbatim beside it either way (E7, E9). **The first stop is clear too**: the promise it keeps is the spec's own ruling of 2026-09-02, and this document keeps it in full. **The second is where the honest answer is a limit rather than a stop** — the line's exact text is on record in no version and will not be got — and that is entered as [Section 18](#18-unresolved) item 27 rather than escalated, because a telling that sometimes does not fire leaves the user exactly where the spec says they were without it, and never worse. **Widening it was refused**: nothing here says what the frames look like, the spec's own sources labelling that step **inferred**, and a warning about the picture would be a claim this project has never observed.

**G5a's two requests, checked 2026-09-02 and declined.** Naming when Row 66's figures are taken adds no surface, no question and no record; it makes explicit a reading V2 already permitted and P10d already excluded from its repeating set. **The third stop:** what the user gets is a figure stated as of its moment rather than a figure that ticks, which is what G4 already says the figure is — no spec promises a live one, and the alternative the replay named, showing no figure at all while a render runs, is the one that takes something Row 66 grants. Nothing is a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost. Declined.

## 20. What this document does not own

Cited, and deliberately not restated or decided here.

- **Atomic job submission, the launch contract, scene and frame arguments, engine and GPU selection, sending the stop and the interrupt's choice, re-run and carry-on as user actions, the render kind's ending classification, the missing-assets warning** — `Rendering`, Rows 1 to 23 and 294.
- **The connected period, reconnect policy, the server list and remembered hardware, stop-and-clear's presentation and the removal offers, untracked-work presentation at removal** — `Servers`, Rows 24 to 46.
- **The hold record, the one release path, first-answer-wins, worker lifecycles, the recorded execution group and the stop sequence, the captured streams and the four collection outcomes, arbitration, health readings, the setup-in-progress state, the Telegram slot and note rules** — `Rented-server runtime`, Row 286.
- **The stores and their rules, identities, desired against observed state, the journal and cursors, reconciliation's steps on both sides, the tombstone, the lifetimes, the atomic-or-partial rule itself** — `State, identity and recovery`, Rows 285 and 292.
- **The directory layout, path and name rules, the manifest and attempt records, the recorded reading, the redirect, the inventory and count, the walk and the two picks, the derivative set and its routes, the three hold conditions, completeness entire** — `Job files and outputs`, Rows 118 to 139 and 297.
- **The resolved set, the disturbance marks' content, the remap material and its cleanup** — `Dependency Upload & Remap`, Rows 159 to 179.
- **The transfer contract, channels, budgets, priority mechanism, per-waiter deadlines, idempotent submission** — `Server protocol and transfers`, Rows 281, 287, 289, 291 and 306.
- **Notifications and their four kinds' presentation, the now-versus-catching-up boundary's enforcement, the platform adapters, receiving-filesystem collisions, every presentation surface** — `Desktop app and delivery`, Rows 280, 282, 284, 288, 290, 303 and 304–305.
- **Downloading anything, the arrival counting, destination rules, the auto-download arrangement** — `Download`, Rows 202 to 220 and 298–299.
- **Everything a phone is sent or sends, pacing, the conversation** — `Telegram`, Rows 255 to 277 and 296.
- **Browsing, the disturb warnings' wording and moment** — `File Browser`, Rows 180 to 201 and 307. **Checked 2026-08-29 and left as it stands.** The `File Browser` gate routed a correction here — that this locator undercounts by nine, **Rows 340 to 346, 428 and 486** being that document's too. **All nine are Design rows**, and every bullet of this list names its document's spec rows plus the spec rows cross-filed to it and omits its Design rows — this list's own locator convention, which `Render Settings` §15 states in terms. So **none of the nine are owed here** and the range is unchanged; what a bullet gives is a locator for the topics named, not an exhaustive ownership claim.
- **Wording, layout, and every appearance call the spec hands to Design**, including the queue and history screens, entry packing, the catch-up notice's form, the stale marking, and the offer wordings.
